# Supervisor rulings of the resumed programme (from 2026-09-29) — log R-1 …

Status: supervisor rulings under D-99, to be transcribed as D-100. Engineering and security rulings are in force for the
lanes on dispatch; accounting rulings are supervisor rulings pending the independent revenue accountant (G12) and are
never "approved". Times before 2026-09-30 01:00 PDT were estimates and may run ahead of the machine clock; commit
timestamps are authoritative. The lanes read this file through `.run/sup2/rulings.md` (a link to it).

- **R-1 (2026-09-29 21:10 PDT; lane FIX-B question).** Adapter `secret_ref` under the local key provider: the
  product stays as DG-KRN-KEY-01 / 05 KEY-09 state (EnvSecretStore serves the three master keys only; no
  environment resolution of adapter references). Reason: dev-guide and 05 outrank PRD and BUILD_SPEC; `secret_ref`
  and `base_url` are tenant-supplied, so environment resolution would let a tenant exfiltrate any process secret;
  00-GOAL §4 puts live third-party calls out of 1.0. The three integration tests and the audit-coverage hook get a
  key ring over a secret-store double (shared helper under backend/tests/support/), assertions unchanged
  (STALE TEST WORLD). OPEN PRODUCT DECISION for Ray (post-1.0): adapter credentials for self-hosted deployments
  that run key_provider=local; PRD J-23.1 / J-23.6, 04 T-INT-01 and 03 REQ-INT-006 wording to be reconciled to
  KEY-09 when CLO-27 / DIN-18 are built.
- **G7 measured 2026-09-29 on cf110ae6:** 36 of 49 controls with a passing tagged test; after e139ade8 (three
  existing witnesses tagged) 39 expected. MISSING by unbuilt feature: CTL-009 (CTR-6), CTL-024 / 025 / 026
  (CLO-16 / 17), CTL-032 (SOP-2 witness `test_release_stamping.py::test_ctl_032_runs_stamped_with_release`),
  CTL-041 (RPS-16), CTL-043 (SNP-4), CTL-045 (AIX), CTL-048 (LMG-3); CTL-038 fails with the audit-coverage walk (FIX-C).
- **R-2 (21:35; FIX-C).** `test_ctl_038_every_command_route_writes_audit` has 31 problem lines spanning several
  lanes' causes: each lane fixes its own lines; the test is measured on the merged tree. Close-world order
  dependence (conftest `_forget_stamped_release` + the dev/test latest-row release fallback of D-98 60) is fixed
  at the world (close_world stamps its release).
- **R-3 (21:35; FIX-D1).** Sandbox load: ordering is the only governed fix for frozen deferred references
  (self-references referenced-first; LOAD_ORDER moves the rule-set and approval graph ahead of sod_exception and
  role_assignment); the unit guard must check installed immutability and grants. The two cyclic edges
  (contract_event.estimate_version_id, .manual_adjustment_id) are a second slice, not an accepted allowlist: a
  tenant with an ESTIMATE_CHANGED event must load.
- **R-4 (21:35; FIX-C → FIX-D2).** P1 product defect: the snapshot registry refuses CONTRACT_BALANCE_ROLLFORWARD
  and RPO_ROLLFORWARD line rows (required column `contract_external_id`), so PERIOD_LOCK answers 500 on any
  period with contract-balance activity. Owner FIX-D2; witness = a public-path lock of K-01 August freezing all
  twelve kinds with contract liability 39,708.49. FIX-D2 also takes `test_as_locked.py` (CLO-8c, merged unrun).
- **R-5 (21:35; FIX-E).** Legacy migration mode (a), a contract whose legacy minimum Current Period is after the
  cutover: reading B — booked and activated at its legacy inception, NO OPENING_BALANCE_ESTABLISHED, recognised
  natively, nil cumulative amounts in the reconciliation. Rejected: clamping inception to the cutover (falsifies a
  contract fact; moves the contract into the pre-cutover RPO), an event before inception, refusing the contract.
  Docs first: ENGINE_SPEC S07-R-11 (and S07-R-01 wording if needed). `onboarding.method` pin: built in FIX-E,
  derived from the contract's own OPENING_BALANCE_ESTABLISHED event (reason LEGACY_MIGRATION), scope CONTRACT
  level C; the import witnesses assert the WLD-X-27 / EX-07-A values. P6 property failure: class (c), the
  machine's model (credit note of a voided locked-period billing booked in the clock's period instead of the
  first later open period per S01-R-12 / S14-R-08 / RCP-04); model fixed, engine unchanged.
  ENG-FXREM-RATEPIN-1 moves from FIX-E to a separate engine lane with ENG-S14R10-FX-SIGN-1.
- **R-6 (drafted 21:50; CLO-LOCK-ORDER-1; to dispatch to the close lane after FIX-C merges).** Chronological
  ordinary lock. Risk: a later period is locked and certified while an earlier period of the same entity and book
  is still postable; a posting into the earlier period moves the locked period's opening balance and its frozen
  rollforward no longer continues from the ledger. Control (preventive, automated; new BR-CLS-07, SM-07 guard on
  `closing → closed`): a period is submitted for lock, and locked, only when no earlier period of the same entity
  and book is postable (`open`, `closing` or `reopened`); earlier `future` periods precede the entity-book's
  first kept period and cannot open (SM-07 guard "previous period not `future`"), so they do not block. Checked
  at the request AND at the approval execution under the existing period-state row locks (a request can outlive
  an earlier period's state); refusal 409 with a new catalogue problem mirroring ERR-16 `later-period-closed`
  (working slug `earlier-period-open`, copy "Lock <period> first. An earlier period of <entity> in book <book>
  is not closed."). The permanent-lock order rule (all earlier permanently locked) is unchanged; BR-CLS-05
  (no reopen under a later closed period) is unchanged. Rejected: keeping out-of-order ordinary locks with
  separate report bases; rerouting earlier postings. Consequences: T1's strict-xfail pin becomes a refusal
  witness and the machine's chronological restriction is the product rule; test worlds, the demo seed and the
  e2e journey that lock a period above postable earlier periods are STALE TEST WORLDs under this ruling and close
  the earlier periods first. Basis: standard period-close control in general ledgers; supervisor ruling, not a
  GAAP conclusion.
- **R-7 (22:00; FIX-D1).** 05 SBX-06: `tenant.snapshot_loaded` is the sandbox load's single summary audit event,
  written after verification (report id / SHA-256, `derived_mismatches`, `blocked_periods`); the words "the
  sandbox's first audit event" predate rev 1.20 and are withdrawn (05 rev 1.34). Order: copy → deferred
  references → period replay → recompute → verification → summary. The test asserts exactly one summary event,
  every earlier sandbox event being one of the load's own replay / recompute kinds, and the nine detail keys
  (STALE EXPECTATION). A separate leading provenance event is not built.
- **R-8 (22:00; FIX-D1).** A sandbox recompute carries no `job_id`: the load job row belongs to the source tenant
  and 04 NC-06 makes `(tenant_id, job_id)` cross-tenant references impossible (the loader's attempt was a
  ForeignKeyViolation on every sandbox recompute — PRODUCT DEFECT). The witness asserts `job_id is None` plus an
  attribution fact the product records (STALE EXPECTATION). No sandbox job row, no migration.
- **R-9 (22:20; FIX-D1; 05 SBX-05).** Sandbox determinism check: the raw `output_sha256` of source and sandbox
  can never be equal (the output hash covers `input_sha256`, the input hash covers arrival metadata that SBX-04 /
  DB-08 re-stamp). The check hashes the sandbox OutputBundle with `input_sha256` replaced by the source
  computation's stored `input_sha256` and compares with the source's stored `output_sha256`. Witnesses: equality
  on k01, a negative witness, and a measured multi-currency group (arrival-dependent stage-12 layer keys, CV-26
  (b)) reported; a T-CON-25 / RCP-28a L1 comparison supersedes this when SOP-3's evidence exists.
- **R-10 (22:20; FIX-D1).** `test_replay_reaches_the_source_lock_chain` is owed to a new design, **SNP-2b
  sandbox-side close replay** (the sandbox's own journal run, replayed acknowledgement and reconciliation
  evidence, period-end release postings — needs CLO-14, CLO-16 / 17, CLO-19 / 20). Until it lands SBX-04's
  blocked path governs (D-98 137 (3)). The test keeps its assertions and is pinned strict xfail by this ruling;

- **Open-item pin policy (R-10a).** A strict `xfail` is placed only by a supervisor ruling, names the owning item

- **R-11 (22:30; ENG-FX).** Ledger subject key. Finding: the subledger line stores no subject key and the
  read-back (`bundles._posted`, the unruled [J] L3-1-Q-32) respells every non-obligation subject as

- **R-16 (23:00; FIX-D2 slice 2).** PRODUCT DEFECT: `tie_outs.balances_at` builds its trace-node prefix from the

- **R-17 (23:25; FIX-A; AD-41 opened).** K-02 prospective modification, PRD WLD-X-06 allocation split. The PRD

  60,000.00 lies below their range 69,750.00 – 85,250.00 and counted O1's remaining increments by whole and half

  added seats 775 × 90.00 = 69,750.00 and S06-R-11 / D-97 (3) give O1 2,400 × 472 ÷ 730 × 100.00 = 155,178.08;

  S06-R-19: the landed shape check is right; K-02's body is CO_TERM, K-11's ADD_OBLIGATION; the word "Upgrade"
  in PRD / SCREENS stays open as TA-K02-MOD-KIND-1. Volume seed `domain/demo/volume.py:2754` sends the refused

- **R-18 (2026-09-30 00:40; FIX-B).** A defect in a landed revision's own DDL may be corrected in place while
  no deployed database has run it (D-99 (3)) when the correction adds no object and the round trip and head
  pins are unchanged — 0072's `sync_run` UPDATE grant gains the SC-M columns in place. A new object, column or
  function takes a forward revision.
- **R-19 (00:40; FIX-D2 slice 3).** Period-lock freeze. Finding: the lock decision freezes at the application
  clock (`uow.now`) while contract versions are known at the database clock (DB-08), so a version recorded in the
  skew before the decision is excluded from the version-based kinds while the line-based kinds include it;
  nothing compares the frozen datasets, and the period closes certified. Ruling: (a) the freeze cutoff is
  max(uow.now, transaction_timestamp()) read in the decision's transaction and stored as the lock's cutoff;
  (b) the lock is refused by name when the frozen version-based kinds do not cover the contracts and entities
  that carry the period's lines in the frozen JE population, or a version-based kind is empty beside populated
  line-based kinds; builder tie-outs as lock conditions are ruled separately from the lane's table;
  (c) a registry refusal at the decision is a named 4xx problem, never a bare 500; (d) the lane reports whether
  a posting transaction can commit into the period while the decision is open.

  - Production database lock budget (supervisor, 00:30): `audit_event`, `schedule_line` and `subledger_line`
    have 181 monthly partitions each with several indexes, so one unpruned query takes hundreds of lock-table
    entries; neither the Terraform Cloud SQL flags nor the compose Postgres set `max_locks_per_transaction`

  - Platform answer-key path (from FIX-C): five gaps behind POS-117 (SSP study attachment, approval through
    engine.decide, activation checklist prerequisites through commands, D-83 ruling 1 in product_in, period
    state for the book) — PRP-1 to PRP-3.
  - Volume seed: `perf_seed._close_and_lock` stops at close-gates-failed until the close commands exist (G9).
- **R-5a (2026-09-30 01:15; FIX-E; refines R-5).** Migration mode (a), contract whose legacy inception is after
  the cutover: inception is never clamped (final). Lane measured that T-MIG-04 (revision 0067) requires an
  opening event per captured version (five NOT NULL columns read back from it). Mechanism, in order of
  preference: **variant E** — the contract receives its one OPENING_BALANCE_ESTABLISHED with the batch's
  `cutover_date` and nil rows, EFFECTIVE on the later of the cutover and its inception (S07-R-01 amended), so the
  capture evidence model is unchanged; if E breaks a stage 07 invariant, **B with revision 0079** — the five

  event; CONTRACT_BOOKED document_ref of the batch; inception after the cutover). The witnesses' calendar gains
  FY2023-P02 (PRD J-20 precondition). A by-name refusal for a missing inception period is a migration-lane
  follow-up.
- **Test-support defect (FIX-A, 01:15).** `support/interleave.py` polled `pg_stat_activity` inside the holder's
  open transaction, where `state` / `query` are frozen at the first read (stats snapshot), so every
  `blocked_in` assertion raced ("READ-SIDE-BLOCK-1", recorded cause OPEN since 09-21). Fix:
  `pg_stat_clear_snapshot()` before each read; product unchanged.

## Security rulings (2026-09-30 00:45 PDT) — from the SEC review; digest `.run/sup2/sec/findings-digest.md`
Every finding below is a PRODUCT DEFECT unless marked otherwise. A fix lane first re-runs the reviewer's PoC in
its own worktree (copy it under `.run/`; PoCs live in `/Users/rsang/dev/erev-wt/l10/.run/sec/<helper>/`), fixes
docs first where a contract changes, then turns the PoC into a committed regression test that asserts the
refusal (the PoC's positive assertion inverted), under the named test module of the touched area.

- **R-20 (SN-13, SN-12; Step 1 path).** ENGINE_SPEC Table 2.2-A stands: the engine is a function of the events.
  The defects are the platform's: it appends status-moving events on the recorder's authority.
  (a) A `COLLECTIBILITY_ASSESSED` / `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` names an ENABLED book of the contract's contracting
  entity, and its judgement record is of that book: otherwise 422 `validation-failed` (rule id `STEP1_RECORD`
  family; new message). (b) The not-a-contract gate (`events._gate`, L4-1-Q-10; PRD SM-02 row
  DRAFT → NOT_A_CONTRACT, guard "judgement NOT_A_CONTRACT submitted") is appended only when, with the request's
  events applied, the latest assessment of EVERY enabled book of the contracting entity is not probable;
  otherwise the assessments are recorded, the contract stays DRAFT and the only way to a status is the approved
  activation (its checklist item `STEP1_RECORD` already requires a reviewed record per enabled book), where the
  engine decides per book. (c) PRD SM-02 row NOT_A_CONTRACT → PENDING_REVIEW governs ("record criteria met …
  on approval: ACTIVE with catch-up"): `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` is appended only by the approved activation.
  `/submit-activation` admits a NOT_A_CONTRACT contract whose criteria-met re-assessment is recorded with a
  REVIEWED judgement for each book that moves; it runs the same checklist, routing flags and second step as any
  activation (REQ-CON-005, REQ-CON-006, CTL-004, CTL-005); on approval the SYSTEM principal appends
  `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` (per book, effective date as submitted) and `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED` with the request id.
  The stored row stays NOT_A_CONTRACT while the request is pending and the read model answers PENDING_REVIEW
  (the L4-1-Q-18 pattern). `POST /contracts/{id}/events` refuses `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` and
  `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED` from any caller (409 `invalid-transition` naming the activation command). 04 E-03 row
  `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` approval cell: "CONTRACT_ACTIVATION (appended by the approved activation; reviewed
  judgement record required)". (d) Witness of the invariant: over the Step 1 witness matrix, a book status in
  {ACTIVE, COMPLETED, TERMINATED} implies an APPROVED CONTRACT_ACTIVATION request of the contract or the SYSTEM
  activation path (BS3-D-19) or a MIGRATION-origin activation. Open question for the lane to answer in its
  report (not to fix): can a book be enabled on an entity that already has contracts (`put_entity_book`), and
  what then gives the existing contracts their Step 1 record in the new book.
- **R-21 (SN-7; product-level policy values).** POLICIES §0.5 rule 3 governs: a level-P value is pin K — the
  value resolved at the contract's inception is pinned on the computed contract version for the life of the
  contract; a later change reaches only contracts whose inception is on or after it. `bundles.py` reads the live
  product row on every recompute: PRODUCT DEFECT. Fix: the OBLIGATION-scope values resolved from the product
  (and the `ProductInput` members that feed the engine: `principal_agent`, `distinctness_default`,
  `revenue_category`, default template, `assurance_cost_per_unit`, `unit_of_measure`, bundle components,
  `is_franchisor_preopening_service`) are pinned at the first committed computation of the contract and read
  from the pin afterwards (extend `pinned_policies` / T-CON-07 `pinned_refs`; docs first in 04 T-CON-07 and
  dev-guide DG-KRN-REG-02). A pin-P parameter admitted at PRODUCT level, if any exists, is reported, not
  guessed. Second half (§0.6 CFG): a product edit that changes a CFG-coded value at PRODUCT level needs
  maker-checker — route it through the approval subject that `principal_agent` already uses; an edit of
  non-accounting members stays direct. `apply_to_existing` for product changes is out of scope for 1.0 (stated
  in the known-limitations register): existing contracts keep their pins.
- **R-22 (SN-6; regroup).** REQ-CON-012 ("through an approved command with comment") and 04 E-03 `REGROUPED`
  (approval MODIFICATION) govern both branches. Both contracts DRAFT: direct (activation approves the result).
  Either contract not DRAFT: the regroup is a MODIFICATION approval whether or not lines have posted; before
  posting, the approved regroup re-runs both contracts from inception. The source's status is validated against
  SM-02 (VOIDED / TERMINATED / NOT_A_CONTRACT sources are refused unless a document says otherwise) and the
  command takes `If-Match` on both contracts' row versions.
- **R-23 (SN-5; holds).** REQ-POL-010: a SYSTEM hold ends when its condition ends. `release-hold` on a hold whose
  `hold_source` is not MANUAL answers 409 `invalid-transition` with a named rule; nothing is appended. Verify
  and, if true, fix in the same slice: a judgement with `subject_type` product / combination_group plus
  `contract_id` applies no hold (`judgements.py:384-390, 428`) — REQ-POL-010 "unreviewed judgement on the
  contract" makes no exception.
- **R-24 (SN-11; manual events).** CTR-6 (REQ-DAT-014, CTL-009, P0) is built now, not deferred: it moves to the
  front of F-CTR-A. No interim refusal is shipped separately.
- **R-25 (SN-4, SC-5, SC-N5; approval scope).** REQ-PLT-012 applies to approvals. Every approval subject bound
  to legal entities states them: contract-bound subjects (MANUAL_EVENT, JUDGEMENT_RECORD with a contract,
  CONTRACT_ACTIVATION, ATTRIBUTE_CHANGE, POLICY_OVERRIDE, SSP_OVERRIDE, ESTIMATE_VERSION) → the contracting
  entity; JOURNAL_RUN → the run's entity; ENTITY-scope REGISTRY_VERSION → that entity; COMBINATION_GROUP → the
  contracting entities of all members; IMPORT_COMMIT → every entity named by the upload's rows. A decision
  needs the step's permission for EVERY entity of the subject; a request is listed and readable only for a
  principal whose scope for that permission (or for the subject's read permission) covers at least one of them,
  decidable only when it covers all. Single-entity subjects fill `approval_request.entity_id`; multi-entity
  subjects are resolved by a spec callback at list and decide time (no schema change unless the queue filter
  needs a stored set — then Alembic number on request). A role requirement on a step ("a Controller", REQ-CLS-011)
  is satisfied only by an assignment whose scope covers the subject's entity. The BR-JE-01 runner check compares
  the acting person through delegation (`on_behalf_of`). Decisions L6-3-Q-9 and L4-2-Q-5 and the docstring at
  `subjects.py:82-90` are superseded where they say otherwise (D-100).
- **R-26 (SN-9; approval rules).** REQ-PLT-016 admits auto-approval of "system-originated standard items" only.
  (a) An AUTO_APPROVAL or APPROVAL_ROUTING rule with no condition is invalid; an AUTO_APPROVAL rule names the
  subject types it covers. (b) Auto-approval applies only when the request's preparer is a system-originated
  principal (SYSTEM, an API client / service account, an adapter or migration job) AND the subject type is on
  the allow-list the documents already name (system-originated contract activation REQ-CON-006; import commit
  for API clients; AUTO-MIG-01); never to RULE_SET_VERSION of kind AUTO_APPROVAL or APPROVAL_ROUTING, any
  access subject (ROLE_ASSIGNMENT, ROLE_CHANGE, SOD_EXCEPTION, SUPPORT_GRANT), period lock / reopen, or a
  registry value in `HUMAN_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`. (c) A routing rule may add steps or raise a requirement; the
  subject specification's own permission, approver count, second step, quorum and role requirement are a floor
  that routing output cannot lower (validated at publish and enforced at submit).
- **R-27 (SN-8; SSP book scope).** REQ-PLT-014: the approved content includes what the approval puts in force.
  The book's scope members (entity, currency, segment, channel, resolution mode) enter
  `ssp_book_version_content`, and `update_ssp_book` refuses a scope change while any version of the book is
  SUBMITTED (409) — both.
- **R-28 (SN-1, SN-2, SN-3, SN-10, SC-3, SC-4; entity scope on tenant-level surfaces).** REQ-PLT-012: "reads,
  commands and reports return only in-scope entities; out-of-scope ids return 404". A row that belongs to a
  contract, run, period or upload is visible only through a visible owner: judgement records with a contract;
  combination groups (≥ 1 visible member, only visible members listed); postings (`GET /subledger-postings/{id}`
  needs a visible line; totals cover visible entities); imports (the upload stores the entities its rows name;
  list / rows / diff / error report are filtered by the caller's `contract.read` scope; an upload naming an
  entity outside the uploader's scope is refused at validation); SSP calculator runs (provider limited to the
  requester's `contract.read` scope, scope stored on the run and applied to reads); SSP books with an entity
  (`require_for_entity` in every SSP command; reads scoped); `open_period` and `_authorize_reference`
  (`require_for_entity` for the held permission, SYSTEM path kept). Rows with no entity attribution that expose
  entity financial data — `GET /audit-events`, `/jobs`, `/control-executions`, `/source-records/{id}` — require
  the guarding permission at scope `*` (all entities) until the row carries an entity; a holder restricted to
  named entities gets 403 `forbidden` on the tenant-wide list (documented in 05 and the permission catalogue
  note), never a filtered-looking list.
- **R-29 (SC-2; imports).** Decision L5-1-Q-11 is narrowed: the import runs as SYSTEM on behalf of the uploader
  WITHIN the uploader's entity scope. At validation the upload's named entities are resolved and stored; an
  entity outside the uploader's scope for the template's write permission is a row finding (ERROR) and the
  diff's `before` never reads outside that scope; `import.approve` must cover every named entity (R-25).
- **R-30 (SC-6; evidence files).** A file that is evidence is not shreddable while the record stands: lock
  datasets (T-CLS-05) for the life of the lock, the source of a COMMITTED or SUBMITTED import, journal batch
  files, report outputs of a locked run, approval impact previews. `shred_refusal` refuses by reference (409
  with a named reason); no retention date is invented. `GET /imports/{id}` answers for an upload whose source
  was shredded before commit (state shown, content gone). The identical-bytes refusal of D-98 145 stands.
- **R-31 (SC-1; lock race).** PRODUCT DEFECT against DB-07 / REQ-CLS-002: the two period guards take
  `FOR SHARE` on the `period_state` row (the lock decision holds `FOR UPDATE`), so a posting either commits
  before the lock decision reads the ledger or fails the guard. New Alembic revision (number in the package);
  04 DB-07 states the row lock. Witness: the reviewer's two-session PoC inverted (the late posting is refused
  or included in the lock's head), plus a deadlock check of lock-vs-posting order under the interleave helper.
- **R-32 (SC-7, SC-8, SC-N1, SC-N4).** SC-7: journal coverage is per (entity, book, period) regardless of mode;
  a second non-cancelled run over seals already covered is refused (DB-16 key and `_covered_from` without
  `mode`). SC-8: the cockpit / checklist GET writes nothing when no gate state changed and never an audit event
  for a read; `POST /audit-events/verify` defers unless a verification is pending and is rate-limited. SC-N1:
  dispatch re-reads the batch state under a row lock before calling the adapter. SC-N4: reported to the close
  lane as a question against REQ-CLS-009 (do custom blocking tasks gate the lock) — answered from the PRD, not
  assumed.
- **R-33 (SF-2, SF-1, ST-1 / lead #1, SF-3; platform).** SF-2: REQ-PLT-005 is enforced by the server — a session
  whose user must enrol and has no confirmed factor reaches only the session, sign-out and MFA-enrolment routes
  (403 `mfa-enrolment-required` elsewhere); `GET /session` carries `mfa_enrolment_required`. SF-1: SBX-08 —
  creating or activating a webhook endpoint in a SANDBOX tenant is refused 403 `sandbox-restricted` with the
  DENIED audit, and a DB-15 trigger backs it (with SNP-4). ST-1 / lead #1: file content is authorised by the
  file's purpose and owning subject, not by `audit.read` alone; an operator's support grant reads files only of
  purposes the grant's scope names. SF-3: the step's approval permission reads the request's impact preview.
- **R-34 (SD-1..SD-7; deployment).** To OPS: compose api / worker run with the app role only (owner URL on
  migrate only); cookie `Secure` from the configured public origin; HSTS at the TLS-terminating tier and in the
  self-host guide; `/metrics` 401 on any malformed bearer; the fail-closed doctor subset (email backend fake in
  production, placeholder or repeated master keys, loopback integration URLs) refuses startup; SMTP STARTTLS
  fixed and witnessed against a local TLS sink (functional P1: no mail is delivered today); `.map` files are
  not served by the production web image.

- **R-35 (2026-09-30 01:00; F-RPS-REG).** RPT-31 `loss_provision_register` waits for CTR-14 (persistence of T-CON-17
  `loss_provision_version`); no interim reader over the calc trace (a register reads the stored record; no second
  derivation of the loss test). RPS-11 is PARTIAL until then; the item travels with F-CTR-B.
- **R-36 (01:00; FIX-A; extends R-17).** ENGINE_SPEC_B EX-09-A / EX-15-A are true for their own inputs (point SSP

  155,178.08 : 69,750.00 (shares 148,451.55 / 66,726.53, PRD rev 1.21), and names AD-41 as pending the

  `test_user_guide_lists_extract_datasets` goes to the RPS-14 lane.
- **Revision numbers (01:00).** No lane picks a document revision number itself any more (collision: FIX-D2 took
  index 13 while SECFIX-APR held it). Register: FIX-D2 slice 2 = index 21, slice 3 = index 22.

- **R-38 (2026-09-30 04:28; SECFIX-APR; refines R-26 after the lane's independent review of its change).**
  (i) *System-originated means the content, not only the submitter.* A CONTRACT_ACTIVATION is auto-approvable only
  when every event of the contract's stream up to the submission, and the draft itself, were written by
  system-originated principals. A `replace_draft`, a line or memo change or an event by a USER principal on an
  integration-booked DRAFT makes the activation a human approval (the routing flag that says "manual" reads the
  stream's authorship, not `contract.source_system` alone).
  (ii) *An import commit never stands in for a stricter approval.* PRD §2.5 row `IMPORT_COMMIT` / `AUTO-IMP-01`
  (API client, control totals match, zero ERROR findings) stays for templates whose commit writes transactional data
  of an integrated source or creates DRAFT objects that still take their own approval. A template whose commit
  performs an approval-coded act — activates a contract (legacy v1 `Contract Setup`), puts an SSP version, an
  estimate version, an account mapping or an FX rate set in force — is never auto-approved, and its human
  IMPORT_COMMIT approval must satisfy the underlying subject's own steps (permission, thresholds, second step). The
  lane lists every template with what its commit puts in force; SECFIX-APR enforces the no-auto-approval half through
  a per-template class, SECFIX-IMP the approver-requirement half.
  (iii) *An API client's scopes are an access grant.* Creating an API client, or adding a scope to one, takes effect
  only after approval by another person under the routing of ROLE_ASSIGNMENT (same approver rule, same
  AUTO-BOOTSTRAP treatment during setup); the secret is issued after approval. Reason: a Tenant Admin holds no
  finance permission by design (SoD), and an API client with `event.record`, `import.upload` or `contract.create`
  is that permission under another name — integrated-source events are appended without approval (REQ-DAT-014,
  PRD ACT-10). Docs first: 03 REQ-PLT-033, PRD §2.5 routing row, 04 approval subject table, CTL-037 evidence.
  A product behaviour change (Developer settings show a client pending approval) — recorded for Ray in the RESUME
  record.
  (iv) *Bootstrap.* `AUTO-BOOTSTRAP` matches only the bootstrap Tenant Admin — the user of the role assignment
  that `tenant.provision` created — not every holder of `tenant_admin` (PRD BR-PLT-02 says "the bootstrap Tenant
  Admin"); 04 §14.3 states the condition. No time box is added (the rule ends with setup completion, as documented).
  (v) *AUTO-REC-01.* R-26 (a) reads: an AUTO_APPROVAL rule carries a discriminating condition — `subject.type` for
  rules the approvals engine matches, `reconciliation.kind` for auto-certification rules (BUILD_SPEC BS4-D-09,
  CLO-17), which only the reconciliation certification path matches. A rule with neither is invalid; the approvals
  engine never matches a rule without `subject.type`, the certification path never one without
  `reconciliation.kind`.
  (vi) The three test gaps the review named (the submission-time admissible filter, the AUTO-BOOTSTRAP rule-set pin,
  `engine._any_role_required`) get isolating database tests.

- **R-40 (2026-09-30 04:32; FIX-D2 slices 2 and 3; supersedes the owner named in R-31).**
  (a) *Waterfall row key.* PRODUCT DEFECT: `revenue_waterfall` groups OBLIGATION rows by a key joined from raw ids
  (contracts `A:B` / obligation `C` and `A` / `B:C` become one row of 19,726.02). Option (a): every identity
  component of the RPT-01 row key goes through the engine's `encode_key` (the D-98 104 rule already in force for
  RPT-05 and the frozen long-form keys); legacy keys holding a CV-21 delimiter change their key text
  (`POB #1` → `POB %231`), labels do not. SCREENS_B rev 1.26 (1.25 is lane F-RPS-REG's). The other presentation
  row keys joined from raw ids (RPT-02 / 03 / 04, 09 / 10, 11, RPO exempt rows, the two monitor dedupe keys) show
  no wrong figure; they take the same encoder in the reports lane as item RPT-ROWKEY-ENC-1, not in this slice.
  (b) *Lock race (SC-1) moves to FIX-D2 slice 3.* Measured twice now (the security helper's two-session proof and
  FIX-D2's public-path lock): a posting commits into the period while the lock decision is open. Fix as R-31
  states — the two period guards read `period_state` FOR SHARE — in a NEW revision 0084 (not in place: dev and
  review databases past 0040 / 0046 must receive it by upgrade), with the deadlock-order witness and the
  per-line cost measurement of the SECFIX-CLO package's SC-1 bullet. SECFIX-CLO keeps SC-7, SC-8, SC-N1, SC-N4.
  (c) *D1.* The lock's cutoff is part of the lock record: column `period_lock.cutoff_known_at` (04 T-CLS-04 docs
  first; NOT NULL for LOCK rows), in the same revision 0084; the audit event and the CTL-016 control-execution
  detail carry it as well. The sandbox replay of a lock (SNP-2b) will read the column.
  (d) *D2.* The coverage guard reads the source rows in the decision's transaction at the freeze cutoff, as the
  lane proposed: refuse by name when a subledger line of the entity, book and period is recorded after the cutoff;
  when a contract with lines in the period has no contract version known by the cutoff or has a later version known
  only after it; when WATERFALL froze empty while the frozen JE population holds REVENUE lines.
  (e) *D3.* 409 `close-gates-failed` with one error per finding (rule id S15-R-18 for a registry refusal, a new
  document rule id for the coverage guard); the request stays PENDING.
  (f) *Noted for the accountant and the contracts lane (AD-42 opened).* RPT-02 shows a member contract's liability
  as all noncurrent: the engine publishes the current part per group and entity only and nothing writes the
  per-member T-CON-09 current columns (REQ-BIL-010). Attributing a group's current / noncurrent split to member
  contracts is an accounting decision; until it is taken the report must not present 0.00 as a measured current
  part (to the reports lane with CTR-14).

- **R-41 (2026-09-30 04:35; SECFIX-APR; from the lane's two independent reviews of its uncommitted change; refines R-25 and R-26).**
  (1) *Delegation conveys the permission, not the view.* A person decides — in person or as a delegate — only when
  their OWN entity scope covers every entity of the subject (REQ-PLT-015: the approver sees what they approve).
  Otherwise the decision is refused before the content hash is recomputed; a request is never voided STALE because
  the decider could not see part of it. A delegation is effective, listed and notified for a request only under the
  same condition.
  (2) *Entity sets are re-derived at decision.* The set frozen at submission is compared with the set the subject
  states at decision; a difference voids the request as STALE_SUBJECT (REQ-PLT-014) — this also closes the
  SSP-book re-scope window until R-27 lands.
  (3) COMBINATION_GROUP excludes the author of the combination record from deciding, in person, through a delegate
  and in bulk (the judgement-author rule).
  (4) A subject whose content names entities states them even when its table is tenant-level: ROLE_ASSIGNMENT → the
  entities of the proposed grant (an all-entities grant needs the approval permission at scope `*`);
  ACCOUNT_MAPPING_VERSION → the entities its rules name. The lane lists any further subject of this kind.
  (5) IMPORT_COMMIT by template: a template whose rows name no legal entity (customers, products, FX rates, GL
  accounts, SSP values of a book without an entity) is tenant-level — decidable by any holder of the permission;
  a template whose rows name entities takes the named set, which includes performing / selling entities (legacy
  modification ADD rows); only an unresolvable upload needs scope `*`. SECFIX-IMP stores the set.
  (6) A request for which no eligible decider exists at submission (nobody but the preparer holds the step's
  permission with a covering scope) must not be silent: item APR-NO-APPROVER-1 for the platform lane (notify the
  access administrators; show it in the queue). Not in this lane.
  (7) R-26 floor: the requirements that live outside `SubjectSpec` today — PERIOD_LOCK's Controller role
  (`quorum.route`), ESTIMATE_VERSION's Controller second step (PRD §2.5) — enter the specification so the floor
  protects them; `apply_floor` merges a requirement a routed step already satisfies instead of adding a second
  step (one Controller must be able to decide); SYSTEM counts as system-originated only when the job acts on
  behalf of no user; MIGRATION_SSP_REPLAY is pinned to its seeded rule set as ROLE_ASSIGNMENT is; the rule-set
  evaluate / example-case path applies the same admissibility and floor as submission.

- **R-42 (2026-09-30 04:37; FIX-D2 slice 3, after its report; adjusts R-40 (d) and (e)).**
  (a) The coverage guard as built stands: four source-row checks (a line recorded after the cutoff; a journal run
  created after it; a contract whose posting version is known only after it; the period's line or run count changed
  between the freeze and the last lock write). The emptiness condition of R-19 ("a version-based kind is empty beside
  populated line-based kinds") is withdrawn: a period whose only revenue is a non-contract's 606-10-25-7 revenue
  legitimately has journal rows and an empty WATERFALL; the exact form of that condition is the tie-out below.
  (b) The refusal is 409 `invalid-transition` with the governed rule id (S15-R-18, S15-R-18c), as built —
  `close-gates-failed` keeps its meaning (04 16.8: a gate check code). PRD §5.5 gains the ERR rows that 04 §15.4
  requires for a governed rule id used as `errors[].rule_id` (S15-R-07a, S15-R-18, S15-R-18c); S15-R-19 and
  DG-PAR-05, used the same way without a row, go to the reports lane.
  (c) *Builder tie-outs at the lock.* The freeze computes every builder tie-out and stores the results with the
  lock (none is dropped). The five internal identities — rollforward opening + lines = closing (balances and RPO),
  balances = rollforward closing, cost rollforward, journal population = the runs' totals — can fail only on a
  defect: a FAIL refuses the lock decision by name. The two journal ties — waterfall = revenue journal total
  (CTL-019) and disaggregation = revenue journal total (CTL-028) — can differ legitimately for revenue outside the
  disclosed population: they are close gates with the checklist's waiver path, evaluated on the frozen datasets.
  `TO_ROLLFORWARD_EQ_GL` stays NOT_APPLICABLE until a trial balance exists (CLO-15).
  (d) A control's result must not depend on who decides: the lock guard and the gates of a lock decision read the
  entity's rows under the tenant's SYSTEM scope, not under the decider's row-level scope (a line of the lock's
  entity whose contract belongs to another entity must be seen). The guard in this slice; the gates' own reads as
  item CLO-GATE-SCOPE-1 in SECFIX-CLO.
  (e) The lock's `ledger_head_chain_seq` is read inside the decision after the FOR SHARE guard of R-40 (b) is in
  place, so it names exactly the seals the datasets hold; the seal range of S15-R-18 stays with CLO-19. The sandbox
  replay of a lock (`sandbox_periods._lock`) takes `freeze.freeze_cutoff` and `freeze.assert_covered` in F-SNP.

- **R-43 (2026-09-30 04:38; FIX-D1 after its slice 1 report; refines R-9 and R-3).**
  (a) *What the sandbox load verifies (05 SBX-05).* Finding accepted as measured: the engine is incremental
  (`previous_stream_heads`, `posted`), the sandbox recomputes each group once, so an output hash — raw, substituted
  or L1 — can equal the source's only for a group the source computed exactly once (Avenmoor: arrival metadata
  alone changes nothing but `input_sha256` in 11 of 11 groups; against the source's latest versions 18 of 18
  pairs differ in intents and trace, never in the monetary state). The verification becomes a comparison of the
  MONETARY STATE: RCP-28a L2 category M — contract versions, balances (T-CON-09), schedule lines (T-CON-16) and
  obligation-version columns — excluding by name the per-version activity columns of T-CON-11 (`revenue_amount`,
  `billed_amount`, `pre_standard_revenue_amount`, `delivered_quantity`, `ssp_delivered`), posting intents and
  the trace. A difference is a SANDBOX_DETERMINISM_MISMATCH naming the first differing member; the hash comparison
  of R-9 stays as the stronger check for a first computation (`version_no = 1`). Item SNP-2c, built in F-SNP.
  Interim class NOT_COMPARABLE (option C) is used only if SNP-2c cannot land in the same lane round. Ledger period
  attribution is NOT verified by SBX-05 (the sandbox recomputes with every period open); it belongs to SNP-2b.
  (b) *Cyclic edges (slice 2).* The row-ordered component {contract_event, obligation, estimate, estimate_version,
  manual_adjustment} as designed: ordering only, one copy-phase transaction for the component, a result that
  reorders events or finds a cycle is SNAPSHOT_NOT_LOADABLE; `manual_adjustment.applied_event_id` becomes the
  deferred (updatable) edge. The manual-adjustment edge is witnessed with directly inserted rows, marked to be
  replaced by a product-path witness when CLO-12 lands.
  (c) The split test's pin of the four gate names stays strict. The SNP-2 acceptance wording of build-spec fragment
  14 ("first audit event", `test_sandbox_copy_k03`) is amended under header 1.26 and fragment 14 rev 1.4.
  (d) Into F-SNP: `blocked_periods` gets a queryable home (a member of the sandbox / snapshot API answer) and the
  refusal by name that SBX-04 promises for consumers needing point-in-time equivalence; a failed load must not
  leave an ACTIVE sandbox tenant with the requester's membership; the sandbox's key provisioning is verified
  against the hosted key-provider fake; the lock replay takes the freeze cutoff and coverage guard of R-42.

- **R-44 (2026-09-30 04:40; ENG-FX and FIX-E after their reports).**
  (a) *ENG-S14R10-FX-SIGN-1: option B.* A role delta whose transaction and functional amounts differ in sign posts
  as two lines of the same role in its entry — a transaction line (functional 0) and a functional line
  (transaction 0), each on the side of its own amount; the functional line's key is the ten members plus
  `currency_leg` = `FUNCTIONAL`. No amount or rate is derived, journals, CSV rows, answer keys and goldens are
  unchanged (summarisation nets per account and currency). Decision L2-5-Q-34's refusal is withdrawn; the stateful
  machine's rate bound is removed and any new failure is reported, not bounded again; no corpus key is added now
  (the engine witness and a database witness on the events route pin it; a key goes to the accountant's package).
  The presentation choice (B against a transaction line at a stated rate) and the period-end relief convention for
  an invoice dated inside the month (note §7 items 1 and 3) are opened as **AD-43** for the independent accountant;
  neither is changed.
  (b) *R-11 details.* The FX match of Part 1 stays as the explicit regrouping rule, generalised to ONE rule for
  every group-level subject (FX remeasurement, refund-liability components, any later family): a posted amount
  stored under the `<group>@<entity>` key of a group whose members now belong to the current group answers the
  same subject under the current group, so a combination posts only differences. Uncombining stays with
  L9-RUN-Q-9 / PR-7.8. The key is inside the sealed line document. The loss-unit family keeps its engine witness
  until the close passes run on the platform (CLO-19 / 20), when a database witness is owed. `subject_key` is not
  exposed in API-S-SubledgerLine for 1.0. The one-stamp convention for a line behind several rates (S14-R-28) is an
  engineering convention, listed for the accountant as information.
  (c) *FIX-E, variant E as built* (R-5a): S07-R-01 admits the opening event on the contract's inception when that
  is after the cutover, for every reason except BUSINESS_COMBINATION (a contract that begins after the acquisition
  date is not an acquired contract, PT-11 — the lane's exception is adopted); S07-R-04 starts the remaining term
  the day after the CUTOVER; S07-R-03 refuses a non-nil cumulative member for such a contract. SYSTEM_ONBOARDING
  follows the same sentence. The reconcile "0 differences" of PRD J-20.3 needs the dry run to compute under the
  preset the promotion will publish (BS3-D-26): migration-lane item LMG-DRYRUN-PRESET-1. A missing inception period
  becomes a named prerequisite finding of the import (date, entity, period) instead of an engine invariant:
  LMG-PREREQ-PERIOD-1.
  (d) *Steps a lane's permission system refused.* Two lane sessions were refused actions by their own permission
  system: FIX-E a read of `backend/tests/pg/test_migration_capture_pg.py` (the last world edit of R-5a: fiscal
  period FY2023-P02 on the witnesses' calendar) and merges / cherry-picks of main; ENG-FX the edit of the shared
  ledger row builders under `backend/tests/support` that revision 0082 needs. A refusal in a lane session is not
  lifted by the supervisor and the step is not taken over at the lane's request; both are put to Ray with the
  permission rules the lanes named. Until then: the three capture witnesses stay red for the missing period (no
  product cause), and R-11 stays a patch under `l19/.run/engfx/r11-wip/`. Lane branches that cannot take main are
  integrated from the supervisor's side as they stand.

- **R-45 (2026-09-30 04:42; F-DIN after its Part 1 and Part 2 reports).**
  (a) *Gap item (DIN-13).* `SOURCE_VERSION_GAP` / PRD IMP-126 stands with severity **INFO**: the sweep has
  recorded and processed the version, so nothing is left to clear and the item must not hold a period lock; a
  version that does not apply cleanly raises its own remediable item or fails the run. The baseline rule stands
  (the first sweep a connection completes raises nothing; `checkpoint.swept_at`), and DIN-13's acceptance sentence
  gains the qualifier (build-spec fragment 13 rev 1.5, header 1.34).
  (b) `CONTRACT_NOT_FOUND` raised by a sync for a document whose subscription has no contract, the failure step
  `bill`, the COA_SYNC decisions 1 to 5 (deactivate what is the ERP's, conflicts named, empty chart = fetch
  failure, record_count = ERP accounts, configured dimensions) — adopted as built.
  (c) A connection's `base_url` is refused at create / update under production unless it satisfies the static
  SAR-15 rule (https; no literal loopback, private or link-local address; no `localhost`, `.local`, `.internal`
  or single-label host) — the enforcing layer for R-39. A probe records FAILURE also for a transient provider
  failure (the person asked a question; the webhook receiver keeps its 5xx so the source retries). A NetSuite
  connection's "Test connection" is a single-attempt chart read. `sync.PRODUCT_UNMAPPED_MESSAGE` takes PRD IMP-41's
  copy. The CSV v2 invoices template's sign for credit memos is verified against 04 T-SRC-04 / -05 and corrected
  if it differs.
  (d) *Close gate for sync runs (CTL-002, BR-INT-03, SCREENS_B BLK-07)* — item CLO-GATE-SYNC-1 for the close lane:
  the interface gate counts, for the period's entity, sync runs that ended FAILED or CONTROL_TOTAL_MISMATCH; such a
  run stops counting when a later run of the same connection and kind SUCCEEDED over the same window, or when
  every failure item of the run is resolved or waived in the exception queue.
  (e) *Hosted `secret_ref` is not tenant-scoped* (any secret the api identity can read resolves for any tenant's
  connection; security lead finding 4): a per-tenant namespace is enforced at save and at resolve before the first
  live adapter — platform security package.

- **R-46 (2026-09-30 04:43; lane ACCT; memo `docs/accounting/reviews/AD-13-14-34-MEMO-2026-09-29.md` at e6bf4195 with its
  independent review, `.run/sup2/acct/memo-review-report.md`).** Supervisor rulings pending the independent
  revenue accountant; every key's `review.status` stays pending and nothing here is an approval.

  L1-LIC allocated_amount 2,000,000.00 → 2,050,000.00; remaining_allocation 1,450,000.00 → 1,500,000.00. Nothing
  else changes.
  (b) *SFC-S3-EX26-RETURN-RIGHT (AD-13): evidence-supported ORACLE correction to the reading D-87 L6-5-Q-25 ruled
  for the engine (a return right lapses on its window-end day), no engine change.* Form A — values at the existing

- **R-48 (2026-09-30 04:45; platform security package SECFIX-PLT; security report S2, S3, S21 and P3-4, -13, -17, -23; lead
  findings 2 to 6; extends R-33).**
  (a) *S3 — delegation.* Creating, changing or ending an approval delegation is an access-administration act: it
  needs an MFA-verified session and a fresh step-up of the delegator, names only permissions the delegator holds,
  never the delegator themself, and is audited; the delegate decides only under R-41 (1) (own entity scope) and
  REQ-PLT-011 (never an item they prepared or already decided). BR-PLT-07's limits (period, scope) are enforced by
  the command.
  (b) *S21 and lead finding 6 — MFA is per user.* REQ-PLT-005: MFA is mandatory for a user who holds any
  `requires_mfa` permission. Such a user has no usable session without the second factor: until enrolled, only the
  session, sign-out and enrolment routes answer (403 `mfa-enrolment-required` elsewhere); once enrolled, sign-in
  completes only with the TOTP or a recovery code, and a password-only session of such a user reaches nothing but
  the challenge. `GET /session` carries `mfa_enrolment_required`. Operators likewise (the sign-in answer states
  `mfa_required` / `mfa_enrolment_required` for an operator).
  (c) *S2 — files.* File content is authorised by the file's purpose and owning subject: the caller needs the read
  permission of the subject that owns the file (and its entity scope, R-28); `audit.read` alone opens audit
  artefacts only (chain digests, audit exports). An operator's support grant reads a file only when the grant's
  scope names its purpose. The step's approval permission reads the request's impact preview (P3-4), and the
  approve action is refused while the preview cannot be read (REQ-PLT-015). Attachments accept every subject type
  the documents name (ATT-SUBJECTS-1).
  (d) *P3-13 — OIDC.* An identity provider is bound at creation to the email domains it is authoritative for
  (operator command); a sign-in through it is accepted only for a verified email in those domains, and links to an
  existing identity only when that identity was invited for that provider or is already linked to the same
  provider subject — never by email alone.
  (e) *Lead finding 5.* `/oauth/token` and `/session/invitations/lookup` take per-address rate limits of the
  route table's classes; lockout and lookup answers do not reveal whether an account exists.
  (f) *P3-23 / lead finding 4.* An integration `secret_ref` resolves only inside the tenant's own namespace of the
  secret store (a prefix carrying the tenant id, enforced at save and at resolve); the webhook receiver enforces a
  replay window where the source's scheme carries a timestamp. No live adapter ships before this.
  (g) *P3-17.* The password-reset link is not persisted in clear in a tenant's outbox: the outbox row carries a
  reference and the link is composed at send.
  (h) APR-NO-APPROVER-1 (R-41 (6)): a request with no eligible decider at submission notifies the access
  administrators and is marked in the queue.

- **R-38 (iv) addendum (2026-09-30 04:57; SECFIX-APR).** PRD BR-PLT-02 gives the reason for `AUTO-BOOTSTRAP`: "a new tenant has
  no second approver". The exception therefore also ends — whatever `setup_completed_at` says — as soon as another
  active member holds the approval permission of the ROLE_ASSIGNMENT step: from then on the bootstrap Tenant
  Admin's role assignments route to that person. This closes the prolonged window of a tenant that never meets the
  setup conditions (for example one that books only through API-client imports). 04 §14.3 states the condition.

- **R-49 (2026-09-30 04:59; SECFIX-IMP; refines R-30 on the lane's UNDECIDED report).** The documents require both: 03 REQ-DAT-001
  keeps the original file of an import, and 05 PRV-06 / RB-14 make the import source the erasure path for personal
  data inside an upload (the only place the clear values live; the row, its SHA-256, the tokenised import rows,
  lineage, totals and the approval remain as evidence). Both are served by a second person:
  (a) The source of a SUBMITTED, APPROVED, COMMITTING or COMMITTED import is not shreddable by one person. For a
  COMMITTED import the shred is requested (the privacy-side administrator, `settings.manage`, with a reason
  naming the data-subject request) and takes effect only on the approval of a finance-side holder — one step, the
  Controller role, covering the upload's entities (R-41) — through a new approval subject; on approval the SYSTEM
  principal shreds on behalf of the requester and the audit event names the approval. Before commit the path stays
  "reject or withdraw first". Docs first: 05 PRV-07 (b), runbook RB-14 step 4, 04 approval subject table and
  PRD §2.5 routing row.
  (b) Lock datasets, journal batch files, report outputs of locked runs, approval impact previews, computation
  evidence and audit digests are not shreddable while their record stands, with no override in 1.0: they are
  accounting records (05 PRV-09; GDPR Art. 17(3)(b), (e)) and the refusal is the recorded outcome of a request,
  as RB-14 already states for `contract_cost_asset.payee`. RB-14 and PRV-06 say so.
  (c) `GET /imports/{id}` and the rows / diff routes answer for an upload whose source was shredded (state and
  rows shown; the file content gone).

- **R-48 (b) correction (2026-09-30 05:04; SECFIX-PLT).** 04 §15.2 Table 15.2-S makes `mfa-enrolment-required` a synonym that is
  never emitted (D-73); the catalogue stands. The refusal of a session that must enrol or must pass the challenge
  is the canonical 403 `mfa-required` with PRD ERR-27's two detail copies; a client tells "enrol" from "verify"
  by `GET /session` (`mfa_enrolment_required` / `mfa_required`). Adopted as the lane designed it: the rule is
  evaluated by the function the sign-in answer uses (mandatory = a `requires_mfa` permission in any active
  membership, or an operator; a user with a confirmed factor must pass the challenge whatever their permissions);
  enforcement lives in `authenticate_session`; a pending session reaches only `GET /session`,
  `POST /session/logout` and the enrolment or challenge routes — not tenant selection, `GET /me` or the password
  change.

- **R-50 (2026-09-30 05:40; FIX-C round 2 report; the fourteen problem lines left in the CTL-038 audit-coverage walk).**
  (a) *Jobs (lines 4 to 8).* PRODUCT GAP against 04 T-PLT-27 ("the commands that start and finish jobs are
  audited"): one change in the jobs registry writes `job.start` in the deferring transaction (the deferring
  principal, the job kind and id, under the command's request id), `job.finish` in the transaction that records
  the terminal state (SYSTEM; state and problem slug) and `job.cancel` at cancellation. 04 T-PLT-27 names the three
  actions docs first; `domain/platform/jobs.py`'s docstring is corrected. Owner: lane FIX-C with SECFIX-CLO (it
  edits the registry for SC-8). The audit volume of job-driven computations is measured at G9.
  (b) *MFA (lines 12, 13).* Issuing a seed and replacing the recovery codes are security-relevant writes (SAR-26):
  two new E-79 kinds, `MFA_ENROLMENT_STARTED` and `RECOVERY_CODES_REGENERATED`, written to `security_event` by
  `mfa.enroll` and `regenerate_recovery_codes` (04 E-79, T-PLT-04, T-PLT-05 docs first; one enum revision).
  Owner: SECFIX-PLT, inside revision 0092.
  (c) *SSP calculator (line 11).* PRODUCT DEFECT: `_proposed_entry` copies the replaced entry without its
  `value_basis` and `quantity_unit`, so a run can never create a draft version for a series product. It copies
  both; a series product with no entry to copy stays refused by name until the dialog carries a basis input (item
  SSP-CALC-BASIS-1). Owner: SECFIX-SCOPE (it changes the calculator for SN-10).
  (d) *Scenarios.* Lines 1 and 2 (the walk's integration hooks use the secret-store double of ruling R-1): lane
  F-CLO-A, which owns the helper. Line 9 (sandbox request needs a SUCCEEDED snapshot): lane F-SNP. Lines 10 and 14
  (a second observation for the exclusion; the `migrations_reconcile` scenario): lane FIX-C. Line 3 is fixed on
  main by revision 0073.
  (e) *Release-stamp guard (item TEST-REL-STAMP-GUARD-1).* In dev / test a process without a stamped release
  falls back to the latest engine_release row, so a test whose world stamps nothing passes after any test that
  stamped and fails alone (measured: `tests/api/test_report_runs.py::test_start_after_end_rejected`). The
  conftest fixture will also forget the remembered environment, so every unstamped world fails in every order;
  the enumeration is run on main by the supervisor with the full database suite, and each unstamped world is
  STALE TEST WORLD for the lane that owns it.
  (f) `test_req_plt_019_categories_covered` stays red until the AI proposal lifecycle (AIX) writes its events.

- **R-51 (2026-09-30 05:58; F-CLO-A, CLO-12).**
  (a) *Journal kinds.* ENGINE_SPEC_B §14.1 and Table 14-A ("Manual adjustment") state that stage 14 posts the
  approved lines of MANUAL_JOURNAL / ACCOUNT_RECLASS adjustments; the engine has no such part, and by the delta
  rule a platform-posted manual journal would be reversed by the next computation of its group (posted amounts
  with no target). The engine part is built with CLO-12 — the approved lines as role targets of entry kind
  MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT, carried in the engine payload of MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT_APPLIED; a recompute over a posted
  adjustment emits nothing (engine and database witnesses). CLO-12's "Engine: none" is corrected in the build-spec
  fragment. Owner: lane F-CLO-A, in a module of its own under `s14_posting` with one call site (lane ENG-FX is
  changing `intents.py` and the regrouping rule in `assign.py` at the same time). The bundle builder resolves the
  T-SL-05 row into the payload for the three schedule kinds as well (S09-R-38 to R-40).
  (b) *Withdraw and discard.* PRD SM-10 has no way back from SUBMITTED and no way to discard a DRAFT, so a mistaken
  draft would count as pending for ever. The transitions of T-CON-06 are adopted for T-SL-05: SUBMITTED → DRAFT
  (withdrawn, or the request voided as stale), REJECTED → DRAFT (revise and resubmit), DRAFT → VOIDED (discard).
  PRD SM-10, 04 E-94 / T-SL-05 and the DB-03 pairs docs first; Alembic revision 0093.
  (c) *Deferral past lock.* The deferral is a MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT request of the same adjustment that replaces the
  pending posting request (flag DEFER_PAST_LOCK, same routing rows, approver ≠ preparer). Approved: the adjustment
  is marked deferred past lock, stays SUBMITTED without a pending request and holds neither gate; a later submit
  routes it for posting, and an approval after the lock posts into the first open period with its origin period
  (S08-R-08). Rejected: SUBMITTED, not deferred, still counted.
  (d) The API-S shapes of API-R-37, the server-derived `amount_functional_abs` and the request's entity (the
  contract's contracting entity, R-25) as the lane proposes, docs first in 04 §16.

- **R-52 (2026-09-30 06:07; SECFIX-CLO, consequence of SC-7).** (a) With journal coverage independent of the mode (revision
  0085) nothing can be journalised twice, so the reason of D-87 L6-3-Q-32 is gone: `tie_outs.journal_revenue` sums
  the REVENUE journal lines of every non-cancelled run of the entity, book and periods, whatever its mode;
  L6-3-Q-32 is superseded and its test moves to witness that a run requested in the other mode is counted once.
  Owner: lane FIX-C (one hunk; lane FIX-D2 works elsewhere in the same file). (b) PRODUCT DEFECT: `cancel_run`
  cancels any draft or approved run; coverage is the highest sealed position, so cancelling an earlier run while a
  later one stands leaves its seals uncoverable. Only the latest non-cancelled run of a key (entity, book, period)
  can be cancelled; an earlier one is refused 409 `invalid-transition` naming the later run. Owner: lane F-CLO-A
  with SC-N1 (the same function).

- **R-39 (given to lane OPS by message on 2026-09-30 about 04:35 and referred to by R-45 (c); recorded 2026-09-30 06:15).**
  (1) Startup refuses, under production, configuration-only members: the fake email backend; a master key that is
  a documentation placeholder or repeats another. "An ACTIVE connection has a loopback or private base URL" is NOT
  a startup refusal — a tenant-writable row must never be able to stop the platform for every tenant; it stays a
  doctor rollout gate. (2) The enforcing layer for connection URLs is the integrations domain: a non-public
  `base_url` is refused at create / update under production (R-45 (c); built by lane F-CLO-A Part 0, including the
  refusal of credentials in the URL and of addresses written in another notation). (3) compose-verify and
  zap-baseline run with per-run master keys held in the script's process environment only; the example
  environment file carries no usable key. (4) SAR-15 lists SMTP hosts; the relay is the operator's configuration,
  so an explicit operator opt-in (default off, reported by doctor as a notice) admits a private or loopback SMTP
  relay, still with STARTTLS and certificate verification; SAR-15 and the self-host guide say so.

- **R-53 (2026-09-30 06:21; OPS round 2 report).** (a) *SD-2.* The session cookie is Secure unless the configured public origin
  is an http LOOPBACK origin (configuration, never the request Host): a production deployment configured with a
  plain-http public host keeps the fail-closed effect (a Secure cookie the browser will not return), and doctor
  `session-cookie` fails it. Supersedes "follows the scheme" of R-34. (b) `max_locks_per_transaction` ships as
  4096 with floor 3072, as built (05 §2.7). (c) The per-run master keys of compose-verify / zap-baseline live in
  the script's process environment only, as built. (d) A build must not leave a generated `release-manifest.json`
  in the working tree where a development process then refuses on it after the schema head moves: the embedded
  manifest is written under the run directory and copied into the build context from there, or removed when the
  target ends. (e) SQLSTATE class 53 (insufficient resources, e.g. 53200 lock table full) is answered 503 with
  `Retry-After`, not 500; a job attempt is retried by its policy. (f) Operator CLI commands other than
  `doctor` and `verify` refuse under production when the startup subset fails (no workspace is provisioned
  under placeholder master keys); the compose example's placeholder database passwords are refused by the role
  init script. (g) The contract computation's reads of `schedule_line` and `subledger_line` without the
  partition column (2,002 of 2,257 locks and 181 index probes per lookup, measured) are item
  PRF-PARTITION-PRUNE-1 for the performance work before G9; the lock budget absorbs them meanwhile. (h) The
  invitation link stored in clear in the outbox row joins R-48 (g) in the platform security package.

- **R-54 (2026-09-30 06:39; F-CLO-B questions 1–7; FIX-D2 rework design; SC-N4).**
  (a) *Generation is a job* (04 API-R-40, SCREENS_B §2.1): job kind `RECONCILIATION_GENERATE` joins E-14 — Alembic
  revision **0095** (lane F-CLO-B), which also makes `source_file_id` and `sync_run_id` of T-CLS-06 set-once
  updatable for the trial-balance attachment and retires the DB-03 pair REOPENED → DRAFT (D-98 79). CLO-16's
  "Schema: none" is corrected in the build-spec fragment.
  (b) *The current reconciliation.* Every generation inserts a new DRAFT row; the current reconciliation of (kind,
  entity, book, period) is the latest generated one; the gate, the unsigned count, the cockpit and the lock's
  certification read current rows only; prepare / sign / explain / attach on a superseded row answer 409. Extends
  D-98 79 from "after a reopen" to every regeneration; API-S gains `is_current`.
  (c) *Reopen of a reconciliation.* 04 T-CLS-06 and SCREENS_B's Certified state govern: a CERTIFIED reconciliation
  leaves that state only through the period reopen. The `reopen` action needs `recon.signoff` (it discards a
  reviewer's sign-off) and is admitted from PREPARED and REVIEWED; on a CERTIFIED row it answers 409. SCREENS_B
  §2.2's roles row is corrected (SCREENS_B rev 1.27); the acceptance test asserts both halves.
  (d) *CLO-18 waits.* The engine's stage 10 `close_gate_facts` (ENGINE_SPEC_B §10, S10-R-19) is specified and not
  built, and no table stores it. The platform does not re-derive V8 from stored rows (the R-35 principle). Item
  ENG-S10-GATE-FACTS-1 (an engine output member, its storage and the effect on output hashes — a design note
  first) goes to the engine lane; CLO-18 is BLOCKED on it and the close run (CLO-19, CLO-20) proceeds without it.
  (e) *Trial-balance port.* `domain/journals/ports.TrialBalance` gains `lines` (account, currency, closing
  amount) and `details` (one GL document per account and period, with the ADP-10 external id when the ERP holds
  one), additive with defaults; lane F-CLO-B writes it, CLO-15's mocks (lane F-CLO-A) fill it.
  (f) The gate's count in `test_gates_fail_closed_before_producers` follows the gate's documented counting rule
  per kind; the count the test pins today for the state before any producer is kept. If the BUILD_SPEC sentence
  ("counts 1 until …") cannot be produced by that rule, both computations are reported, not chosen.
  (g) *FIX-D2, revision 0084 as designed:* both DB-07 guards FOR SHARE; the next period's row is locked by the lock
  decision only while it is `future`; `period_lock.cutoff_known_at` with its CHECK added NOT VALID and validated
  where no earlier LOCK row exists; `lock_snapshot.tie_out_results`; rule S15-R-18d for a failed internal identity
  (TO_RPO_ROLLFORWARD_EQ_RPO counted with them); gate codes WATERFALL_TIES_TO_JOURNAL and
  DISAGGREGATION_TIES_TO_JOURNAL after JE_COMPLETE, evaluated on the frozen datasets at the decision and
  provisionally at cockpit and request-lock time. The two gates fail like every other gate; the waiver's effect
  is SC-N4's.
  (h) *SC-N4 is decided by the documents.* SCREENS_B ("the gate counts as cleared once another user approves
  it") and 04 (`/waive` → EXCEPTION_WAIVER by another user): an approved waiver of a checklist item clears it at
  request-lock and at the lock decision — `certification.failing` taking only the computed results is a PRODUCT
  DEFECT; PRD BR-CLS-01 lists custom blocking tasks among the lock gates. Which automatic gates may be waived is
  read from T-CLS-02 / T-CLS-03 and SCREENS_B; if the documents would let an integrity gate (journals balance,
  journals complete, no dirty groups) be waived, the lane reports it before building.

- **R-55 (2026-09-30 06:58; lane SECFIX-CLO's SC-N4 reading of the documents; completes R-54 (h)).** Control design — a
  supervisor ruling for Ray's review with the ITGC guide; it changes no accounting figure.
  (a) *Custom blocking close tasks refuse the lock.* PRD BR-CLS-01 lists "custom close tasks signed" among the
  lock gates and SM-07 requires every gate; the code decided on the system gates only: PRODUCT DEFECT. Request-lock
  and the lock decision refuse while an item of an active MANUAL template with `is_blocking = true` is not
  PASSED, WAIVED or NOT_APPLICABLE (the BLK-16 population); `is_blocking = false` never refuses. The 409
  `close-gates-failed` carries one `errors[]` entry per such task with `rule_id` = the item's template code
  (04 §16.8 amended: `rule_id` is the checklist item's code — the gate check code of a system gate, the template
  code of a custom task; T-CLS-02 keeps codes unique per tenant) and the message "Close task not signed: <name>";
  the tasks count in ERR-14's number. The T-CLS-04 certification keeps the system gates; a task's evidence is its
  T-CLS-08 sign-off.
  (b) *An approved waiver clears its item* at request-lock and at the lock decision (SCREENS_B SF-05: "The gate
  counts as cleared once another user approves it"; 04 §16.8 `/waive` → EXCEPTION_WAIVER by another user). A
  pending, rejected or voided waiver has no effect. The certification records a waived gate as
  `{gate_check_code, status WAIVED, count, evaluated_at, waiver_approval_request_id, waived_count}`: `count`
  is the gate's computed result at the decision, `waived_count` the item's stored result when the waiver was
  approved — so the Controller who decides the lock, and the evidence afterwards, see what the waiver covered and
  what it covers now.
  (c) *Never waivable:* `JE_BALANCED`, `JE_COMPLETE`, `CONTROLLER_CERTIFIED`. The first two are identities of
  the ledger against its journals that a journal run can always restore, and a period locked without them could
  never be journalised completely afterwards; the third is the certification itself. `/waive` on them answers 409
  `invalid-transition` by name; API-S checklist items gain `is_waivable` and the cockpit offers "Request waiver"
  accordingly (SCREENS_B rev 1.28). Every other gate, the two journal-tie gates of R-42 (c) included, is waivable:
  `NO_DIRTY_GROUPS` too, because a group the engine refuses stays dirty until its contract is repaired or voided
  and would otherwise hold every close of its entity — what a dirty group has not computed by the lock is
  recognised in the next open period as a late event with its origin period, as any late information is.
  (d) *Open control question, item WAIVER-SPECIFICITY-1 (for Ray / the ITGC owner; not built):* a waiver covers its
  gate for the period whatever the gate's count becomes afterwards; whether it should lapse when the count grows
  (the item returning to FAILED and needing a new waiver) needs a checklist transition the documents do not have.
  In 1.0 the two counts on the lock request are the control.

- **R-56 (2026-09-30 07:07; lane SECFIX-CLO's readings of R-45 (d); lane SECFIX-APR's consequence of the R-38 (iv) addendum).**
  (a) *"A later run over the same window", per run kind.* INBOUND_POLL: a later SUCCEEDED run of the same
  connection whose `checkpoint_before` equals the failed run's (the failed run did not move the replay position,
  so the later one read the same changes). RECONCILIATION_SWEEP and COA_SYNC: a later SUCCEEDED run of the same
  connection and kind that ran to completion (each reads the whole set; for a sweep, the run that stamps
  `swept_at`). WEBHOOK_BATCH, JOURNAL_EXPORT and TRIAL_BALANCE_PULL: no window of their own in `sync_run` — they
  stop counting only through their failure items, until the items that build export and trial-balance runs
  (CLO-14, CLO-15) give those runs a subject; two runs without a checkpoint are never "the same window".
  TEST_CONNECTION runs are not counted. "Every failure item resolved or waived" needs at least one item: a run
  with none is not cleared by that limb. The gate counts every uncleared run of a connection whose `entity_ids`
  is empty or holds the period's entity, whatever the run's date (fail closed). A run that neither limb can
  clear holds the gate until the gate itself is waived (R-55); if the fixtures hold such a run shape, the lane
  names it (item SYNC-RUN-CLEAR-1).
  (b) *Demo seed.* With the bootstrap exception ending once another ACTIVE member holds `access.approve`, the seed
  follows the product: the bootstrap Tenant Admin invites every persona without a custom role before anyone
  accepts (all setup grants, PRD J-01.3 and BS1-D-20 unchanged in substance); the persona with the custom role is
  granted through the second Tenant Admin's approval. `test_bs1_d_20_commands_as_personas` moves from fifteen
  setup grants to thirteen plus two approved grants: STALE EXPECTATION under R-38 (iv), no financial figure.

- **R-57 (2026-09-30 07:41; lane SECFIX-CLO, exposed by SC-8 running the monitors at start-close and request-lock).**
  04 Table 15.4-E ("`ERROR` results block lock"; a tenant rule may raise a monitor's severity), 03 REQ-CLS-019
  and PRD BR-CLS-01 ("data-quality findings of severity ERROR cleared") give data-quality findings their own lock
  rule and their own gate (`DATA_QUALITY_CLEAR`). D-88 L7-2-Q-1 ("exceptions count while OPEN or IN_PROGRESS
  with severity BLOCKING or WARNING") was written before the monitors existed and is read as silent on them: an
  exception item of source `DATA_QUALITY` counts in `EXCEPTIONS_CLEARED` and in `exceptions_open` only when
  its severity is BLOCKING; a WARNING finding stays in the queue with its notification and holds no lock.
  PRODUCT DEFECT against Table 15.4-E (an ordinary period with an inactive contract or unbilled revenue could be
  locked only by waiving the exceptions gate). Tests that expect a data-quality WARNING to hold the gate are
  STALE EXPECTATIONS under this ruling. Open, for the close lane to measure before anything is built (item
  DQ-WAIVER-STICKY-1): whether a finding that was resolved or waived and whose condition persists is raised again
  at the next monitor run, and what Table 15.4-E's "while unresolved" then means for an ERROR finding.

- **R-58 (2026-09-30 08:32; lane QA-BE's B-1 on the supervisor's browser-QA finding Q-15; lane F-CLO-B's CLO-17 notes).**
  (a) *ENG-CAL-PREBOOK-1 is a platform defect.* ENGINE_SPEC §0.4 / CV-12 and 05 RCP-03 give the engine the calendar
  "from the group's inception through the horizon"; `bundles.py` handed it every period of the calendar, so an
  entity whose calendar holds periods before its book's first period (04 T-REF-03) was refused on CV-13 by the
  first stateless period (the demo's AVM-JP; any mid-fiscal-year go-live; a second book kept later). Rule, as the
  lane measured it: per entity the bundle carries the calendar from the period containing the group's inception
  date on, preceded by the earlier periods that carry a state for every enabled book the entity keeps. Engine
  unchanged; no input or output hash of a computation that succeeds today moves. 05 RCP-15 states the range.
  (b) *A contract that begins before the first period of a book its entity keeps* (a second book kept from a later
  period; a contract booked or migrated with an inception before go-live) is not supported in 1.0 — option A:
  `PUT /entities/{id}/books/{code}` refuses a first period that starts after the inception date of a contract of
  the entity (422 on `first_period_key`, by name); a contract booked with an earlier inception stays a quarantine
  whose problem names the rule, the period and the book. An engine rule for "before the book" (nothing posts
  there; the opening position of the later book, REQ-BK-007) needs the accountant and is not built: open
  question for the package; items CTR-PREBOOK-INCEPTION-1 (refusal at booking instead of a quarantine) and
  LMG-PREREQ-PERIOD-1 stand.
  (c) *The quarantine names its cause.* PRD IMP-75 governs the copy ("<contract> failed invariant <invariant> and
  was quarantined. …"); for ENGINE_INVARIANT_VIOLATION the invariant is the engine rule with its detail (rule,
  period key, book). The other engine codes keep their copy.
  (d) *PRD SM-07's guard on future → open ("previous period not future") is not enforced* — the lane opened
  FY2026-P07 of a book while P01 to P06 were future, after which that book posts nothing in any period (stage 14
  stops at the first future period). PRODUCT DEFECT; item PERIOD-OPEN-GUARD-1, lane SECFIX-CLO with R-6.
  (e) *A signed reconciliation that later postings have overtaken does not satisfy the gate.* The current
  reconciliation of a kind counts as reviewed only while its `as_of_known_at` is not earlier than the record time
  of the latest subledger line of its entity, book and period (and, for billing to subledger, of the latest
  source invoice of the period); otherwise the gate names it ("out of date: generate it again"), as the attach
  of a trial balance is already refused. Lane F-CLO-B. A zero-variance subledger-to-GL reconciliation is
  prepared and reviewed, never auto-certified, in 1.0 (T-CLS-06 records the certifying rule at insert only; the
  documents show AUTO-REC-01 for billing to subledger): known limitation REC-AUTO-GL-1.

- **R-59 (2026-09-30 08:36; lane WEB-QA, found while fixing the Policies crash of browser-QA finding Q-9).** Four editors send a
  date-only string into a field the API types date-time (accounting policy and account mapping `effective_from`,
  SoD exception `valid_from` / `valid_to`) and are refused 422: saving an effective date or requesting an SoD
  exception cannot work from the screens — PRODUCT DEFECT (frontend). The documents do not say which instant a
  picked date becomes: DS-I18N-08 calls effective dates "plain dates in the entity's time zone", the screens show
  the UTC date part (DS-FMT-17), the backend's period-start rule reads the instant in each entity's time zone, the
  demo seed writes 12:00 UTC, one editor sends 00:00 UTC. Engineering rule for 1.0 [J]: **a picked effective date
  of a versioned configuration is sent as 12:00:00Z of that date**, by one helper used by every editor (the
  rule-set editor included); the screens keep showing the UTC date part, so what is picked is what is shown, and
  the entity-local date equals it for every zone from UTC−12 to UTC+11. An entity beyond UTC+11 (New Zealand,
  Fiji) reads the next day: known limitation, item CFG-EFFECTIVE-DATE-1 (the API should take a date and the
  server own the instant per entity — a backend and data-model change, not for this lane). An SoD exception's
  window is platform time: `valid_from` = 00:00:00Z of the first day, `valid_to` = 23:59:59Z of the last day.
