- **R-61 (2026-09-30 09:09; SECFIX-ACT report on SN-13 / SN-12, merged as main 0c33ba0d; refines R-20 and R-23).**
  (a) *Confirmed as built.* The criteria-met date and record of a book are those of its latest non-voided
  probable `COLLECTIBILITY_ASSESSED` dated on or after the gate and carrying a REVIEWED record ("effective date as
  submitted" of R-20 (c) reads so); the books that have one are the books that move and at least one is needed.
  `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED` of a criteria-met activation is dated the latest criteria-met date. The approval appends
  twice inside the existing DB-03 pairs (no pair NOT_A_CONTRACT → ACTIVE, no migration). A rejected or voided
  request returns the read model to NOT_A_CONTRACT with no SM-02 row of its own (the stored row never moved).
  `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED` on the events route answers 409 (was 422).
  (b) *Family closures beyond the ruling's list — accepted.* An assessment dated before the contract's
  `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED` is refused (422); a void request of a `COLLECTIBILITY_ASSESSED`, `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` or
  `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED` is refused unconditionally (409) — an assessment is corrected by a later assessment, an
  activation by voiding the contract, and no approval of a lower floor (MANUAL_EVENT) replays Table 2.2-A; an
  assessment may not ride in a request that creates an ATTRIBUTE_CHANGE submission (422). The void of a
  `SIGNIFICANT_CHANGE_FLAGGED` stays admitted (the contract has an approved activation).
  (c) *Refinement of R-20 (c): never auto-approved.* A criteria-met activation is not a "standard item"
  (REQ-PLT-016): `submit_activation` withholds auto-approval whenever the submission carries criteria-met books,
  whatever the routing flags and whoever wrote the stream. Docs first (PRD §2.5 auto-approval rows, 04 §16.10);
  witness: a stream written only by an API client, a gate, a reviewed re-assessment and a published
  auto-approval rule that would match — the request stays PENDING. Owner: SECFIX-ACT, next slice.
  (d) *Refinement of R-23.* "`hold_source` is not MANUAL" reads "`hold_source` is SYSTEM": a USER_RULE hold has
  no condition that clears by itself (PRD §5.2), so its manual release stays (permission, comment, audit).
  (e) *The preview's catch-up figure — engineering default, HUMAN REVIEW POINT (candidate AD-45, never recorded as
  approved).* In the impact preview of a criteria-met activation the catch-up of an obligation is
  `revenue_cum` of the dry-run version less `revenue_cum` of the stored version (the S02-R-04 measure at the
  dry-run version's date); it is the performance to the criteria-met date unless a later-dated event is already
  recorded, in which case it includes the days since; `revenue_by_period` shows what JET-02 posts in the
  transition period. No posting depends on the figure.
  (f) *Open items.*
  ACT-STEP1-UI-1 (release blocker for the candidate): the workbench offers "Record criteria met", which the API
  now refuses; the Step 1 drawer dates every assessment at inception and sends topic COLLECTIBILITY for a
  not-probable outcome; the approval view does not name the criteria-met books, dates, records and catch-up.
  Backend half (API-S-Approval `impact_preview.summary` members `catch_up_total` and `criteria_met[]`, OpenAPI):
  SECFIX-ACT. Screens (SCREENS §4.1.6 amended docs first; re-assessment, "Submit for activation" on a
  NOT_A_CONTRACT contract, the approval view): lane WEB-QA as W-10.
  CTR-STEP1-REENTRY-1 (functional gap, not a regression): a book left NOT_A_CONTRACT under an ACTIVE header —
  books that differ, a 25-5 failure later cured, an engine-determined NOT_A_CONTRACT — has no criteria-met path.
  Design: the same approved path for a contract whose header is ACTIVE and which has a book in NOT_A_CONTRACT with
  a reviewed criteria-met re-assessment; the approval appends `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` for those books and no
  `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED`; the header does not move (PRD SM-02 note and 04 T-CON-01 docs first). Owner: SECFIX-ACT
  after its security slices; a known limitation until built. 04 T-CON-01 states meanwhile that the header is the
  projection of the events and the per-book status (T-CON-08) governs the accounting.
  STEP1-BOOK-ADOPTION-1 (invariant (d) of R-20 in a book kept later): `PUT /entities/{id}/books/{code}` (keep or
  re-enable) is refused while a contract whose group would be computed in that book is behind a not-a-contract
  gate (header NOT_A_CONTRACT); the refusal names the contract. Owner: lane QA-BE, in the guard of R-58 (b)
  (PRD ERR-62). An ACTIVE contract is computed in a book adopted later on the strength of its approved
  activation; that no per-book Step 1 re-assessment is demanded at adoption is candidate AD-46 for the
  accountant and a known limitation.
  STEP1-JR-STALE-1: recording the assessment that cites a SUBMITTED record moves the contract's head and makes
  that record's pending review stale (04 §16.10 rev 1.49, D-98 101c), so the not-a-contract gate leaves a record
  that must be resubmitted. Recorded; not now.
  ENG-S02R04-REASON-1 (engine against its specification; measured by SECFIX-ACT, nothing changed): stage 14
  emits no `reason_code = 'STEP1_MET'` and stage 09 classes `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` as NORMAL (`catch_up_amount`

  performance of earlier periods is recognised at the transition, the 606-10-50-12A amount depends on it.
  Owner: lane ENG-FX measures (keys, S15 consumers) and reports before any change.

- **R-62 (2026-09-30 09:11; SECFIX-CLO question on R-50 (a) and its measurement of item DQ-WAIVER-STICKY-1).**
  (a) *Job audit scope.* The three transport kinds — `OUTBOX_RELAY`, `EMAIL_DELIVERY`, `WEBHOOK_DELIVERY` — write
  no `job.start` and no `job.finish`: their evidence is the originating command's event and the message and
  delivery rows (04 T-INT-03 and T-PLT-36 are AUD-OPS; §1.7 "no audit event for routine writes"). Every other
  kind writes both, whoever defers it: a command's unit of work (the principal, the command's request id), a
  job's own unit of work or the scheduler's fan-out (SYSTEM; request id `job-<id>` or the sweep's). 04 T-PLT-27
  names the three actions and the exempt set docs first; a test compares the registry's kinds with
  "audited ∪ exempt" so that a new kind cannot fall outside both.
  (b) *Cancellation.* A job cancelled while QUEUED gets `job.cancel` only (`after.state` CANCELLED); a RUNNING
  job gets `job.cancel` at the request and `job.finish` (state CANCELLED) when the handler stops; `job.cancel` is
  written for every kind; a repeated cancel that changes nothing and a `defer_single` that returns the pending
  job write nothing.
  (c) *DQ-WAIVER-STICKY-1 — PRODUCT DEFECT* against PRD BR-CLS-01 ("exceptions resolved, waived or dismissed")
  and SM-06 (the approved waiver executes the clearance): the monitors raise a waived finding again as a new
  OPEN item on the next run, so an approved `EXCEPTION_WAIVER` of a BLOCKING data-quality finding never reaches
  the gate evaluation since SC-8 runs the monitors first. Rule: when a monitor's finding has no OPEN or
  IN_PROGRESS item and the latest item of its `dedupe_key` is WAIVED under an APPROVED waiver request, and the
  finding's code, severity and message equal that item's, no item is raised (the waived item's `last_seen_at`
  moves; AUD-OPS); a finding that differs in severity or message — the facts changed after the waiver — is
  raised as a new OPEN item. The key carries entity and period, so a waiver never reaches another period. A
  RESOLVED item whose condition persists is raised again, as today (the resolution claimed the condition was
  gone). `dismiss` on an item of source DATA_QUALITY is refused if it is admitted today (BR-DAT-04: only input
  that was never committed is dismissed without approval). Owner: SECFIX-CLO, docs first (04 T-IMP-05, 05 close
  monitors); with it one approved item-level waiver clears both `DATA_QUALITY_CLEAR` and `EXCEPTIONS_CLEARED`.

- **R-63 (2026-09-30 09:12; F-RPS-REG report on RPS-8 / RPT-17, the R-13 gate and the facts it measured; also fixes the
  time of R-62 to the minute it was logged).**
  (a) *RPT-23 to RPT-27.* PRD J-17.6 (the Auditor runs the user access listing and the SoD conflict report),

  `approvals_register` (RPT-26, amounts and comments of every request) need `report.run` AND `audit.read`. A
  viewer and the two SSP roles lose the five registers. Docs first: PRD §5.6 note under `report.run` /

  speaks of a fact per late event, ENGINE_SPEC_B S15-R-18b attributes lines) is item REG-LATE-NOLINE-1: the
  engineering default, when the facts exist, is that it is listed with zero amounts (a register of late entries
  is judged on completeness); recorded for the accountant's package.
  (c) *Imported events in RPT-16 and RPT-17.* The user and the approval of an event written by SYSTEM for an
  import are those of its upload (the uploader; the upload's IMPORT_COMMIT request), resolved by one helper for
  both registers; `import_upload` joins the definitions' sources. An event with no upload keeps its own fields.
  (d) *RPT-15.* `created_by` names the run's preparer (the BR-JE-01 runner), not the calculation job's system
  principal — accepted as built. The approval-column defect (`approval_decision.decided_at`), which answered
  500 at the LOCK of any period holding a really approved journal run, is a PRODUCT DEFECT of RPT-15, fixed.
  (e) *Entity-scoped grants cannot be created — PRODUCT GAP against REQ-PLT-012 and PRD J-22.1.*
  `POST /role-assignments`, `POST /users` (`roles[].entity_codes`) and `POST /api-clients` refuse every entity

  (f) *ENG-S12-DUE-DATE-1 — P1, measured by F-RPS-REG, nothing changed.* A `BILLING_RECORDED` whose `due_date`
  lies in a later period than its issue date fails S12-INV-03 and the contract's computation is QUARANTINED

  it SUCCEEDS). Invoices on ordinary payment terms are of this shape; it is hidden because an import commit

- **R-64 (2026-09-30 09:15; independent review, by code reading, of SECFIX-APR commit e40bf7c7 against R-41 (1) to (4) and
  (8); record kept by the supervisor as `.run/sup2/reviews/secfix-apr-entity-scope-review-e40bf7c7.md`; refines
  R-41).** The review found no way for a signed-in person to read, decide, withdraw or bulk-approve outside the
  rules. Its seven findings are ruled as follows; owner lane SECFIX-APR, as the part after its Parts 1–2 report.
  (1) *Content is reader-independent.* The approval content and every staleness basis of a subject are read under
  the tenant's SYSTEM scope (tenant isolation only), never under the entity scope of the preparer or the decider;
  the entity check of R-41 (1) is what refuses a decider who does not cover the subject. The hook's recompute of
  an approved subject is reader-independent in the same way. A subject whose approval recomputes or reverses a
  combination group — MODIFICATION, CONTRACT_VOID and any other the lane finds — is bound to the contracting
  entities of every current member of the group, as COMBINATION_GROUP is (a singleton group gives the contract's
  own entity, as today). Until this is built, a request of a cross-entity group decided by a person scoped to one
  of its entities is voided as STALE_SUBJECT: it must be on main before entity-scoped grants can be created
  through the product (R-63 (e)) — the two lanes' merges are ordered accordingly.
  (2) *A superseding version binds what it ends.* ACCOUNT_MAPPING_VERSION is bound to the entities its rules name
  AND the entities named by the version in force that its approval ends at `effective_from`; dropping an entity's
  rules is not tenant configuration. The lane lists every other subject of this shape and applies the same rule.
  (3) `reject` requires a signed-in person before it reads the request (no 403 / 404 difference for an API
  client).
  (4) *"Waiting for me" is `can_decide`.* The inbox list, its total and the home count hold exactly the requests
  the caller can decide now: the step's role, the excluded deciders of the subject and a barred delegator are
  part of the condition.
  (5) *Notifications follow the engine.* NTF-01 / NTF-04 go to a delegate only through an unbarred delegator and
  only when the step's role is met; a delegate with an empty scope is told of a tenant-level request it can
  decide.
  (6) *Submission.* A preparer whose own scope does not cover every entity the subject names is refused at
  submission with one named 403, for one entity and for several (today: a bare 403 for one, an unreadable own
  request for several).
  (7) (a) A DRAFT judgement record — a COMBINATION record included — is changed only by its creator, so the
  author rule of R-41 (3) cannot be stepped around by a second editor; the lane reports first if a document names
  shared drafting. (c) `can_decide` answers false, and never raises, for a pending row of a subject type that has
  no specification yet. (d) The `entity` filter of the approvals list accepts only entities inside the caller's
  scope. (b) stays with R-27; (e) requests frozen under the earlier rule are voided at their first decision — no
  backfill before a first release; item UPGRADE-NOTES-1 records it for the release notes.
  The review's "untested" list becomes tests where a finding above is fixed; the HTTP-level parity of approve,
  reject, withdraw and bulk on an entity-bound request is tested in any case.

- **R-66 (2026-09-30 09:32; independent review, by code reading, of SECFIX-APR commit e40bf7c7 against R-38 and R-41 (7);
  record `.run/sup2/reviews/secfix-apr-auto-approval-floor-review-e40bf7c7.md`; refines R-38 and R-41).** The
  review found the kernel sound: no published, stored or forced rule lowers a floor, approves a subject off the
  allow-list or outranks a seeded-only subject. Its findings sit where facts are established.
  (1) *Authorship follows the preparer (finding 1; refines R-38 (i)).* Content a person put into a contract
  through an approval is written by a person although the hook appends as SYSTEM: an event whose
  `approval_request_id` names a request prepared by a USER, an `is_manual` event, a `COMBINATION_CHANGED`, and
  an APPROVED policy override of the contract each make the activation a human approval (MANUAL_ENTRY).
  (2) *The bootstrap exception is one-way (finding 2; refines R-38 (iv)).* Once a person other than the bootstrap
  Tenant Admin has been ACTIVE in the workspace while holding `access.approve`, AUTO-BOOTSTRAP never matches
  again, whatever that person's later status, revocation or expiry. So that the workspace is not left without an
  approver by a command: suspending or removing a member, or revoking or shortening a grant, is refused with a
  named 409 when it would leave fewer than two ACTIVE holders of `access.approve` and a holder has existed
  beside the bootstrap admin. Expiry and an identity provider's deprovisioning cannot be refused: that state is
  shown by APR-NO-APPROVER-1 (R-41 (6)) and repaired by an operator command under a support reference, audited —
  item APR-RECOVERY-1 (lane OPS: command and runbook), a candidate blocker for the release decision.
  (3) *Routing facts are re-measured at decision (finding 3).* A subject whose routing flag is measured on moving
  data — ESTIMATE_VERSION's `PL_IMPACT_GE_50K` — is measured again at each decision; a flag set that differs
  from the one the request was routed by voids the request as STALE_SUBJECT (REQ-PLT-014), and the decision's
  audit event records the measure that took effect. The content does not pin the stream head (a request on a
  contract with integration traffic would never reach a decision).
  (4) *The measure (finding 4).* The P&L impact of an estimate version sums every member contract of the
  combination group; it is measured in the tenant's primary book, else in every enabled book of the entity with
  the largest absolute amount taken; when no book yields a figure the flag is SET (fail closed). An estimate
  version approved on a DRAFT contract recognises nothing, so its weight reaches the approval of the
  activation: the activation thresholds read the larger of the booked consideration and the transaction price of
  the activation's dry run. The lane measures the DRAFT case before it builds (the review inferred it).
  (5) Finding 5 is the merge plan: the no-auto-approval half of R-38 (ii) is lane SECFIX-IMP's f60274fe; the
  candidate is cut only with both lanes on main.
  (6) *Seeded rule sets (finding 6).* AUTO-BOOTSTRAP and AUTO-MIG-01 take no tenant version: creating one is
  refused by name. No backfill before a first release (item UPGRADE-NOTES-1).
  (7) *Steps (finding 7).* A person who is the only eligible decider of a later step is refused an earlier step
  of the same request with a named 409 (the request would otherwise stop at the later step; withdrawal and
  resubmission is the repair for requests already there). That PERIOD_LOCK cannot be decided by a delegate who
  is not a Controller is intended (R-41 (7)).
  (8) *Evaluation equals submission (finding 8).* Submission passes the subject's entity codes to the matcher; the
  three smaller differences are removed; a rule-set test evaluates each seeded and demo rule through both paths.
  (9) `submit` refuses the combination `auto_approval=False` with a routing decision that auto-approves. Authoring
  and publication refuse a rule whose operator the matcher cannot evaluate on its field (today such a rule on
  `flags` makes every submission raise, including the one that would publish its correction) — platform
  validation, no engine change.
  Owners: (1), (3), (4) — the contract domain — lane SECFIX-ACT after SECFIX-APR's Parts 1–2 are on main; (2),
  (6), (7), (8), (9) lane SECFIX-APR. `domain/demo/volume.py` approves estimate versions with one reviewer: the
  lane that lands (3) keeps the volume seed green.

- **R-67 (2026-09-30 09:34; WEB-QA question 2 on W-9 — where the permanent check "every sort and filter the frontend sends
  is one the route admits" belongs).** Facts from the lane: the allow-lists are `ListSpec` constants enforced by
  `api/lists.py::paginate` (sort keys, directions, filter names and choices, search, limit); none of it is in the
  OpenAPI document; the frontend sends 55 sort keys from 22 route files, most of them only when a header is
  clicked.
  (a) *Static check, now.* `backend/tests/architecture/test_list_query_contract.py` (CPU only; the lane's file
  scope is extended by this one test file, no product backend code): it reads every `ListSpec` with the route it
  serves, reads the frontend's sort literals, default sorts and filter names per list path, and fails on a key
  or filter the route does not admit. A literal it cannot tie to a route fails with the line to add (one
  annotation convention or one table in the test — the lane chooses and documents it in dev-guide DG-FE).
  (b) *Crawl, as item W-13.* Two product defects reached browser QA because no e2e step opened their routes
  (Q-9, Q-30). A Playwright project of the existing `make e2e` gate opens every route of the router as a seeded
  persona that may see it (parameter routes through a small table of seeded ids), asserts that no route error
  boundary renders and that no API request of the page answers 4xx or 5xx outside a named allow-list, and clicks
  every sortable column header once with the same assertions. Filters are driven where the shared filter bar
  allows it deterministically; where not, the lane says so. A route the crawl cannot open is listed with the
  reason, never silently skipped.
  (c) The per-page vitest that pins a query string against constants typed in the test is not the device (it pins
  the frontend against itself); the one for access reviews is kept as the W-9 witness.
  (d) *Item API-LIST-CONTRACT-1 (not now).* The OpenAPI document carries the list contract (`x-erev-list`: sort
  keys, default, filters with choices, search) and the frontend's sort keys and filter names are typed from it.

- **R-68 (2026-09-30 09:34; F-CLO-B report on CLO-16, merged as main 144cd2d3).**
  (a) *A billing document dated in a period that is already locked* falls in no open period's population today
  and so escapes the control (CTL-024 completeness). Rule: the billing-to-subledger population of a period is the
  documents dated in it AND the documents dated in an earlier period of the entity and book that were recorded
  after that period's LOCK record; such a document is compared in the first period open at its recording and
  carries its origin period in the item (the late-event pattern of ENGINE_SPEC S08). Both sides follow the same
  rule. Docs first (04 T-CLS-06 / T-CLS-07 note, 05 close section, SCREENS_B §2.1 column "Origin period" if the
  grid has none). Owner: lane F-CLO-B, after CLO-17 and the R-58 (e) commit. A control-design point for the ITGC
  owner with the other close rulings.
  (b) *Generation against a concurrent posting.* After FIX-D2's revision 0084 is on main (the DB-07 guard takes
  the period state FOR SHARE), the generation takes the period state FOR UPDATE so that a posting that began
  before a generation and commits after it cannot fall between the reconciliation and R-58 (e)'s "later" test.
  One line and a witness, lane F-CLO-B; item REC-GEN-LOCK-1.
  (c) *"Pull from <connection name>" (CLO-25).* API-S-Reconciliation names the general-ledger connections of its
  entity (id and name only) for a holder of `recon.prepare`; `/integrations` gains no new reader.
  (d) Recorded, not now: a custom role with `recon.prepare` and without `import.upload` cannot upload a trial
  balance (item REC-TB-UPLOAD-PERM-1); the rule editor's field list mirrors the engine's by hand and lacks the
  HOLD line facts and the three reconciliation fields, so AUTO-REC-01 cannot be authored in the editor (item
  W-14 for the web lane after CLO-17).
  (e) *Alembic chain on main.* A revision keeps the number the register gave it; its `down_revision` is main's
  head at the moment it merges (0095 since 144cd2d3), so the chain follows merge order, not number order. A lane
  that merges main into its branch re-points its own unmerged revision to main's head and moves the head pin of
  `tests/pg/test_migrations.py` to its own number; at the merge into main the supervisor checks one head.

- **R-69 (2026-09-30 09:41; F-CLO-B accounting question on CLO-17 — supervisor ruling pending the accountant and the ITGC
  owner, never recorded as approved).** Measured by the lane (WLD-K-01 through January 2026, POL-004 = ERP): the
  subledger posts Dr 2100 / Cr 4010 16,569.86 and no billing line; a general ledger kept as D-13 says holds 2100
  at −103,430.14, so a comparison of posted lines with the ledger differs on the contract liability account by
  the billing of every period, and every ERP invoice document would be flagged as a direct entry.
  (a) *The documents decide the substance.* Research 07 RC-02 compares "balances … for deferred revenue, contract
  assets / unbilled AR, and revenue"; RP-01 (the contract balance rollforward) "ties to RC-02 closing balances";
  POLICIES POL-123: in ERP mode "the engine mirrors what the ERP posted so that the GL contract liability ties to
  the subledger"; JET-03's ERP row: ingested for the position and the reconciliation. So the subledger side of
  RC-02 is, for the accounts of the CONTRACT_LIABILITY and CONTRACT_ASSET roles, the closing contract balances
  the engine stores — the source the contract-balance rollforward reads, not a second derivation (the R-35
  principle) — by entity, book and currency, compared with the ledger's total of the accounts that carry the
  role (per account where the role has one account); for every other account it is the posted lines by account,
  as BUILD_SPEC CLO-17 states. This also carries the opening balances of a migrated tenant, which posted lines
  never hold.
  (b) *Differences.* Before the period's JET-06 reclass and the last journal batches are exported the two sides
  differ by them: class unposted batch / timing. A ledger document whose number matches an ingested invoice or
  credit memo of the entity is ERP billing, not DIRECT_GL_ENTRY. A foreign-currency invoice the ERP translated
  at its own rate leaves a difference the preparer explains.
  (c) *Subledger-controlled accounts* (no document defines the set): every account that carries a subledger line
  of the entity and book through the period end, and the accounts the mapping in force gives the
  CONTRACT_LIABILITY and CONTRACT_ASSET roles for the entity. The reconciliation states its two limits: a
  clearing account also carries another subledger's documents; in ERP mode the contract liability account is
  shared with the billing system.
  (d) *Build.* CLO-17 lands the posted-lines basis first (what BUILD_SPEC states). The role basis of (a) follows
  as its own commit after a short pre-build note from the lane: where the closing balances are stored and in
  which amounts, how a role's accounts are read, what the grid shows for a role with several accounts, the
  classification of (b), and what the NetSuite mock's computed trial balance must hold (lane F-CLO-A, CLO-15).
  No engine change in 1.0; if the stored balances cannot carry it, the lane says so before building anything
  else (candidate item ENG-ERP-BILLING-MIRROR-1).
  (e) Recorded for the accountant's package as candidate AD-47 (the definition of the subledger side in ERP
  billing mode) and for the ITGC owner with the other close rulings.

  entities of every current member of the combination group, AND the content and every staleness basis are read

  through the decider's row-level security.

- **R-71 (2026-09-30 09:48; F-CLO-A design points for CLO-14; refines R-32 and R-52 (b)).**
  (a) *SC-N1 without a network call inside a transaction.* After the relay's committed claim (message DISPATCHING)
  `dispatch_batch` takes the run and the batch FOR UPDATE, re-reads the state and releases before it calls the
  adapter; `cancel_run`, under its run and batch locks, answers 409 `invalid-transition` while a batch of the run
  has its export message DISPATCHING. Every interleaving ends "cancelled, adapter never called" or "cancel
  refused, export completes"; a claim abandoned by a dead relay returns to PENDING by the relay's own recovery,
  after which the cancel is admitted. Two witnesses (deterministic; interleaved).
  (b) *The failed state* (item says "Schema: none"; E-34 has no pair approved → failed). A permanent failure, and
  the last transient attempt, move the batch approved → exported → failed in one transaction with a
  `posting_ack` of kind REJECTED that carries the adapter's message (PRD SM-08), raise the exception item and
  EXPORT_FAILED and roll the run up (SMAP-08). The `exported` leg is not observable: no notification, webhook or
  acknowledgement wait is produced for it. Retry from `failed` writes a new outbox message with dedupe key
  `<external id>#<attempt>`; the ERP external id of the chunk is unchanged (BR-JE-02).
  `test_relay_permanent_failure_dead_at_once` moves from the interim "approved" (decision L6-3-Q-13) to
  "failed": STALE EXPECTATION.
  (c) Numbers: exception code `JOURNAL_EXPORT_FAILED` is PRD IMP-130; register index 36 for CLO-14 — 04 1.127,
  05 1.66, dev-guide 1.110, PRD 1.56, build-spec header 1.58; fragment 14 own revision 1.11.

- **R-72 (2026-09-30 09:59; F-RPS-REG pre-build report on RPT-ROLLFWD-KINDS-1; corrects the diagnosis of browser-QA finding
  Q-20; supervisor ruling pending the accountant where marked).** Measured by the lane: the demo's difference of
  30,087.60 is not a deposit transfer — K-09 has none — but an ordinary invoice: under `billing.posting = ERP`
  (the default) an invoice posts no line, the builder walks posted CONTRACT_LIABILITY lines only, so the
  identity TO_ROLLFORWARD_BALANCES fails in every period that holds an invoice (K-01 January and February, K-04
  April, K-09 September); a late event of a locked period breaks the next period's opening; and every credit on
  the role is labelled "Billings". Three PRODUCT DEFECTS of the builder:
  (a) *D1, the flows.* Against ENGINE_SPEC_B S15-R-03 / S15-R-07 and 03 REQ-RPT-006 under POL-004 `ERP`. The
  billing of a period is read from the engine's stored node `billed_unconditional_cum` (S10-R-08; the trace,
  subject key and grain of the S15-R-07a balances) as its delta less the BILLING and CREDIT_MEMO lines already on
  the role, ordered by the effective dates of the billing events; a difference between the documents and that
  delta stays in OTHER (nothing is plugged). No engine change, no second derivation (R-35, R-69).
  ENGINE_SPEC_B §15.2.2 says so docs first (its pseudocode names posted lines, which is true in ENGINE mode only).
  (b) *D2, the opening.* ENGINE_SPEC_B S15-R-20 governs: the opening of period t is the closing of the
  CONTRACT_BALANCE_ROLLFORWARD snapshot of t − 1 when t − 1 is locked at the run's cutoff, else the version's
  node; the lock that was read enters the run's binding. Built after FIX-D2's revision 0084 slice is on main.
  (c) *D3, the lines — option A.* The nine line codes of SCREENS_B RPT-03 stand (03 REQ-RPT-006 and the glossary
  state that set); SCREENS_B gains the table kind → line: BILLINGS = billing less credit memos; the two revenue
  lines = revenue relief, the intercompany contracting side included, net of negative revenue; RECLASSIFICATIONS
  = deposit transfer, noncash consideration, financing interest, refund liability and release, receivable contra
  and the transfers between the asset captions; BUSINESS_COMBINATIONS per S15-R-06; OTHER = the unexplained
  difference only. The frozen dataset's key, columns and registry do not change; values do. A line per kind on
  screen (S15-R-03 to the letter) is not built in 1.0.
  (d) *For the accountant (candidate AD-48; engineering defaults above):* a credit memo net in BILLINGS; negative
  revenue net in the revenue lines; the intercompany relief shown as revenue in the contracting entity's
  rollforward and in RPT-04; the presentation of a deposit transfer under RECLASSIFICATIONS. A year-1 cell for
  the platform key DISC-S10-DISCLOSURES-REPORTS-EX21-ROLLFORWARD-AND-TIMING (its summary states billings
  140,000.00; the key asserts 2027 only) is an oracle addition and goes to the accountant's package, not into
  the key now.
  (e) *Consequence for R-42 (c).* TO_ROLLFORWARD_BALANCES of the contract balance rollforward cannot refuse a
  lock until (a) and (b) are on main; lane FIX-D2's tie-outs slice keeps waiting for them.
  (f) Recorded: the demo's one credit on the role is negative revenue of background contract BG-AVM-0023 (why it
  reverses revenue is not examined — lane QA-BE); 56 of 56 report descriptions end with an internal id (catalogue
  text and one revision of `report_definition.description`; item RPT-DESC-1, lane F-RPS-REG later).

- **R-73 (2026-09-30 10:05; supervisor's own correction of R-66 (2), before anything was built on it).** The one-way end of
  the bootstrap exception stands: once a person other than the bootstrap Tenant Admin has been ACTIVE while
  holding `access.approve`, AUTO-BOOTSTRAP never matches again (R-38 (iv) addendum made live, R-66 (2) made
  permanent; 04 `setup_completed_at` remains the documented end for every other case). The refusal that R-66 (2)
  added — no suspension, removal or revocation that leaves fewer than two access approvers — is WITHDRAWN: taking
  access away from a person who has left must never wait for a second approver. Consequences: a workspace can
  fall to one access approver by an ordinary offboarding; that person's own requests then have no decider.
  The state is named to the user who causes it (a confirmation on the suspend, remove and revoke actions — lane
  WEB-QA with W-12) and shown afterwards (APR-NO-APPROVER-1, lane SECFIX-PLT); it is repaired by an operator
  command that invites a named person as Tenant Admin under a support reference, audited under the operator
  principal and notified to the workspace's administrators and Controllers (item APR-RECOVERY-1, lane OPS:
  command, tests, runbook). APR-RECOVERY-1 is in the scope of the first release.

- **R-74 (2026-09-30 10:35; F-CLO-B pre-build note for R-69 (d); completes R-69; pending the accountant and the ITGC owner as
  R-69 is).**
  (a) *Source.* `tie_outs.balances_at` (the S15-R-07a period nodes, per member contract and entity, in transaction
  currency; refuses by name what it cannot read). The functional amount of a balance is stored only at a
  version's latest period (04 T-CON-09). The role basis is therefore stated when every contract of the entity
  either has the entity's functional currency as its transaction currency or has its stored functional balance at
  the reconciled period. Otherwise the role's row is "not stated": it carries no subledger amount, names the
  contracts and the reason, is a variance of its own class that the preparer explains and the reviewer signs, and
  never auto-certifies. The platform does not translate a balance. Item ENG-FUNC-BALANCE-NODE-1 (a functional
  balance node per period) records the engine gap; not in 1.0 unless the accountant requires it.
  (b) *Roles.* Three position roles take the role basis, each with its own row: CONTRACT_LIABILITY, CONTRACT_ASSET
  and UNBILLED_RECEIVABLE (the third stored balance of S15-R-07a).
  (c) *Accounts of a role.* The rules of the published mapping in force for the entity and book, and the accounts
  that carry subledger lines posted with that role. A role with several accounts is one `totals` row with
  `account_role` and `account_codes` (04 API-S-Reconciliation and SCREENS_B §2.2 wording, docs first); no refusal
  of a mapping that uses several accounts.
  (d) *Classification.* As the note proposes: unposted batch / timing restricted to the batch's lines of the role;
  a ledger document without an eRev external id whose number is an invoice or credit memo number of the entity is
  ERP billing and raises no item; every other such document stays DIRECT_GL_ENTRY; the rest is OTHER.
  (e) *The mock ledger (lane F-CLO-A, CLO-15).* The computed trial balance of the NetSuite mock states closing
  balances in the entity's functional currency: every journal posted to the mock through the period end, the
  fixture's direct entries, and — for an entity in ERP billing mode — its invoices and credit memos as the ERP
  posts them (Dr receivable / Cr the contract liability account, net of tax, at the issue date), each a document
  whose reference is the invoice number and whose external id is null.

- **R-75 (2026-09-30 10:38; F-CLO-A's correction of the premise of R-71 (a); replaces its last sentence).** Fact from the
  lane: 04 T-INT-03 has no pair DISPATCHING → PENDING; the relay's recovery (ADP-32) re-claims a DISPATCHING message
  whose claim is older than 15 minutes and dispatches it. A cancel admitted after that age would leave one case
  open: a relay that died after the ledger accepted the chunk and before the batch was recorded — the ledger
  would keep a document of a cancelled batch and a recalculated run would export the same activity under a new
  external id.
  (a) *Rule.* `cancel_run` answers 409 `invalid-transition` while any export message of the run is DISPATCHING,
  whatever the age of its claim. The recovering relay completes the dispatch (the ledger answers Duplicate when it
  already holds the document, BR-JE-02) and the batch ends exported or failed; the run is then corrected by the
  means of that state, not by a cancel. The refusal names the export in flight and the recovery interval. While
  no worker runs, such a run cannot be cancelled — stated in the runbook; the product cannot know what the
  ledger holds without asking it.
  (b) The detective follow-up the lane offered (the recovering dispatch of a cancelled batch asks the ledger and
  raises an exception item) is not needed under (a) and is not built.
  (c) *Found on the way — PRODUCT GAP against 03 REQ-PLT-034 (P2, 1.0), item WEBHOOK-EMIT-1:*
  `events/webhooks.emit_webhook` has no caller, so none of the six documented event kinds (`run.completed`,
  `import.committed`, `period.locked`, `journal_batch.exported`, `journal_batch.acknowledged`,
  `exception.raised`) is ever delivered although endpoints can be registered. Lane F-CLO-A emits the two
  journal-batch kinds where CLO-14 writes those states; the other four are assigned when their lanes' current
  parts are merged (imports, the lock decision, the exception queue, the run).

- **R-76 (2026-09-30 10:52; QA-BE design note `docs/reviews/loop/prod/QA-BE-CTR-ASOF-KPI-1-DESIGN-NOTE.md` (sprint/w2 990733f8) on
  browser-QA finding Q-1, and its report on Q-2 / quarantine visibility).**
  (a) *CTR-ASOF-KPI-1 — accepted as designed.* PRODUCT DEFECT against 04 API-C-10 and 03 REQ-REC-021. The members

  cannot answer is refused by name, never replaced by the version column; no engine change. A contract whose

  F-CLO-B, CLO-19 / CLO-20), which needs the link.
  (c) *"Last computed".* API-S-Context gains `computed_at` (the computation that produced the version) and
  `measured_period`; SCREENS §4.1.2 binds the label to it; `known_at` keeps feeding the "Known at" banner.
  (d) *The contract read names its computation — PRODUCT DEFECTS against 04 API-S-Contract and API-R-28:*
  `open_exception_count` is the constant 0, the list filters `has_exceptions` and `modified_in_period` answer
  nothing, and the BLOCKED / NEEDS_ATTENTION half of the step rule is not built (the notes that deferred them
  name items that have landed). All four are built as 04 states them. API-S-Contract gains `computation`
  {`latest` {id, status, created_at, code}, `computed_stream_version`, `dirty_since`} (04 docs first).
  (e) *A dirty group is computed.* 05 IPL-11 governs and the interim of L5-1-Q-12 ends: an import commit defers
  the computation of the groups it dirtied (the RCP-19 chunking), and a periodic sweep defers a computation for
  any dirty group with no queued or running job, so that no path leaves a group dirty unseen until the next
  command or the close run. No "Recalculate" action is added.
  (f) *Screens* (lane WEB-QA, item W-17, after the API is on main): SCREENS §4.1.5 banner 1 (latest computation
  QUARANTINED, the IMP-75 sentence, "Open exception"), banner 2 (VOIDED), banner 4's second condition bound to

  the latest change." and "Recalculation is queued." only while a job exists; the meta row of (c); figures
