  (g) Recorded, not now: an event's `computation` member names the first computation that included it, so the
  Events tab keeps showing QUARANTINED after a repair (as 04 specifies; item EVT-COMPUTATION-LATEST-1).
  Owner of (a) to (e): lane QA-BE, in the order (a)–(c), then (d)–(e); each reported on its own.

- **R-77 (2026-09-30 10:55; independent review, by code reading, of the merged fix of SN-13 / SN-12 (main 0c33ba0d), commissioned by
  the supervisor; record `.run/sup2/reviews/secfix-act-step1-p1-review-0c33ba0d.md`; refines R-20 and R-61).** The
  review confirms that SN-13 and SN-12 are closed as reported — only the approved activation (and the migration
  capture) appends `CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET` or an activating `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED`, every refusal precedes any
  write, and the gate decision equals the engine's reading. It found that the fix validates one of the date
  relations Table 2.2-A depends on and not the others. Owner of everything below: lane SECFIX-ACT, as ONE slice
  ahead of SN-5, after its first small slice.
  (1) *Step 1 history is not rewritten by back-dating — HOLE, P1 class.* On an ACTIVE contract that a 25-5
  reassessment has moved to NOT_A_CONTRACT (significant-change flag, then a not-probable assessment), a probable
  assessment dated between the two clears the flag on replay: the book never left ACTIVE and the revenue since
  posts as catch-up, on the recorder's authority. Rule: once the stream holds a `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED`, a
  `COLLECTIBILITY_ASSESSED` or `SIGNIFICANT_CHANGE_FLAGGED` dated earlier than the latest Step 1 event of its book
  is refused (422) — in either direction. With it no assessment corrects a 25-5 transition under an ACTIVE
  header: the approved void of the flag undoes a wrong one, and a cure is CTR-STEP1-REENTRY-1, which therefore
  follows this slice directly; the void refusal's copy stops promising "a new assessment" where none works.
  (2) *A DRAFT activation reads the assessment in force — HOLE.* `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED` is dated the inception
  date, so an assessment dated after inception sorts behind it and the engine activates a book against its
  reviewed not-probable conclusion. Rule: for a DRAFT contract the checklist item STEP1_RECORD requires, per
  enabled book, that the assessment in force at the inception date is the book's latest assessment; and an
  activation is not auto-approved while the latest assessment of any enabled book is not probable.
  (3) *A criteria-met re-assessment needs a new judgement — HOLE.* Today it may cite the gate's own reviewed
  NOT_A_CONTRACT record or a collectibility record reviewed before the gate. Rule: the record is of topic
  COLLECTIBILITY and was reviewed after the gate was recorded.
  (4) *Regroup into an ACTIVE target without approval* is finding SN-6, ruled as R-22; it stays in the lane's
  order and is named here because the review reached it independently.
  (5) *Book adoption through combination.* A contract behind a gate does not join a combination group in which
  it would be computed in a book its own entity does not keep (no assessment exists there) — the guard of
  STEP1-BOOK-ADOPTION-1 in `combination`, beside the one lane QA-BE builds in `put_entity_book`. The gate
  decision and the book change are serialised: the gate reads the entity's enabled books under a lock the book
  command takes exclusively (the two lanes agree the row; the lock order of DG-KRN-DB-08 governs).
  (6) *A dropped gate is not silent.* When the engine confirmation drops the gate (an oversize group, an engine
  refusal, a book of another entity) the answer names the reason; the contract stays DRAFT as ruled.
  (7) *A reviewed record is read with its facts.* Every reader of a Step 1 record (recording, the checklist, the
  criteria-met reader) filters contract, topic and book; a record re-reviewed for another book satisfies nothing
  for the first.
  (8) The preview of a not-probable assessment shows the gate that recording would append.
  (9) An approval hook that appends a stored event submission applies the refusals of R-20 and R-61 (b) again
  before it appends; `SUBMITTABLE` no longer admits a stored PENDING_REVIEW that no path produces.
  The review's "untested" list becomes tests with each rule; the invariant witness of R-20 (d) is extended to a
  25-5 transition, a DRAFT activation with assessments not dated at inception, and two books with one moving.

- **R-78 (2026-09-30 11:07; FIX-D2 on the content of revision 0084 and its measurements of the builder identities).**
  (a) *Revision 0084 = guards and cutoff column only.* The lane reverts its two tie-outs commits on `sprint/l6`
  (history keeps them), merges main, re-points 0084 to main's head (R-68 (e)), gates and reports. The guards are
  wanted on their own: the lock race of the security review, the reconciliation generation lock (R-68 (b)) and
  the rollforward's locked opening (R-72 (b)) wait for them. "Store now" is not taken.
  (b) *The tie-outs slice* (`lock_snapshot.tie_out_results`, the gates WATERFALL_TIES_TO_JOURNAL and
  DISAGGREGATION_TIES_TO_JOURNAL, rule S15-R-18d) is parked with Alembic revision **0096** and PRD **ERR-63** for
  S15-R-18d. It lands when the identities it would refuse on can pass: the contract balance rollforward (R-72
  (a), (b)), the RPO rollforward ((c) below) and the waterfall's entity ((d) below).
  (c) *RPO rollforward — measured by FIX-D2, cause not examined:* TO_ROLLFORWARD_BALANCES of `rpo_rollforward`
  fails in the period after a contract's first period (K-01 P02: closing 99,596.71 against opening plus movements
  116,166.57, difference January's revenue 16,569.86; K-04 AVM-UK P05: difference April's revenue 9,647.75).
  Item RPT-RPO-ROLLFWD-1, lane F-RPS-REG, measured and reported before it is built, with the rollforward item.
  (d) *The waterfall's entity — engineering default, for the accountant's package.* For a contract one entity
  concludes and another performs (K-04) the waterfall counts revenue under the contracting entity and the
  journal holds it in the performing entity's books, so the tie cannot pass for either entity (AVM-US: waterfall
  0.00, journal 4,790.61; AVM-UK: 9,647.75 against 4,857.14). Rule: a report that states an entity's revenue
  states the revenue recognised in that entity's books — each obligation under its performing entity, as the
  disaggregation report already does (REQ-ENT-005) — so the waterfall's population changes, not the tie's
  definition. Owner: lane F-RPS-REG (SCREENS_B RPT-01 docs first), after R-72; candidate AD-49.
  (e) The test helper `support.close_world.acknowledged_run_for` journalises revenue that no obligation
  recognised; its correction (a balance-sheet reclassification) travels with the parked slice, and any lane whose
  new close test needs it before then takes that one hunk from `sprint/l6` 76287eb5 by asking the supervisor.

- **R-79 (2026-09-30 11:07; F-CLO-B early note on CLO-19 / CLO-20; supervisor ruling, the accounting point pending the accountant
  as candidate AD-50).**
  (a) *What RELEASE_SCHEDULES posts — the engine as built governs, the documents are corrected.* BUILD_SPEC CLO-20
  (with 04 T-ENG-02, 05 RCP-08 (a), PERF-22 and PRD §2.8) expects the step to post K-01's September revenue
  (O1, 9,764.38). The engine posts the revenue of a deterministic schedule at the computation, in every open
  period (lane decision L3-2-Q-15 of the first programme; the same for JET-07 / JET-11 and JET-09), and 250
  answer keys and the parity corpus pin that behaviour; its CLOSE_RELEASE pass posts what only a period end
  decides — loss provisions (JET-12), the dated 606-10-25-7 releases (JET-01b) and the parts with a TIME share.
  Changing when the engine posts would move every key and every report world and is not considered. So the step
  posts what the pass posts: for K-01 nothing, the 9,764.38 being shown as posted by the contract's computation;
  a loss-unit world witnesses a real release. The acceptance sentence of CLO-20 and the four document sentences
  are corrected docs first (a STALE EXPECTATION of a test not yet written); the point goes to the accountant as
  candidate AD-50 ("scheduled revenue of an open period is posted at computation, not at close").
  (b) *Order.* 05 RCP-08 rev 1.9 (D-94 (3)) governs execution: FX_REMEASUREMENT, then CLOSE_RELEASE, then
  NETTING_RECLASS, each reading the earlier passes as posted. 04 T-CLS-01 keeps its array as the display order;
  a resumed run restarts at the first step not SUCCEEDED in execution order. 04 T-CLS-01 and BS4-D-03 say so.
  (c) *The engine entry.* For 1.0 the pass is bound in `domain/close` over the engine's public stages, behind one
  function. Item ENG-COMPUTE-PASS-1 (an additive public `erev_engine.compute_pass(bundle, pass_name)` with the
  guards and identities of `compute`; no change to `compute`, the canonical input or any output) goes to the
  engine lane after its P1 item; the close lane's function then becomes a call.
  (d) *What a pass stores, for 1.0.* Sealed postings only, with the kinds and keys of 04 T-SL-01 (per group for
  the release; one per entity for FX remeasurement and one for the netting reclass, in one step transaction); no
  computation row and no contract version; a line carries contract, period, role and trace node id. Known
  limitation CLOSE-PASS-TRACE-1: a close-run line has no stored calculation trace and no replay evidence until
  the pass is a member of the canonical input (item ENG-PASS-CANONICAL-1, an engine change, not in 1.0). It is
  recorded for the accountant and the ITGC owner with the other close rulings.
  (e) *Fan-out, for 1.0.* The passes run group by group in the job's process; batch loading (RCP-16) and the
  process pool (RCP-23) stay a performance item measured at G9 (item PERF-CLOSE-FANOUT-1).
  (f) *Corrects R-76 (b).* No schedule line is stamped on a ledger line: the engine posts cumulative role deltas
  and an intent names no schedule line. A schedule line's RECOGNIZED state is read from the ledger line of the
  contract, obligation, period and REVENUE role (lane QA-BE, as R-76 (b) first half); 04 API-S-ScheduleLine says
  so; `subledger_line.schedule_line_id` stays unwritten and 04 states that.
  (g) The lock's reuse of the files the close run froze (BS4-D-04) sits in the freeze call of
  `close/commands.py`: lane F-CLO-B edits it once revision 0084 is on main.
  (h) 04's multi-entity paragraph ("409 for an active close run") against `test_start_returns_active_run` and
  SCREENS_B §1.2 (the active run is returned): the latter is built; the sentence is corrected with CLO-21.

- **R-80 (2026-09-30 11:10; lane SECFIX-ACT's proposal for R-77 (5), accepted; binds lanes SECFIX-ACT and QA-BE).** The lock is
  on the tenant's `book` rows (04 T-REF-02). *Exclusive side:* `put_entity_book` and `update_book` already take
  the `book` row of their code FOR UPDATE before they read or write `entity_book`; the guards of R-58 (b) and
  STEP1-BOOK-ADOPTION-1 read the contracts after that lock, as a fresh statement; `PATCH /books/{code}`
  re-enabling a tenant book that entity rows still keep is the same adoption and takes the same guards.
  *Shared side:* every decision that reads the enabled books to decide a gate — `step1_events` (events route, CSV
  v2 recorded events) and the combination guard — takes every `book` row of the tenant FOR SHARE in code order
  after its group and contract locks and before its first read of `entity_book`, held to the commit that appends
  the gate. *Order* (dev-guide DG-KRN-DB-08, stated docs first): combination groups, the proposal or judgement
  record, contracts, then `book` rows; a book command takes `book` first and no combination-group or contract
  row lock afterwards in its transaction, so a recompute on adoption is a job, never inline. The entity row was
  considered and rejected: the set of entities whose books a gate depends on is the group's and is known only
  under the group lock.

- **R-81 (2026-09-30 11:13; ENG-FX report on ENG-S12-DUE-DATE-1, measured and nothing built; supervisor ruling on an accounting
  measurement point, pending the independent accountant as candidate AD-51 — never recorded as approved).**
  *Measured by the lane* (engine level, 24 variants on frozen key bundles, 12 fail): under the default
  `EARLIER_OF_RECEIPT_AND_UNCONDITIONAL_DUE` (forced in IFRS15) an ERP-mode billing line's layer is dated at the
  payload `due_date` (decision D-87 L6-5-Q-14, "S10-R-06 still governs balances"), while the position counts the
  line from its S10-R-06 date — the issue date in ERP mode. At every period end between the two dates the open
  layers differ from the position by the line amount, S12-INV-03 fails and the contract is quarantined, for good:
  every later computation checks the old period end again. A second trigger of the same class needs no due date:
  a first receipt dated in an earlier period than the line it names (both billing modes). D-87 L6-5-Q-14 was
  decided on a key whose three dates lie in one period and was never carried into ENGINE_SPEC_B S12-R-04,
  POL-160 or ALG-08. Every production channel that carries payment terms (the events route, the CSV v2 invoice
  import, the billing adapter) produces the failing shape; no key, parity case or seed does.
  *Ruling — the lane's option B.* The POL-160 layer date may not leave the accounting period in which the line
  enters the position: a later date gives that period's end, an earlier date gives the S10-R-06 date, and inside
  the period nothing changes. It covers both triggers, in stage 12 `layer_date` alone. Precedent: D-92 (5) in
  S12-R-04 ("a layer cannot predate the contract liability it represents"); this adds the mirror — nor may the
  layer outlast the period end that presents the liability. Measured: 24 of 24 variants compute and the 1,648
  output hashes of the 721 corpus bundles are identical, so no key, parity or golden figure moves, there is no
  DDL and no engine version step (no output of an input that computed before changes).
  *What it costs, for the accountant (foreign-currency contracts only):* an invoice neither paid nor due by the
  end of its issue period is measured at that period end's spot rather than at the later due or receipt date;
  an advance received before its invoice is measured at the rate of the day the invoice enters the position
  rather than at the receipt date (IFRIC 22.8 reads the receipt). Alternatives the ruling does not close: C (the
  issue-date rate outside the period; a cliff at the period end) and D (a pending element converted on the due
  date; exact, and it needs rulings of its own). Also recorded for the accountant, not changed: in ERP mode
  nothing posts the difference between the ERP's credit at its invoice-date rate and a layer at another date's
  rate (JET-10c does so for credit memos).
  *Build:* docs first — ENGINE_SPEC_B S12-R-04 (the definition of D-87 L6-5-Q-14 it never received, and the
  clamp), POLICIES POL-160 note, ALG-08 §2.9.1; then `layers.py layer_date`; witnesses: the twelve failing
  variants and their controls as engine tests, one database witness through the events route on the K-07 shapes
  lane F-RPS-REG measured, fail-first on the present engine, the corpus hash comparison. Stop-gap until it is on
  main, US book only: `fx.cl_layer_date = INVOICE_ISSUE_DATE`.

- **R-82 (2026-09-30 12:10; lane SECFIX-ACT's report on slice (i) — R-61 (c) and the API half of ACT-STEP1-UI-1, merged as main
  3d7de6bf — and its design for the review-residuals slice of R-77 / R-80; refines R-61 and R-77).**
  (a) *Summary members are plain readings.* `impact_preview.summary.catch_up_total` and `criteria_met` of
  API-S-Approval read the stored preview's `after` side for every subject type, so a modification's and an estimate
  version's request also state their catch-up total; null only when the preview states none. Accepted as built (one
  rule for the approver, no per-subject branch in the reader).
  (b) *R-77 (1) as designed:* rule id REQ-POL-008 on `events.<i>.effective_date` for the assessment and for the
  flag; the flag, which acts in every book, is compared with the latest Step 1 event of any book; a
  `SIGNIFICANT_CHANGE_FLAGGED` no longer rides in a request that creates an ATTRIBUTE_CHANGE submission (the same
  closure as the assessment's, 422 API-R-30).
  (c) *A Step 1 judgement is not dated ahead of the day it is recorded — new rule.* With (b) and the refusal to void
  an assessment, an assessment recorded with a future date could be neither preceded nor removed. A
  `COLLECTIBILITY_ASSESSED` or `SIGNIFICANT_CHANGE_FLAGGED` whose effective date is later than the contracting
  entity's current date (05 TZ-03) is refused, 422 REQ-POL-008, on every channel that records one (the events
  route, the import, an adapter); a contract whose inception lies ahead is assessed today and that assessment is
  the one in force at inception. *Measured before it is built:* the lane lists every seeded or test-world Step 1
  event dated after its world's clock (the demo seed first) and reports if the seed holds one. *Not closed, for
  the known-limitations register (item CTR-STEP1-REDATE-1):* an assessment recorded on an activated contract with
  a wrong date that is not in the future cannot be re-dated; it is corrected from its date onwards by a later
  assessment, or by the approved void of a wrong flag.
  (d) *R-77 (3) as designed:* "reviewed after the gate was recorded" is T-CON-19 `reviewed_at` strictly later than
  the gate event's `created_at` (both the unit of work's clock; the event's `recorded_at` is the database's and is
  not compared); the route refuses by name a probable assessment behind the gate that cites a record of another
  topic or one reviewed before the gate.
  (e) *R-77 (6) as designed:* the 201 body of the events route gains `step1_gate {appended, reason, message}`, null
  when no gate is decided; reasons BOOK_NOT_ASSESSED, GROUP_TOO_LARGE, ENGINE_REFUSED, BOOK_NOT_CONFIRMED; a
  deferred computation carries it in the job's result and the audit detail carries it. The reason code is the
  contract the screens bind (item W-10); the message comes from the copy catalogue.
  (f) *R-77 (9) as designed:* the approval hook answers 409 `invalid-transition` with the refusal named in the
  detail, and the request stays PENDING — the approver rejects it or its preparer withdraws it; it is not voided
  by the attempt.
  (g) *R-77 (2):* the checklist item that fails because the book's latest assessment is dated after the inception
  date says so: PRD **IMP-131** (the lane names the code; IMP-105 keeps "Step 1 review not recorded." for the case
  it states).
  (h) *The questionnaire guard is an item:* MOD-QUESTIONNAIRE-GUARD-1 — PRD SM-03 DRAFT to SUBMITTED names
  "Questionnaire complete" and `modifications.submit` does not check it; a modification is not submitted with a
  proposed answer its preparer has neither confirmed nor changed (BR-MOD-01; only a stored answer counts).
  PRODUCT DEFECT, lane SECFIX-ACT, after SN-6, with a witness; lane F-CTR-WEB's wizard (CTR-27) binds the refusal.

- **R-83 (2026-09-30 12:29; lane F-CLO-WEB's mismatch report before building the cockpit's lock commands, item 1b of its package).**
  (a) *The permanent-lock request is a Controller's — PRODUCT DEFECT in the route.* `POST
  /periods/{id}/request-permanent-lock` takes `period.close` (api/v1/periods.py `periods_request_permanent_lock`;
  the domain's scope check the same), while PRD ACT-27 and SM-07 and SCREENS_B §1.1 give the action to
  `period.lock` and the route's own docstring says "a second Controller's approval executes it". As built a
  Revenue Accountant requests and one Controller approves: the permanent lock, which nothing reopens, passes
  with one Controller instead of two. The documents govern: the route and the domain check take `period.lock`
  for the entity; the requester cannot decide the request (the approval kernel's self-approval rule, unchanged);
  the step-up stays where the lock executes (the second Controller's decision), as for the ordinary lock. 04
  API-R-18's permission cell names the permission of each period command instead of the list "other commands".
  Owner: lane SECFIX-CLO (item CLO-PERMLOCK-PERM-1), with a witness in which a holder of `period.close` alone
  is refused and the routing still finds a second holder of `period.lock`. The screen renders the item for
  `period.lock`, as SCREENS_B says; until the route is on main the item's e2e step is owed.
  (b) *Pending permanent lock on the cockpit* (SCREENS_B §1.1 had no state for it): an information banner
  "Permanent lock requested by <name> on <DD MMM YYYY HH:mm UTC>." with "View request" (the request page
  decides, with its step-up); the menu item is not rendered while the request is pending. Stated docs first
  under the lane's SCREENS_B number.
  (c) *A menu item that a state, not a permission, makes unavailable is shown disabled with its reason*
  (§1.1: aria-disabled, tooltip "Permanently lock <earliest earlier period label> first."). The kit's Menu gains
  a disabled item with a reason — an additive change of the shared component by lane F-CLO-WEB, to DESIGN_SYSTEM's
  menu rules, with its own tests (not focusable as an action, announced as disabled, the reason reachable by
  keyboard). Hiding stays what SCR-PERM-03 says it is: the answer to a missing permission.
  (d) *Pending reopen:* the banner of §1.1 with the dual-approval line, "Review in Approvals", the requester's
  copy and "Withdraw request"; "Request reopen" is not offered while one is pending. The reason clause waits for
  an API member: API-S-Approval states the request's own reason code and comment (item APR-REQUEST-REASON-1,
  lane QA-BE); the banner is built without the clause and gains it when the member is on main.
  (e) *Lock period:* the step-up dialog of SCR-PERM-05 with the same-key resend and the copy of ERR-02, ERR-04
  and ERR-14, as the lane proposes. The toast's "Download evidence pack" is owed to SF-09:pack (RPS-20, unbuilt).
  (f) *The preparer's factor in the demo world.* A sign-off needs an MFA-verified session whatever the permission's
  `requires_mfa` flag (T-CLS-08 `mfa_verified_at`; the checklist sign and the reconciliation's preparation), and
  the demo world enrols only holders of a `requires_mfa` permission (PRD WLD-U-R2), so `maya` — the only holder
  of `recon.prepare` — cannot prepare a reconciliation without enrolling first, and the seeded close history
  (CLO-22) could not be written through the commands. The product rule stays; the world changes: WLD-U-R2 also
  enrols a persona whose journey signs off (J-13.10). Item DEMO-MFA-PREPARER-1, with the demo-seed items of lane
  QA-BE (B-4); until then the e2e steps that need a prepared reconciliation are owed to the seed.

- **R-84 (2026-09-30 12:47; lane SECFIX-ACT's measurement for R-82 (c) and its question on R-82 (e); amends both).**
  *Measured by the lane:* the Avenmoor demo seed holds no Step 1 event dated after its clock; the volume seed does
  (contracts originate across a 24-month horizon and each is assessed at its inception: 12 of 100 inceptions of
  the 1/100 manifest lie after today), and the legacy Contract Setup import composes its assessment at the
  contract's inception, which may lie ahead. And 04 §16.3 (b) already requires, once the stream holds a
  `CONTRACT_ACTIVATED`, an assessment dated on or after the earliest one — for a contract activated ahead of its
  inception that date is the inception itself, so under R-82 (c) as written ("not after the entity's current
  date") no assessment could be recorded at all until the inception had passed.
  (a) *R-82 (c) amended:* a `COLLECTIBILITY_ASSESSED` or `SIGNIFICANT_CHANGE_FLAGGED` is not dated after **the later
  of the contracting entity's current date (05 TZ-03) and the contract's inception date**; 422 REQ-POL-008 on
  `events.<i>.effective_date`, on every channel. The judgement "at inception" of a contract booked ahead of its
  inception stays what the documents make it; nothing else may be dated ahead. No seed, import composer or
  engine reading changes. What the rule leaves possible and is corrected going forward (CTR-STEP1-REDATE-1,
  known-limitations register): a wrong date that is not in the future.
  (b) *R-82 (e) amended:* the 201 body's member is `step1_gate {appended, reason}` — the reason code alone (the four
  literals of R-82 (e), an enumeration in 04). No `message` member and no PRD copy rows: the copy belongs to the
  screen (item W-10, lane WEB-QA, its SCREENS number) and an API client reads the code. The job result and the
  audit detail carry the same two members.
  (c) The repair of the test world `ifrs_sw01` is lane SECFIX-ACT's (main 47049877); lane F-RPS-REG, which repaired
  the same function independently (4ea1f3c0 on `sprint/l4`), takes its commit out before its slice merges.

- **R-85 (2026-09-30 12:48; lane QA-BE's question on B-2 and its addendum; refines R-76 (a) 1; no accounting figure, answer key or
  engine output moves).**
  (a) *The returns reduction is measured at the cut (the lane's option B).* R-76 (a) listed `kpis.transaction_price`
  and API-S-Obligation `current.*` as version facts that do not move with `as_of`. For an obligation with a right
  of return under POL-053 REDUCE_CONTRACT_QUANTITY that is incomplete: the expiry of the return window is
  time-driven and creates no version (ENGINE_SPEC_B S09-R-23, S09-R-26; lane decision L5-3-Q-1), and the approved
  key says what the figures are — `RET-CHK-029-S3-EX22`: transaction price 9,700.00 with expected returns
  −300.00 at 2026-02-28 and 9,800.00 with −200.00 at 2026-03-31, no event between. With s = ρ(cut) − ρ(d_v) read
  from the `revenue_target_exact:<ob>#FIXED` period nodes: per obligation `current.allocated_amount` −s,
  `remaining.allocation` and `scheduled` −Δ−s, `rpo` −Δ−s for an included obligation not satisfied at the cut;
  per contract `kpis.transaction_price` −Σs and the ratios follow; version reads stay as stored. Option A (refuse
  the read by name) would make such a contract unreadable from the end of its return window and is not taken.
  Where no oracle speaks (the `scheduled` column at the expiry checkpoint) the identity of 04 DB-17 at the cut
  governs and the report says so. Docs first in 04 rev 1.132 and SCREENS §4.1.4 (the exception to "the
  transaction price is the version's").
  (b) *`remaining.billing` moves with the cut:* by −(billed at the cut − billed at d_v), the plan staying the
  version's (S10-R-05), so that billed to date and remaining billing add up to the plan on the obligation pane.
  Left at d_v and named in the report: `current.remaining_unit_revenue_rate`, `netting_reclass`.
  (c) *Mechanism accepted:* a period's end date is read from the period node (`params.as_of`), not from the
  calendar of an entity the reader's scope may not cover.
  (d) *An obligation names its performing entity to every reader of its contract — PRODUCT DEFECT on main.* With a
  session scoped to the contracting entity, `obligations.contract_obligations` raises KeyError on a performing
  entity outside the scope (`queries.obligation_outs`; `legal_entity` is RLS-TE): `GET
  /contracts/{id}/obligations` and `GET /obligations/{id}` would answer 500 for SF-ORD-UK-2001 read by an AVM-UK
  user. Rule: who may read the contract reads its obligations whole, with the performing entity's reference
  (id, code, name) resolved under the tenant's scope for that reference alone; nothing else of the entity is
  disclosed. Item CTR-OBL-PERF-ENTITY-SCOPE-1, lane QA-BE, inside B-2 (it is in the functions B-2 changes), with
  a witness through both routes and a sweep of the other reference lookups of the contract reads for the same
  shape. Lane F-RPS-REG's SECFIX-SCOPE is told, so that the scope work does not hide what this shows.

- **R-86 (2026-09-30 12:50; lane SECFIX-IMP's report on part A, merged as main 4ac6c1d1, and its three questions; refines R-30 and R-49).**
  (a) *Part A accepted as built:* the uploader's bounds read from the uploader's grants in the upload's own
  workspace at each job; the template scope table of 04 §16.6; reads of an upload outside the reader's
  `contract.read` scope answer 404; `approval_floor` and the backstop in the IMPORT_COMMIT hook; the evidence
  registry over every foreign key to `file_object` with its architecture test. Revision 0087 was re-pointed to
  main's head 0084 at the merge.
  (b) *Q1 — the migration's and the reconciliation's source files are evidence.* `migration_batch.source_file_id`
  (the legacy database a capture was taken from: the opening balances rest on it) and
  `reconciliation.source_file_id` (the trial balance or billing extract a sign-off's snapshot hash covers) join
  EVIDENCE, conditional: the reconciliation's while the row carries a sign-off (PREPARED, REVIEWED, CERTIFIED, or
  superseded after a sign-off); the batch's while the batch is in a state in which its capture is relied on (the
  lane names the states from T-MIG-01 and reports them). Their erasure path is the approved shred of R-49 (a),
  like a committed import source — never one administrator alone.
  (c) *Q3 — an attachment a submission required is evidence of it.* `tenant_snapshot.manifest_file_id` is not
  evidence (confirmed). `file_attachment.file_object_id` is evidence, conditional, in the two cases the product
  itself makes the file a condition of the submission: the SSP study of a version that has left DRAFT and is not
  rejected or withdrawn (`ssp-study-required`), and the attachment a manual adjustment needed at or above its
  threshold once the adjustment is submitted. Every other attachment stays erasable by `file.shred` as PRV-07 (b)
  has it. The erasure path of the two is again the approved shred.
  (d) (b) and (c) are built in **part B**, in the same slice as the EVIDENCE_SHRED subject, so that no file is held
  without a path by which a privacy erasure can still be approved; until part B is on main they stay as they
  are. 04 T-PLT-29's "open" sentence is replaced then.
  (e) *Q2 — tenant-level uploads are unchanged:* `import.upload` suffices, as PRD J-01.6 (a Revenue Accountant
  uploads the legacy SKU SSP file) and the role table of PRD §5.6 have it; the control is the IMPORT_COMMIT
  approval, whose approver holds the underlying subject's approval permission (R-38 (ii)). 04 §16.6 states both
  halves in one sentence: a row that names an entity needs the template's write permission for that entity; an
  upload that names none needs `import.upload` alone. Recorded as a control-design point for the ITGC owner
  (RESUME §6): a holder of `import.upload` without the command's own write permission creates tenant-level
  master data and SSP versions through an approved import.
  (f) *Other points of the report:* the three older `file.shred` refusals (retention, already shredded, plaintext
  purpose) are audited as DENIED too, in part B (one rule for every refusal of a destructive command). The SSP
  calculator's observations file (an IMPORT_SOURCE file named in run parameters, outside the foreign-key
  registry) is item SSP-CALC-OBS-EVIDENCE-1, unassigned. Exception items of an upload not scoped by the upload's
  named entities → SECFIX-SCOPE (item SCOPE-IMPORT-EXCEPTIONS-1); `GET /files/{id}/content` of an import source
  or diff not tied to the upload's entities → SECFIX-PLT (R-48 (c)). Migration capture and integration sync run
  as SYSTEM for every entity: unchanged, tenant-level by their documents.

- **R-87 (2026-09-30 12:52; lane SECFIX-APR's four readings of R-64 and R-66 before it builds Part 3; all four confirmed as the
  lane's first reading, with the additions below).**
  (1) *R-64 (1), "any other the lane finds".* Every contract subject whose approval recomputes the group OR marks
  it dirty is bound to the contracting entities of every current member of the group: CONTRACT_ACTIVATION,
  CONTRACT_VOID, MODIFICATION, MANUAL_EVENT, ATTRIBUTE_CHANGE, POLICY_OVERRIDE, SSP_OVERRIDE, ESTIMATE_VERSION of
  a contract element, JUDGEMENT_RECORD that names a contract, and IMPORT_COMMIT of a template whose commit
  computes groups. A deferred recompute moves the group's figures on the same decision as an inline one. For
  IMPORT_COMMIT the entities are lane SECFIX-IMP's `scope.named_entity_ids` (on main since 4ac6c1d1) joined, at
  the decision, with the contracting entities of the current members of every group a named contract belongs
  to; that wiring (and `apply_floor` → `scope.approval_floor`) is lane SECFIX-APR's, being the second of the two
  to merge.
  (2) *R-66 (7), the step-deadlock refusal.* A person who is the only one able to decide a later step is refused
  approve AND reject on the earlier step, and `can_decide` is false for them until that step is active: one
  answer behind the API's one flag. A delegate deciding on that person's behalf is refused alike; a later step
  nobody can decide refuses nobody.
  (3) *R-64 (4) with (7)(c).* Left as built: a pending row of a subject type without a specification stays in
  "waiting for me" on the SQL clause (BUILD_SPEC RPS-17 counts WLD-B-02, a MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT, for `priya`). It
  ends with CLO-12, which registers that specification; from then an architecture test holds that every
  `ApprovalSubjectType` has a specification, and the fallback clause is removed (item APR-WAITING-SPECLESS-1:
  whichever of SECFIX-APR and F-CLO-A merges second).
  (4) *R-66 (2) / R-73, the one-way bootstrap exception, without DDL.* "A second access approver has existed" is
  read from rows nothing erases: `tenant_membership.activated_at` and the period of an assignment of a role that
  carries `access.approve`; a suspension inside the period is not subtracted; role permissions are read as they
  are now. *Stated edge, accepted:* the exception can open again only when `access.approve` is taken out of a
  custom role by a ROLE_CHANGE — which a second access approver decided while one existed; offboarding,
  suspension and revocation never reopen it. 04 says so in the paragraph that states the exception; it is a
  control-design point for the ITGC owner (RESUME §6).
  (5) *R-64 (7)(a):* `PATCH /judgements/{id}` is creator-only; another preparer may submit the record, and then
  both the creator and the submitter are authors for the self-approval rule (R-66 (1)).

- **R-88 (2026-09-30 12:54; three short points from lanes OPS, WEB-QA and ENG-FX).**
  (a) *CONTRACT_COMPUTE is retried when the machine failed, not when the computation answered (lane OPS's finding).*
  05 §5.6 gives the job three attempts with an exponential wait; the registration gives one, so after
  RES-53-COMPUTE-1 a deferred computation that meets a full lock table ends FAILED with no computation stored.
  Rule: a failure that is the database's or the worker's — SQLSTATE 53200, 40001, 40P01, 55P03, a lost connection,
  a worker that died — is retried up to the three attempts of 05; an outcome of the computation itself (an engine
  refusal, a quarantine, a validation problem) ends the job at the first attempt, as the modification-preview
  tests expect. 05 §5.6's row says "transient failures" docs first. Item JOB-COMPUTE-RETRY-1, lane OPS, after
  its second report; a witness per class (a 53200 on the first attempt then success; an engine refusal not
  retried).
  (b) *One `sort` parameter, several grids (lane WEB-QA's crawl finding F2).* A sort key in the URL is unrecognised
  only when no grid of the screen lists it; each grid applies the keys it lists and ignores the others (SCREENS
  SCR-URL-09 / SCR-URL-21 gain the sentence). The crawl's finding F1 (free text sent to uuid filters on the
  journal run's lines) is item W-19; its `SF-05 cockpit maya` failure (item W-15) is a STALE TEST WORLD — the
  serial project consumes the condition the row asserted — and is repaired in the e2e test alone.
  (c) *Engine version.* ENG-S12-DUE-DATE-1 (R-81), like ENG-S14R10-FX-SIGN-1 before it, turns an error into an
  output for inputs that did not compute; by DG-ENG-10 that is a minor step. `ENGINE_VERSION` stays 0.3.0 on main
  until the release cut, where ONE step 0.3.0 → 0.4.0 covers every such item; the release-candidate checklist
  carries it (with the corpus hash comparison of each item as its evidence).

- **R-89 (2026-09-30 13:01; lane WEB-QA's question 4 on item W-10; refines R-61 (f); binds the screen, leaves the API rule as it is).**
  *Fact (read by the lane, measured earlier by SECFIX-ACT as STEP1-JR-STALE-1):* the Step 1 drawer creates and
  submits the judgement record and then appends the assessment that cites it; the record's review request
  hashes the contract's head, which that assessment moves, so every review of a record the screen created is
  stale when it is decided (409, the request voided, the record REJECTED). The activation checklist needs a
  REVIEWED record per enabled book, so no contract whose Step 1 review was recorded on the screen can be
  activated through the screen.
  *Ruling — the lane's option A, in its strict form for both outcomes.* The screen records a Step 1 judgement in
  two steps: (1) "Record Step 1 review" — the criteria, rationale and evidence; judgement record created and
  submitted, no event; the tracker's Step 1 row shows the record waiting for review; (2) once the record is
  REVIEWED the workbench offers "Record assessment" (DRAFT) or "Record criteria met" (behind the gate): a small
  drawer naming the reviewed record, the books and the date — for a DRAFT contract the inception date (R-77 (2),
  R-84), behind the gate today and editable, with the API's refusals shown on the field — which appends the
  assessment citing the REVIEWED record. Then "Submit for activation" with the checklist's own answers. The same
  order for a not-probable outcome: the screen waits for the review before the assessment and its gate, which
  is stricter than PRD SM-02's guard and leaves no record to go stale. SCREENS §4.1.3, §4.1.5, §4.1.6 and
  §4.9.1 say so, docs first, under the lane's number.
  *Not decided here:* the API still admits an assessment that cites a SUBMITTED record (04 §16.3 (b)), and on
  that path the record's review goes stale as before. Item STEP1-JR-STALE-1 stays open with lane SECFIX-ACT,
  which proposes in its next pre-build note one of: the assessment requires a REVIEWED record on every channel,
  or the record's staleness basis leaves out an assessment that cites it — with the worlds each moves. A later
  improvement, not for the first release: the review's approval appends the assessment itself (one maker step;
  item STEP1-ONE-STEP-1).

- **R-90 (2026-09-30 13:01; lane SECFIX-IMP's question before part B; applies dev-guide DG-MIG-07 rev 1.34 and R-18).** A new member of E-08
  (`EVIDENCE_SHRED`, and any other lane's new approval subject) changes the rendering of the seeded
  `approvals_register` definition, and `test_revision_seed_equals_fresh_rendering` holds the literal of revision
  0048 to that rendering. Before the first release the governed convention stands (the lane's reading A): the
  0048 `REPORT_DEFINITION_SEED` literal is regenerated from `catalogue.seed_statement()`, no object added, the
  round trip and the head pin unchanged. Conditions: the literal is produced by the generator, not by hand, and
  the report names the command; two lanes that each add a member regenerate on their own branches and the
  supervisor regenerates once more at the second merge (the merge check now runs the seed test, the snapshot
  dataset's reference count and the privacy shred rules beside the architecture suite); every database that ran
  0048 before the change is rebuilt before it serves as evidence (test databases rebuild by themselves; the QA
  instance is reseeded from an empty database). From the first release tag a seeded definition changes only
  through a new revision — the release checklist carries the switch (item MIG-SEED-AFTER-1.0: the guard then
  compares 0048 plus the later revisions).

- **R-91 (2026-09-30 13:08; process ruling of the supervisor; amends the working rule of D-99 (2), "database-bound modules run in the
  lane first", in its extent, not in its principle).**
  *Measured:* with 19 lanes on one machine the four gate slots had eight to nine tickets waiting and a quarter of an
  hour per wait; a lane's whole-directory chain cost up to four hours (lane SECFIX-APR's figure), during which its
  tree could not be edited and main moved under its evidence — two lanes withdrew chains whose head the merges
  had already superseded. The whole backend suite, in eight shards with a test database each on the private
  server, takes about 90 minutes outside the slots (`full4`: 6,646 passed). The four cross-lane breaks of today
  (a caller's signature, a route inventory, two test worlds and one pinned expectation) were each found by that
  run, by the merge check or by a neighbouring lane within one to three and a half hours.
  *Rule.* Before a report a lane runs, on the head it names: (A) without a slot — lint, typecheck, the
  architecture suite, the unit directories of the packages it touched, the touched frontend tests and the API
  date guard, the OpenAPI drift check; (B) in ONE slot acquisition and ONE invocation — the database modules it
  added or changed, the `tests/domain/<area>` directory of each domain package its diff touches, the modules that
  drive its code from outside that directory (found by a grep for the changed functions, routes and problem
  codes, and listed in the report) and the `tests/pg` modules when a revision, trigger or grant changed; (C) when
  the engine changed — the release selection of answer keys, parity, the corpus hash comparison, the engine and
  properties directories; (D) a frontend lane — the whole vitest and one `make e2e` per item. Whole
  `tests/domain`, whole `tests/api`, the demo directory and engine-plus-unit chains are no longer asked of a
  lane. The supervisor's merge check runs typecheck, the architecture suite and the pinned counts on every
  merged tree; a targeted verification runs the touched modules on the merged head; and the whole backend suite
  runs on main after each batch of merges. A red that belongs to a lane is that lane's first task, as its own
  commit. *What this accepts:* a cross-lane break may stand on main for up to about two hours; main is not
  deployed from, and the release candidate is cut only from a head on which the whole suite, the e2e gate and
  the runtime gates were run and every remaining red is named with its owner. *What it does not change:* no
  skipped, deselected or loosened test; fail-first evidence for every fix; the report says exactly what was run
  and what was not.

- **R-92 (2026-09-30 13:30; lane SECFIX-APR's question on the wiring of R-87 (1): how the approval floor of R-38 (ii) reaches the routing;
  the lane's reading B).** The IMPORT_COMMIT request of an upload whose commit performs an approval keeps its own
  step; the underlying subject's first step is met by the SAME approver, who must personally hold the underlying
  approval permission for the named entities (as PRD §2.5 has it since lane SECFIX-IMP's part A); the routing
  gains only what one step cannot hold — the underlying subject's SECOND step, with its role, when that subject
  would need one. So one person holding both permissions approves an unflagged import, and a flagged one takes
  two people: the import path asks for exactly the approvers the direct path would, never fewer and never more.
  The requirement is checked at EACH decision, not only at the final one (a per-decision callback of the subject,
  which `can_decide` also asks), so that a first approver without the underlying permission cannot take the step
  and strand the request, and "waiting for me" stays exact. Reading A (every underlying step its own step and
  person) is not taken: with the default roles only a person who is both Revenue Reviewer and SSP Approver could
  then never approve alone what the direct path lets one SSP Approver approve, and PRD J-01's import approval
  would need a second person. *Consequences:* two expectations of lane SECFIX-IMP's
  `test_approval_floor.py` move (the second-step and the Controller-threshold cases: the first approval answers
  200 PENDING where it answered 403) — STALE EXPECTATION under this ruling, moved by lane SECFIX-APR and named in
  its report; on main today an import whose underlying second step applies cannot be approved at all, which this
  closes. *For the ITGC owner:* a workspace in which no person holds both `import.approve` and the underlying
  approval permission (a Revenue Reviewer and a separate SSP Approver) cannot approve a legacy SKU SSP import;
  the version is then created and approved on the direct path.

- **R-93 (2026-09-30 13:30; lane F-CTR-WEB's notes on CTR-24).**
  (a) *The draft editor opens behind a fail-closed guard.* Three event types can follow a booking on a DRAFT and
  change a member the form shows (MEMO_UPDATED; LINE_ATTRIBUTES_CHANGED through an approved attribute change;
  REGROUPED), and no read answers the draft as it stands, so a form prefilled from the booking could overwrite
  them. Until that read exists (item CTR-DRAFT-READ-1: `GET /contracts/{id}/draft` answering the replaceable body
  as the draft stands, with the ETag `replace-draft` takes; lane QA-BE), SF-03:edit is routed and opens the form
  only when every event after the latest booking is a hold applied or released; otherwise it shows a state that
  says why ("This draft was changed after it was booked; it cannot be edited here yet."). A Step 1 assessment is
  NOT on that list: a draft that carries one is not edited on the screen, because the judgement was made on the
  terms it would replace. What the API does when a draft is replaced after a reviewed Step 1 record (the record
  stays REVIEWED and the checklist passes on terms nobody judged) is item STEP1-DRAFT-REPLACE-1, measured by
  lane SECFIX-ACT before anything is built.
  (b) *History.* The chip "Imports" is not rendered until the API answers items of kind IMPORT (item
  CTR-HISTORY-IMPORT-1, lane QA-BE: one item per import and contract with the file name, the import number, the
  count of appended events and the person who committed it; today an imported event shows only as a system
  event, and not at all in the default view). "Show unchanged fields" is not rendered (the compare route answers
  changed fields only); the screen lists the fields it has labels for and says how many it did not list.
  (c) *No money arithmetic in the browser (DG-FE-08 stands).* The Delta column of the version comparison is left
  out until the compare route answers it (item CTR-COMPARE-DELTA-1, with (b)); an exact decimal subtraction in the
  client is still arithmetic the server owns.
  (d) *The line editor* is a shared component (`components/line-editor/`) that follows DS-CMP-10's inline-editing
  rules for lines a form holds in its own state; DESIGN_SYSTEM revision **1.7** states the form-held variant, docs
  first, with the dev-guide sentence under the lane's number.

- **R-94 (2026-09-30 13:30; lane FIX-D2's observations after the merge of revision 0084 and its follow-up 7f9a4282; an independent review
  of the merged change is in progress and may add to this).**
  (a) *The frozen datasets are reader-independent — PRODUCT DEFECT.* The dataset freeze reads under the decider's
  row-level scope: for WLD-K-04 a decider scoped to AVM-US gets 422 on one dataset kind where a decider of every
  entity freezes it. The lock snapshot of an entity, book and period is the same whoever decides: the freeze
  reads under the tenant's SYSTEM scope, as the coverage guard and the gates do (R-42 (d), R-64 (1)). Item
  CLO-FREEZE-SCOPE-1, lane FIX-D2, with a witness per dataset kind on K-04; it must be on main before
  entity-scoped grants can be created through the product (R-63 (e)).
  (b) *The period the lock opens.* BR-CLS-03 inside the lock decision opens the next period through the close
  module's writer and does not re-mark the groups that have an effect dated in it (05 RCP-04, SCH-06), which the
  manual and the scheduled opening do. If that is so, nothing posts the newly opened period's scheduled revenue
  until each group is touched. Item CLO-LOCK-OPEN-REDIRTY-1, lane FIX-D2: FIRST a database witness (lock a period
  whose successor is `future`; are the groups dirty, does the successor's revenue post at the next recompute?);
  if it fails, the re-marking runs after the decision commits, in its own transaction (a job the decision
  defers), so that no combination-group lock follows the closing period's state row (04 DB-07 order).
  (c) *A computation that writes no line.* Serialisation with the lock is per ledger line. A computation that
  plans the closing period and writes no line into it (an invoice under ERP billing mode) holds no row lock and
  can commit beside the decision; its version is known after the cutoff, the frozen balances do not hold it, and
  the coverage guard, which reads lines and journal runs, does not name it. Direction, to be confirmed after the
  review: the computation holds the state row of every period it plans FOR SHARE from planning to commit
  (`posting_period`), so that the decision waits for it and a late one plans the next period — the rule of the
  line guard, moved to where the period is chosen. The lane measures first: where `posting_period` sits in the
  lock order, which computations write period nodes without a line, and what a guard on versions known after the
  cutoff would and would not catch. Nothing is built before that note.
  (d) `period_lock.cutoff_known_at` is a read member of the period-lock API (04 API-S for `GET
  /periods/{id}/locks`), for the close history screen (CLO-24). With (a).
  (e) For the runbook (lane OPS): a lock decision that waited and was refused by S15-R-18c, or answered 409
  lock-conflict, is decided again; no gate on imports or posting jobs in progress is built. For lane F-SNP: the
  sandbox replay's lock states its cutoff as the replay's clock and runs no coverage guard (R-42 (e)).
  (f) The order of `ledger_chain_head` and `numbering_series` (dev-guide DG-KRN-DB-08): an auto-approved
  submission draws its number before its hook takes the ledger head. Item LOCK-ORDER-SERIES-HEAD-1: examined with
  the review's findings; no change now.

- **R-95 (2026-09-30 14:08; lane QA-BE's finding from the sweep of R-85 (d), measured on WLD-K-04; PRODUCT DEFECT on main; refines 05 RCP-18
  and applies the principle of R-64 (1) to the computation).**
  *Measured:* a Revenue Accountant scoped to AVM-UK records an invoice on SF-ORD-UK-2001 (contracted by AVM-UK, one
  obligation performed by AVM-US): 201, and the computation is QUARANTINED — "the performing entity has no calendar
  (rule CV-12)", a BLOCKING exception item, nothing posted. A user of every entity records the next invoice and the
  computation succeeds. Cause (read by the lane): the synchronous computation of a fact-capture command runs in
  the caller's session, bundle assembly reads the entity-owned tables under the caller's scope, and the
  performing entity is absent from the bundle; the same unit of work would also be refused the performing
  entity's ledger and schedule rows. The compute job runs under the tenant's scope and does not have the defect.
  *Rule.* The computation of a combination group is reader-independent: its bundle is read and its outputs —
  versions, schedule and ledger lines, computation and exception rows — are written under the tenant's scope by
  SYSTEM on behalf of the principal whose command triggered it, in every command that computes in its own
  transaction (booking, events, holds, combination, activation, modifications, the import commits) exactly as
  in the job. What stays the caller's: the authorisation of the command (the contract's contracting entity, and
  for approvals the entities of R-64 (1) / R-87 (1)), the command's own writes (the event, the audit event) and
  every read the caller makes afterwards. A caller is not refused a command because the group reaches an entity
  outside their scope (the lane's option 3 is not taken), and the synchronous path of RCP-18 stays (option 2 is
  the mechanism only where R-76 (e) defers anyway). 05 RCP / TXN states where the context changes, docs first.
  *Owner and order:* item CTR-COMPUTE-CALLER-SCOPE-1, lane QA-BE, directly after its B-2 report. Witnesses on
  K-04 with a user scoped to AVM-UK, per command family: the same status, versions and output hashes as for a
  user of every entity; fail-first with the quarantine measured above. It must be on main before entity-scoped
  grants can be created through the product (R-63 (e)), with R-64 (1) and CLO-FREEZE-SCOPE-1 (R-94 (a)); lane
  F-RPS-REG orders its SECFIX-SCOPE merge accordingly.

- **R-96 (2026-09-30 14:08; lane SECFIX-APR, on the tree that holds both its R-41 (7) and lane SECFIX-IMP's part A; refines R-92).**
  *Measured:* every legacy Contract Setup import with a variable-consideration row is refused at approval ("…
  approves its variable consideration estimates, which needs a second approval by a Controller"): ESTIMATE_VERSION
  gained a second step (profit-and-loss impact of USD 50,000 or more) and the import states that subject with
  its flags not evaluated, which the floor reads as "the second step applies". *Ruling — the lane's way (1), with
  its condition stated.* An estimate version that the commit approves on a contract the SAME commit books, before
  it activates it, recognises nothing: its profit-and-loss impact is nil and its weight reaches the approval
  through the activation's own flags, which the dry run evaluates (R-66 (4)). For exactly that case the import
  states ESTIMATE_VERSION as evaluated with no flag. An estimate version a commit would approve on a contract that
  existed before the commit is not that case: it is evaluated through the estimate's own routing facts, or stays
  unevaluated and takes the second step (fail closed). One expectation of lane SECFIX-IMP's test moves with it
  (STALE EXPECTATION under this ruling), and a witness holds the condition (a setup import naming an existing
  ACTIVE contract does not pass on one approver by this rule). The real second steps — a flagged SSP version, an
  activation at the Controller threshold — are routed as R-92 has them.

- **R-97 (2026-09-30 14:21; independent review, by code reading, of revision 0084 and its follow-up as merged (main 7f9a4282), commissioned by
  the supervisor; record `.run/sup2/reviews/fix-d2-lock-0084-review-7f9a4282.md`; refines R-31, R-40, R-94).** The
  review confirms rules 1 to 4 for subledger and journal lines of the lock's own entity, book and period: no
  interleaving found that lands such a line in a locked period, hides it from the freeze or leaves it beyond the
  cutoff unrefused; single writers of both tables; the guard acts on the re-read row after its wait; no cycle
  through the state row (one during tenant setup apart). Its findings are ruled as follows.
  (1) *A seal that hashes what the guard then rewrites — DEFECT of ledger integrity, first.* `subledger.post` reads
  the period's state without a lock, sets `is_post_reopen` in its rows and seals their hash; the DB-07 guard
  stores `is_post_reopen` from its own locked read. When the state changes between the two (start-close or a
  reopen approval in flight on a reopened period — a window the guard's wait now makes certain), the sealed
  hash and the stored row differ and `verify_ledger_chain` fails at that sequence for good. Item
  LED-SEAL-REOPEN-RACE-1, lane FIX-D2, before anything else: the seal is computed over the value that is stored
  (the state row read FOR SHARE where the flag is set, or the inserted value hashed), with a witness through
  `post()` against start-close and against a reopen approval, fail-first on main.
  (2) *DELTA posting mode — conditional HOLE.* The guard keys on a line's own book; under a DELTA run the primary
  book's completeness covers the LEGACY book's lines, the coverage guard counts the primary book only, and a
  LEGACY period takes no run and stays postable: a LEGACY line of the period can be in flight at, or arrive
  after, the primary book's lock and is covered by no run. Item CLO-LOCK-LEGACY-1, lane FIX-D2: FIRST the
  measurement the review could not make (can a computation write a LEGACY line for the period without a primary
  line there; what the product intends for LEGACY period states), then: under DELTA the primary decision takes
  the LEGACY state row of the period as well and the LEGACY guard reads the primary's state.
  (3) *The computation pins its periods — R-94 (c) decided.* A guard on "versions known after the cutoff" would
  not name a line-less computation (a version's `known_at` is its latest event's recorded time, not the
  computation's). And a posting takes the state rows of its second book after the first book's ledger head, so
  a decision on that book makes it wait while it holds the tenant-wide head of the first. One rule for both: a
  computation takes FOR SHARE, before its first ledger write and in one fixed order, the state row of every
  entity, book and period it plans to write into or to measure at (the rows `posting_period` chooses), and holds
  them to its commit; the line guards stay as the enforcing layer for any other writer. 04 DB-07 states the
  order for several books. Lane FIX-D2, with the note R-94 (c) asked for reduced to: where the pin sits in the
  lock order and what it costs on a group with many periods.
  (4) *A checklist read beside the decision overwrites the certified checklist.* `refresh_checklist` /
  `evaluate_gates` read the state without a lock, compute, and store after the decision committed: FAILED over
  the certified PASSED of a closed period, with an audit event (the lock's own certification stays right). They
  pin the state row FOR SHARE before they store and store nothing for a period that is no longer in the state
  they read. Item CLO-CHECKLIST-REFRESH-RACE-1, lane FIX-D2.
  (5) *On the posting path a wait is not an engine failure.* `compute_group` catches every exception, rolls back
  its savepoint and stores a FAILED computation with a BLOCKING ENGINE_INVARIANT_VIOLATION exception item
  without a period (it then counts against every period of the entity) and a failed CTL-012 execution — also
  for the guard's refusal (EREV-LED-003), a lock timeout (55P03) and a deadlock (40P01); the group stays dirty
  and nothing retries. Those three, with 53200 and 40001, propagate: a command answers 409 lock-conflict, the
  job fails its attempt and is retried (R-88 (a)); a refusal by the guard is re-planned by the retry into the
  period that is open. Item COMPUTE-TRANSIENT-NOT-FAILURE-1, lane QA-BE, in the slice of R-95 (the same unit of
  work); lane OPS's retry predicate (JOB-COMPUTE-RETRY-1) follows it.
  (6) *"Decide again" must be able to.* A 409 lock-conflict is stored as the first response of its
  Idempotency-Key and replayed to the retry the rule prescribes. The idempotency store does not keep a
  lock-conflict (item IDEM-LOCK-CONFLICT-1, lane OPS, with the mapping of a statement timeout, SQLSTATE 57014, to
  a named problem instead of a 500, and SCH-05's tick that one 55P03 ends for every remaining period and

  connections and the tenant-row lock of setup completion after a decision: noted for CLO-FREEZE-SCOPE-1, which
  adds reads of that kind — bounded, and a pool timeout is a named problem. A test file a docstring names and

- **R-98 (2026-09-30 14:25; independent review, by code reading, of SECFIX-IMP part A as merged (main 4ac6c1d1), commissioned by the
  supervisor; record `.run/sup2/reviews/secfix-imp-part-a-review-4ac6c1d1.md`; refines R-29, R-30, R-38 (ii),
  R-86, R-92, R-95).** Sound by reading: the uploader's bounds from their grants in the upload's own workspace at
  every stage; the narrowed context; one copy for an unknown and an out-of-scope entity; the five reads; no
  auto-approval of an approval-performing upload; the floor failing closed; the evidence registry and the audited
  refusal; revision 0087. Its findings are ruled as follows; owner lane SECFIX-IMP as ONE slice ahead of part B
  unless another owner is named.
  (1) *An import needs its source.* A source is held from SUBMITTED only, and neither submit nor commit reads
  whether the file was shredded: one administrator shreds the source of a VALIDATED upload, the uploader submits,
  the import is approved and committed without its original file. Submit and commit take the file row's lock and
  refuse a shredded source by name; the shred and the submit are serialised on that row.
  (2) *A reference that does not resolve is not "names no entity".* Keys are looked up as exact text (no
  stripping that the templates' own rules do not do); for a template that is not tenant-level the database scope
  is narrowed to the write-permission scope, never to the union of the uploader's roles; a row of such a template
  that names a reference which does not resolve stores NULL (not resolved: scope `*` to read or approve), not the
  empty set. Closes a write outside scope (a contract whose external id carries white space, an uploader who
  holds any role over its entity) and the read of an unresolved upload by every `contract.read` holder.
  (3) *The import's computations are reader-independent — R-95 applies.* The commit's inline recompute and the dry
  run's computations run inside the uploader's narrowed scope: a combination group with members of two entities
  is recomputed and persisted from the members the uploader sees, and the dry-run figures and modification
  thresholds come from the same partial bundle. Wrong accounting, reachable where a principal is entity-scoped
  (through the product today: an API client). The fix is lane QA-BE's slice of R-95 (the computation of a group
  runs under the tenant's scope wherever `compute_group` is called, the import commit and its dry run included),
  which therefore comes before everything else in that lane; lane SECFIX-IMP adds the witnesses on the import
  side (a legacy progress and a legacy modification file for one member of a two-entity group, by an uploader
  scoped to that member's entity: outputs equal to an unscoped upload's) when that slice is on main. What an
  uploader is shown stays inside their scope; what the engine reads does not.
  (4) *A floor is evaluated only on a computation that succeeded.* A legacy modification whose dry-run computation
  ended QUARANTINED or FAILED records "MODIFICATION, no flags, evaluated"; it records unevaluated (fail closed).
  (5) *Legacy progress:* a repeated key of another entity's contract fails the whole job and answers differently
  from an unknown contract; the rows of a refused lead row are left out of the fail-closed check. (6) *SSP
  values:* the scope findings run before the declaration rule, which otherwise quotes the stored entries of a
  book outside scope. (8) Submit and cancel answer 404, as the reads do, for an upload outside the caller's
  scope. (10) *Quarantine mode commits a contract whole or not at all:* a refused row refuses the other rows of
  its contract.
  (7) *An import names only a source its creator may read* — `POST /imports` took any IMPORT_SOURCE file id of the
  workspace. Found by the review and, independently, by lane SECFIX-PLT's family sweep, which builds it (the
  file-access rule in `upload._file`); lane SECFIX-IMP is told.
  (9) *The second step and attribution:* R-92 routes the second step; with it every approver who takes a step
  holds what the step needs, so the attribution of the commit's acts to the last approver stands.
  *Gaps, each placed:* the floor reads the subject's specification only — a workspace's published rule that adds a
  step to the underlying subject must reach the import as well (lane SECFIX-APR, with R-92: the underlying
  subject is evaluated through the routing, rules included); the FX rate set version an `fx_rates` import opens
  has SYSTEM as its preparer, so its uploader is not excluded from deciding it (lane SECFIX-APR, the authorship
  family of R-66 (1)); a floor refusal leaves no audit event — a DENIED event in its own transaction, as the shred
  refusal has (lane SECFIX-APR, with the per-decision callback); an API client's upload is held to the narrower
  of the client's stored scopes and the token's (lane SECFIX-IMP); the residual oracles the review lists (an
  external id taken elsewhere; the duplicate refusal naming an upload the caller may not read) are stated in 04
  as known properties; the snapshot cut that keeps a partly cut entity set goes to lane F-SNP.
  *The file of an import* (lane SECFIX-PLT's R-86 (f) as built, amended): an import's source and diff are read by
  the upload's own uploader, by a holder of `contract.read` for every named entity and by a holder of
  `import.approve` for every named entity — not by `import.upload` alone.
  The review's list of rule branches without a test becomes tests with each item.
