- **R-100 (2026-09-30 14:35; lane F-CLO-WEB's mismatch note on the "Request reopen" drawer, item 1b).** PRD J-14.1 and SCREENS_B §1.1 have
  the requester of a reopen (a Revenue Reviewer or a Controller, `period.reopen_request`) record the
  estimate-versus-error judgement and attach the job-cost report in the drawer. By the default roles neither
  holds `judgement.create`, and since lane SECFIX-PLT's file access rules an attachment to an approval request
  needs one of eleven subject write permissions, none of which they hold.
  (a) *The judgement: the role table governs, the journey is corrected.* Creating a judgement record stays with
  `judgement.create` (a Revenue Accountant) and its review with `judgement.review`: the separation is the
  control. J-14.1 reads: the Revenue Accountant records the ESTIMATE_VS_ERROR judgement with the job-cost report
  attached to the record; the requester's reopen request names it in its comment; the two approvers of the
  reopen decide on both. The drawer offers the judgement fields to a holder of `judgement.create` only, as
  built. PRD and SCREENS_B say so under the lane's numbers, docs first.
  (b) *A requester attaches to their own pending request.* Whoever submitted an approval request may attach
  evidence to it while it is PENDING, whatever other permission they hold — the permission to make the request
  is what brought them there. Item ATT-REQUESTER-1, lane SECFIX-PLT (its file-access rule for the subject
  `approval_request`), with a witness that another person without a subject write permission is still refused.
  When it is on main the drawer shows "Attachments (optional)" to every requester and the list of eleven
  permission codes mirrored in the client goes; until then the drawer stays as built.
  (c) DESIGN_SYSTEM revision 1.8 (lane F-CLO-WEB) states the disabled menu item of R-83 (c) in DS-CMP-28: an item
  that a state makes unavailable is disabled — aria-disabled, in the arrow-key order, described by a tooltip with
  its reason, never activated; a missing permission hides the item.

- **R-101 (2026-09-30 14:56; two defects measured by lanes FIX-D2 and F-CTR-WEB).**
  (a) *CLO-LOCK-OPEN-REDIRTY-1 — PRODUCT DEFECT of the close path, witnessed.* On WLD-K-01 through the public path:
  September locked while October is `future`; the decision opens October (BR-CLS-03) and the group stays clean,
  the selection of dirty groups finds nothing, and October's scheduled revenue (−10,089.86) is not posted; with
  October opened through `POST /periods/{id}/open` instead the group is marked and the recompute posts it. The
  close module's opening never calls the re-marking of SCH-06. It bites whenever a period is locked before its
  successor was opened by the schedule or by hand; the successor's NO_DIRTY_GROUPS gate passes meanwhile.
  *Ruling — the lane's proposal (a):* the decision defers, in its own transaction, a job of a new kind
  `PERIOD_OPEN_REDIRTY` for the opened period state (queue `close`); the handler re-marks the groups with an
  effect dated in that period as SYSTEM in its own transaction — no period state row held, so no
  combination-group lock follows a state row (04 DB-07) — and is idempotent. The window between the commit and
  the job is accepted and visible: a failed or waiting job of that kind is a job blocker of the opened period.
  Marking before the gates and deferring the opening itself are not taken. Alembic revision **0098** (the E-14
  value); documents under the lane's index 49 (04 E-14 and the DB-07 / T-REF-07 note, 05 SCH-06 as a third
  opening path, dev-guide, SCREENS_B SB-R-06 for the job label). The witness becomes the regression test.
  (b) *ENG-S10-FUTURE-INCEPTION-1 — an engine crash reachable from the draft form.* A contract booked with an
  inception after the entity's last open period (inception 1 October while the periods are open through
  September) answers 201 and its provisional computation fails with IndexError in stage 10
  (`s10_billing_balances/reclass.py` `_version_period`: `measured[0]` of an empty set); the draft has no version,
  later events record QUARANTINED, and the group stays dirty against the close gate of its entity. Booking next
  month's contract is ordinary work, and rulings R-84 and the volume seed assume it. *Rule:* a contract none of
  whose periods lies inside the evaluation horizon (CV-13) computes — a version that recognises and presents
  nothing yet, with its allocation and schedule as the rest of the engine gives them — and never raises. Lane
  ENG-FX: FIRST the measurement (which shapes fail: inception in a `future` period; every line beyond the
  horizon; a combination group with one such member; both billing modes) and what ENGINE_SPEC_B S10 says for a
  version without a measured period; then the fix with engine witnesses, a database witness through
  `POST /contracts`, and the corpus hash comparison (no output of an input that computed before may move). It
  joins the engine version step of R-88 (c). Ahead of the lane's SECFIX-CFG report if that is not already in
  its final gate run.

- **R-102 (2026-09-30 15:04; lane SECFIX-ACT's three pre-build notes — nothing built; lane WEB-QA's question on item W-21; lane SECFIX-IMP's two
  facts on R-96).**
  (a) *CTR-STEP1-REENTRY-1, the criteria-met path of a book that is NOT_A_CONTRACT under an ACTIVE header — the
  lane's design.* The same `submit-activation` admits an ACTIVE header; subject CONTRACT_ACTIVATION with the
  permission, flags, thresholds and second step of any activation, never auto-approved (R-61 (c)). The books that
  move are read by one reader for the checklist, the request content and the hook: an enabled book whose latest
  assessment is probable and rests on a COLLECTIBILITY record reviewed after the event that put the book out
  was recorded (R-77 (3) generalised), and which the request's dry run confirms as NOT_A_CONTRACT on the stream
  as it stands and not after the pending event. The approval appends CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET per moving book with
  the request id, dated the re-assessment's date; no CONTRACT_ACTIVATED; the header does not move; the recompute
  posts the catch-up (POL-013). For an ACTIVE header the checklist item STEP1_RECORD reads "at least one book
  moves". *Q1, an accounting point pending the accountant (candidate AD-56):* in the first release the path
  serves a book the engine put out as NOT_PROBABLE only; a book out for another reason (no commercial substance)
  is refused by name and stays a known limitation. PRD **IMP-133** for that refusal. *Q2:* ACTIVE headers only —
  not COMPLETED, not TERMINATED.
  (b) *STEP1-JR-STALE-1 — the lane's option (i): an assessment cites a REVIEWED judgement record, on every
  channel.* 04 §16.3 (b) "SUBMITTED or REVIEWED" becomes "REVIEWED" (422 REQ-POL-008 on
  `events.<i>.payload.judgement_record_id`); PRD SM-02's guard of DRAFT to NOT_A_CONTRACT reads "reviewed". The
  record's staleness basis stays whole (option (ii) would let a review be decided on a stream the reviewer did
  not see). Measured by the lane: four expectations of one test module move (STALE EXPECTATION under this
  ruling); no world, seed or importer cites a SUBMITTED record; the screen already takes review first (R-89).
  *For the accountant (candidate AD-56, with (a)):* the not-a-contract gate and a 606-10-25-5 reassessment then
  wait for the judgement's review; an ACTIVE book goes on recognising until the reviewed not-probable assessment
  is recorded, which may be dated back to the flag's date — an open period is corrected by the recompute, a
  period locked in between is not.
  (c) *STEP1-DRAFT-REPLACE-1 — a Step 1 judgement is made on the booking it judges; HOLE, measured.* After
  `replace-draft` (another customer, another price) the reviewed record and its assessment still satisfy the
  checklist, and a draft written by an API client under a published AUTO_APPROVAL rule is then activated with no
  person deciding anything after the replacement; behind a withheld gate the earlier book's not-probable
  assessment still counts. *Rule, the lane's proposal:* (1) the SYSTEM append that voids the replaced
  CONTRACT_BOOKED also voids every standing COLLECTIBILITY_ASSESSED of the draft (a DRAFT header has no
  activation event, so no status is replayed); (2) at recording, on every channel, the cited record was
  reviewed at or after the time the contract's latest standing CONTRACT_BOOKED was recorded (422 REQ-POL-008,
  "Use a judgement record reviewed after the draft was last replaced."). Refusing `replace-draft` once an
  assessment stands is not taken (the draft could never be corrected; the legacy file that adds lines and the
  integration's re-book would fail). With it the screen's draft editor may open on a draft that carries an
  assessment, saying that saving asks for a new Step 1 review (lane F-CTR-WEB, when this is on main).
  (d) The three are ONE docs-first slice of lane SECFIX-ACT after its residuals merge, in the order (c), (b), (a);
  numbers of register index 59.
  (e) *Item W-21 — the lane's option (b):* in the browser every answered problem that the API keeps for the key
  ends the intent (any problem below 500 except 401, 403, 412, 428, 429 and 409 `idempotency-in-progress` — the
  mirror of DG-KRN-IDEM-03): a kept problem means the command's transaction rolled back, so a new key cannot run
  anything twice, and the key keeps its purpose for a lost response and a 5xx. dev-guide DG-FE-05 says so under
  a number of the lane's; the record SPEC-Q-197 is left as history.
  (f) *R-96 on the import path (lane SECFIX-IMP's facts):* a legacy Contract Setup row that names a contract
  which is not DRAFT is refused at validation (IMP-16), so the only contract that exists before the commit and
  that a setup upload may touch is a DRAFT it replaces; and with `activate_on_approval = false` the commit books
  drafts and activates none while it still approves their estimate versions. Both are the nil-impact case of
  R-96: evaluated, no flag. The witness R-96 asked for is the validation refusal itself (a setup upload naming
  an ACTIVE contract never reaches an approval); the `drafts_id` expectation of lane SECFIX-IMP's test moves
  with the other (STALE EXPECTATION under R-96).

- **R-103 (2026-09-30 15:59; lane F-CLO-B's reports on CLO-17 and CLO-19, merged at 7faf6389; lane QA-BE's pre-build line for R-95; lane
  FIX-D2's question on F7 (c); lane SECFIX-PLT's reading of the two reds of whole-suite run full6).**
  (a) *CLO-17 and CLO-19 — the lane's judgements confirmed* (one measure on both sides: the closing balance at the
  period end in the functional currency, balance sheet accounts cumulative, revenue and expense accounts fiscal
  year to date; difference = GL − subledger at tolerance zero; the classes UNPOSTED_BATCH, TIMING, DIRECT_GL_ENTRY,
  OTHER; a source written once and refused once a subledger line is later than `as_of_known_at`; signing only
  with a trial balance attached; CTL-026 PASS or NOT_APPLICABLE; the children of RECOMPUTE_DIRTY compute at their
  own record time; a queued child is taken over at the second poll; a waived quarantine whose streams have not
  moved is not computed again and stays dirty; only the newest job of a run writes to it; the cancel rules; 422
  / 404 / 409 for an entity outside the scope, an id outside it and a period that is not open; the run carries
  its job; LOCK is never executed by the job and is marked inside the lock decision's transaction).
  *One correction (CLO-19 judgement 2):* INTERFACE_COMPLETENESS counts the imports and sync runs that name the
  run's entity (the named entities of revision 0087) or name none — a figure an entity-scoped reader reads is
  not drawn from another entity's data. It records and never stops a run. In CLO-20's slice, docs first.
  *Item CLO-BLK08-NEWEST-1 (lane F-CLO-B, in CLO-20's slice):* BLK-08 `blockers.jobs_failed` counts the newest job
  of a close run or journal run, so a run resumed to success stops showing a failed job.
  *Release gate:* no release candidate is cut from a tree that carries CLO-19 without CLO-20 — the cockpit's
  "Run close" command would end FAILED at the first unbuilt step and leave the blocking item CLOSE_RUN_FAILED.
  *Known limitations recorded:* REC-AUTO-GL-1 (a zero-variance subledger-to-GL reconciliation is prepared and
  reviewed, never auto-certified, in 1.0); the account scope of R-69 (c) until the role basis of R-74; a pull in
  two currencies is refused; 04 §15.4 VC_REASSESSMENT_MISSING and ROYALTY_ACCRUAL_MISSING have no producer until
  CLO-18 / ENG-S10-GATE-FACTS-1 (unassigned). *JE-CSV-FUNCTIONAL-1 is a blocker for an entity with a
  foreign-currency contract:* the lane's reading is that a functional-only line a GL booked as 0.00 leaves a
  permanent OTHER variance on both accounts of the entry, so such an entity can never reconcile variance-free.
  (b) *The mechanism of R-95 (item CTR-COMPUTE-CALLER-SCOPE-1) — the lane's design accepted:* the context is
  switched inside the command's transaction by one kernel manager (`UnitOfWork.as_system()`), entered before the
  computation's savepoint and restored at exit; `bundles.build` reads, and `persist`, `_refused`, `compute_group`
  and `recompute` write, under the tenant's scope as SYSTEM on behalf of the caller; the rows are stamped as the
  job's. *Conditions:* (1) an allow-list of the manager's call sites held by an architecture test; (2) nothing in
  the computation's call tree reads the principal's permissions, roles or scope — SYSTEM unlocks no branch; (3)
  the widening is transaction-local: no pooled connection carries it after a commit, a rollback or an exception,
  and a statement of the command after the manager's exit is refused the other entity's row; (4)
  COMPUTE-TRANSIENT-NOT-FAILURE-1 by partition with the default "propagate": only an engine error or a
  validation problem — a reason a person can act on — is stored as QUARANTINED or REFUSED; every database error
  (lock, deadlock, serialisation, timeout, resources, connection, unknown) rolls the savepoint back and
  propagates; (5) *item CTR-PREVIEW-GROUP-SCOPE-1:* a dry run's bundle is read wide, because the stored preview is
  one document whoever prepared it (R-64 (1)); a preview route therefore answers the named 403 of R-64 (6) to a
  caller who does not cover every entity the subject is bound to (R-87 (1)). Lane QA-BE, after lane SECFIX-APR's
  Parts 1 to 3 are on main; with R-64 (1), CLO-FREEZE-SCOPE-1 and the computation itself it must be on main
  before entity-scoped grants can be created through the product (R-63 (e)).
  (c) *A connection pool that gives no connection in time answers 503 with Retry-After*, not 409: it is the
  server's state, not a conflict with another command; a 5xx is never kept for the idempotency key. Lane OPS,
  with IDEM-LOCK-CONFLICT-1 and the 57014 mapping; PRD ERR-64 if a copy row is needed. Lane FIX-D2 builds the one
  SYSTEM reader of the freeze phase (F7 (c) (i)) with F8 and names the two places where the timeout can surface.
  (d) *The two new reds of full6 are STALE TEST WORLDS* (lane SECFIX-PLT's reading, confirmed): the 0067
  downgrade-guard test walks down from a head that earlier tests populated, and DG-MIG-06 lets an enum-value
  downgrade fail when a row uses the value (security_event is append-only) — it starts from a fresh head; the
  frozen lock dataset stays readable to a holder of report.run for the lock's entity, which the tenant admin of
  that world is not (04 T-PLT-29) — the control is read by the Controller and the admin's 404 is asserted
  beside it. No assertion is dropped.

- **R-104 (2026-09-30 16:08; the supervisor's browser pass as `priya` on the QA instance, main e16ce256 reseeded 14:47; findings Q-38 to
  Q-45 of `.run/sup2/qa/findings.md`).**
  (a) *APR-VIEW-1 — an approver reads what they approve; a release blocker.* Measured: the request view of a
  JUDGEMENT_RECORD (APR-000128) shows "No impact on revenue or balances." and the decision form — no topic,
  conclusion, rationale, questionnaire, contract or evidence, and no link (`subject.href` null, `impact_preview`
  null; the product has no judgement record screen); the same holds for JOURNAL_RUN (finding Q-26) and for about
  twenty of the thirty subject types, which have neither a stored preview nor a link path. SCREENS §15.4 asks for
  the link "Open <subject label>" (region 1), the justification (region 3) and a body per subject type (region
  5); built is the generic diff of the stored preview only (BUILD_SPEC WEB-15: "for the subject types built so
  far" — no later item added a body). The decision submits `subject_content_sha256`: a person signs a hash of
  content the screen never showed.
  *Rule.* For every `ApprovalSubjectType` the request read answers, under the reader-independence of R-64 (1):
  (1) `subject.href` wherever the subject, or the record it changes, has a screen; (2) the subject's content in
  display form — ordered rows of label, value and kind (text, money, date, reference with its own link), names
  resolved at the read and never hashed, no surrogate id as a value; what the rows say is what `content_sha256`
  covers, and a test holds the two together per subject type; (3) the request's own reason and comment (item
  APR-REQUEST-REASON-1). An architecture test holds that no subject type is without (2). The screen renders
  regions 1, 3 and 5 from these members; bespoke bodies beyond that are polish.
  *Owners:* the API members — item APR-SUBJECT-CONTENT-1, lane API-GAPS with its G-3, after lane SECFIX-APR's
  Parts 1 to 3 are on main (pre-build note first: per subject type what §15.4 asks, what the API gives today,
  what is added); the screen — item W-22, lane WEB-QA, which takes over W-8.
  (b) *A preview's figures name their dates.* Measured on APR-000121 (BG-AVM-0020, 146,000.00 at 400.00 a day
  from 1 Feb 2026): "Revenue by period" lists the first six periods (72,400.00) without saying so; "RPO
  146,000.00 → 145,600.00" is the dry-run version's RPO at the event's effective date; the journal lines total
  96,800.00, the catch-up February to September. Each is as documented (04 API-S-ImpactSummary: six periods from
  the effective period; `rpo_date`), and an approver cannot reconcile them. *Rule:* the stored preview document
  and `impact_preview.summary` carry `rpo_date` and the first and last period the journal lines post into; the
  screen captions the three figures with them ("first six periods from <period>", "RPO at <date>", "posted on
  approval, <period> to <period>"). API with APR-SUBJECT-CONTENT-1; screen with W-22. Existing stored previews
  are not rewritten (no release yet).
  (c) *Record search is unbuilt and unassigned (BUILD_SPEC CTR-28):* the command palette finds pages and commands
  only; `GET /search` does not exist. Backend (API-R-55, permission-filtered, entity-scoped) — lane API-GAPS after
  G-2; palette and results screen — lane F-CTR-WEB after CTR-25.
  (d) *Q-42:* 379 unread notifications for the reviewer of a freshly seeded demo; the seed marks as read what is
  not the open work its journeys name. Lane QA-BE, item B-4 (demo seed).
  (e) *Q-45, a question to lane ENG-FX (no ruling):* on SF-ORD-10001 the ratable line of the first period is dated
  the inception date (01 Jan 2026) and every later period's line its period end; which rule dates it, and
  whether the date selects an FX rate.
  (f) *Known and in work, confirmed in the browser:* the contract figures mix dates (Recognized 35,077.81 at the
  version's date beside a contract liability of 29,944.11 at the ledger's) — item CTR-ASOF-KPI-1 (lane QA-BE B-2,

  session's); the workbench tabs of K-01 render with no failed request.

- **R-105 (2026-09-30 16:26; erratum to R-103 (b) (4), on lane QA-BE's correction of what the code does and its question on the class the
  ruling did not name).** `compute_group` has three branches by exception class and two stored statuses (E-88
  QUARANTINED, FAILED; there is no REFUSED): a `Problem` is rolled back and re-raised and was never stored; an
  `EngineError` is stored QUARANTINED with its blocking item; every other exception is stored FAILED with a
  blocking ENGINE_INVARIANT_VIOLATION item. R-103 (b) (4)'s "default: propagate" is withdrawn in that wording and
  replaced by this partition:
  (1) *A crash — an exception that is neither a Problem, an EngineError nor a database error — stays stored
  FAILED* (05 RCP-20). It is deterministic: propagating it would fail a close-run chunk of 250 groups at the same
  group at every attempt, leave no computation row and therefore no item that can be waived, and turn the
  fact-capture promise of DG-CMD-09 into "the invoice cannot be recorded until the engine is fixed".
  (2) *A database error is split by SQLSTATE class through one predicate* (`erev_api/db/errors.py`; built by lane
  QA-BE in the slice of R-95, owned by lane OPS afterwards and shared by `from_db_error` and the retry predicate
  of JOB-COMPUTE-RETRY-1). Transient — rolled back and propagated, nothing stored, the command answers 409
  lock-conflict or 503 and a job attempt fails and is retried: classes 08, 25, 40, 53, 55, 57, 58, 72, XX and the
  period guard's EREV-LED-003. Deterministic — the crash's treatment, stored FAILED with the item while the
  command's fact is recorded: classes 0A, 21, 22, 23, 27, 2F, 42, 44, 54 and P0 other than the period guard. A
  class in neither list counts as transient; lane OPS states what a job does with the group after its last
  attempt (expected: the last attempt stores FAILED with the error as its reason, so that an item exists).
  (3) The seam of R-97 (3) as lanes FIX-D2 and QA-BE agreed it: the pin of the period state rows is one call
  inside `computation._persist`, after the replay return and before the first insert, under the SYSTEM scope on
  every path; its 409 (PERIOD_STATE_MOVED) leaves by the Problem branch, its lock timeout or deadlock by the
  transient side. Lock order stated in 04 DB-07: group row, contract rows, the pinned state rows of all books in
  one statement, ledger heads in RCP-11 order, numbering series, audit head.

- **R-106 (2026-09-30 16:39; lane FIX-D2's finding before building CLO-LOCK-OPEN-REDIRTY-1; lane SECFIX-APR's undecided point on R-64 (6);
  lane QA-BE's report on B-2, merged at 4ef12fa5).**
  (a) *R-101 (a) amended — a blocker that feeds no gate protects nothing.* Measured by lane FIX-D2: BLK-08
  (`blockers.jobs_failed`) is a cockpit count that no gate reads, and the jobs API has no way to run a FAILED job
  again; a waiting or failed PERIOD_OPEN_REDIRTY job would be visible while the opened period's NO_DIRTY_GROUPS
  gate passes. *Rule:* (1) the gate NO_DIRTY_GROUPS of a period fails, by name, while a PERIOD_OPEN_REDIRTY job
  of that period state exists whose newest attempt has not SUCCEEDED; never waivable for that reason; (2) the
  handler is idempotent and recovers without a public route — the kernel's retry for transient classes (R-105),
  and the scheduler's period tick defers the job again when it ended FAILED, never two live jobs for one period
  state; (3) the cockpit's blocker row counts it. A period opened by the schedule or by the open command
  re-marks in its own transaction and has no such job. Witnesses: the lane's red one turns green through the
  job; the opened period's lock request is refused while the job is queued; a job forced FAILED is deferred
  again by the next tick; two decisions that open one period state defer one job.
  (b) *The preparer of a quarantine-mode import (lane SECFIX-APR's reading B).* Measured: an uploader scoped to
  AVM-UK, workspace in quarantine mode, a file with two AVM-UK rows and two AVM-US rows — the US rows are refused
  and stay behind, the UK rows are diffed, and the submission answered 403 under R-64 (6), because the request is
  bound to every entity any row names (R-87 (1)). *Rule:* a preparer is held to the entities of what the request
  would put in force — for an import the rows that commit (`scope.committing_entity_ids`, 04 §16.6, 05 IPL-08) —
  while the request stays bound, for its readers and deciders, to every entity any row names. One seam,
  `SubjectSpec.preparer_entities`, registered by the imports domain only; every other subject keeps R-64 (6) as
  it is. Conditions: the engine fails closed when the preparer's entities are not a subset of the request's; an
  architecture test holds that no other subject registers the seam; witnesses that an uploader is still refused
  when a COMMITTING row names an entity outside their scope, and that a decider who covers only the committing
  entities cannot decide or read the request.
  (c) *B-2 (CTR-ASOF-KPI-1, CTR-OBL-PERF-ENTITY-SCOPE-1) — the lane's judgements confirmed:* an obligation links
  the Explain of its billed-to-date measure; a balance row's net position moves with its obligations' billing
  less revenue between the version's date and the cut, and the functional net position is served only at the
  latest measured period (T-CON-09 keeps none per period — a stated limit of a look-back read); an obligation
  the engine measures nothing for stays as the version holds it and refuses no read; only the header read and
  the balances read refuse by name (422, rule API-C-10), a list row and a command's answer carry `kpis` null; a
  list page reads its versions in one statement. Four expectations moved with R-76 and R-79 (f), each
  strengthened (STALE EXPECTATION; the fourth is the exact-dictionary assertion of the Explain context).
  *The corpus probe becomes a committed answer-key test* (every key computed and read through the reader, as a
  reader of every entity and as one of the contracting entity only; version members and posted revenue compared),
  with its seven differences pinned by name and reason: ONB-CHK-121 ×3 (the opening baseline a cutover emits
  and does not post) and the Step 1 deposit cases 25-7A / 25-7B ×4.
  *Item CTR-KPI-25-7-1, a question before it is a ruling (lane ENG-FX reads; candidate AD-57):* for a contract
  that failed Step 1 and whose deposit was released to revenue under 606-10-25-7, the ledger's REVENUE role holds
  the amount and the header's "Recognized" reads 0.00, because no obligation carries it. A figure on the header
  ties to the ledger or says why it does not; what the header shows for such a contract is decided on the
  lane's reading of what the version holds.
  *Noted:* T-CON-09 `net_position_txn` is written at the version's date beside balances of the latest period and
  has no reader but the balance reads (now corrected by the overlay); 04 says which date the stored column
  carries.

- **R-107 (2026-09-30 16:51; lane ENG-FX's measurement for ENG-S10-FUTURE-INCEPTION-1; lane WEB-QA's reports on W-10, W-20, F2 and W-21;
  lane FIX-D2's report on CLO-FREEZE-SCOPE-1, merged at 656582f2; lane F-ADM-WEB's report on RPS-21).**
  (a) *ENG-S10-FUTURE-INCEPTION-1 — the lane's proposal.* Measured on main's engine: a contract, or every member
  of a group, whose inception lies beyond the open periods of its contracting entity raises IndexError in stage
  10 (`_version_period`), in both billing modes; a group across two entities fails when one entity's periods are

  start"); with both guarded 721 of 721 corpus bundles in booked-ahead form compute with no posting intent and no
  balance row, and there is no third reader. ENGINE_SPEC_B C-05 evaluates cumulative targets "from the group's
  inception period through the horizon" and says nothing for the empty set — a gap. *Rule:* per contracting
  entity without a measured period stage 10 presents no period target, entry or member balance, and each
  obligation keeps its version-date `netting_reclass_amount` of zero WITH a node (the exact-column exports bind
  it); stage 12 processes nothing for a unit whose first date lies after the horizon. The version's own
  measures remain those of its date and are not postings (C-05 says so). Witnesses: the failing shapes as engine
  tests, the two-entity case also through the database, the corpus comparison. It joins the engine version step
  of R-88 (c).
  (b) *W-10, the Step 1 screens (R-61 (f), R-89), accepted with the lane's follow-up.* The drawer no longer
  appends the assessment that made its own review stale; review first, then assessment, behind the gate
  "Record criteria met" and "Submit for activation". PRD J-03 gains the reviewer's step and the assessment step
  (J-03.3a, J-03.3b). The lane's two-role walk becomes a committed e2e row — three product defects had reached
  its first commit because no e2e step drove either drawer. The not-probable review asks the two facts 04
  T-CON-19 requires for 606-10-25-7 (the consideration received is non-refundable; the date event (c) was met);
  their labels and help text are candidate copy for the independent accountant, and the help line states what the
  IFRS 15 book does with event (c) as ENGINE_SPEC S02-R-07 has it (IFRS 15.15 knows events (a) and (b) only).
  "Catch-up total" is not shown beside per-book catch-ups: books are parallel ledgers.
  *Item W-23 (lane WEB-QA):* thirteen places build an Idempotency-Key per call, so a lost response and a second
  press send a create twice under two keys (judgement records, attachments, bulk contract commands); one helper
  keeps the key per step until a success or an answer the API keeps.
  *Item CTR-LIST-STATUS-DERIVED-1 (lane QA-BE, B-3):* `GET /contracts?status=DRAFT` lists a contract whose item
  reads PENDING_REVIEW — a filter matches what the row shows.
  (c) *CLO-FREEZE-SCOPE-1 — the lane's judgement confirmed:* the dataset producers of a lock read through a
  read-only transaction of the tenant's SYSTEM principal, the mechanism the coverage guard already has; the
  decider's session is not widened. Measured before the fix: a Controller scoped to AVM-UK froze the waterfall

  rollforward closing at 0.00, with no refusal.
  (d) *RPS-21, the audit log and chain verification screens — the lane's seventeen judgements confirmed.* What
  the API does not answer is marked "Waits" on the screen's document and nothing is approximated in the client;
  the gaps are lane API-GAPS's G-1 and G-2 (now with the actor's membership id, a bounded lookup of the log's

  F-RPS-REG, with the entity-scoped grants):* `/me` states one entity scope, not one per permission, so a holder
  restricted to named entities is offered a command the API refuses.

- **R-108 (2026-09-30 16:55; lane API-GAPS's pre-build note for AUD-API-GAPS-1; lane OPS's second report, evidence head 4bc7e0df).**
  (a) *The events of one contract are found by a stored key (the lane's way K2), not by the object graph at read
  time.* An audit event is keyed by its object's table and id; a modification, an estimate version, a hold, an
  override or an adjustment of a contract does not name the contract, and the booking facts store their ids in
  `detail`. The read-time graph (K3) misses holds, overrides, adjustments and the earlier request of a rejected
  row, and its second arm grows with the tenant's activity: an audit screen is answered completely or told why
  not. *Rule:* every contract-scoped audit event carries `detail.contract_id` (several contracts: the lane
  proposes the form), passed by its writer through one helper; a partial expression index on the tenant, that
  key and the time of the event (Alembic revision 0101); the chain's canonical form is unchanged; the index's
  lock footprint is stated in 05 §2.7 with the doctor's rule holding. Held by the audit-coverage walk (a closed
  list of contract-scoped object types; an object type in neither list fails), a route witness over a world
  with a modification, an estimate version, a hold and a resubmitted request, and a plan test. Before a first
  release "complete from then on" is complete. The other reads as the lane proposes: `outcome` exact over
  E-81; `id` and `chain_seq` as list filters that lift the default window; `object_label` from a closed list;
  the window always has a start (30 days when none is sent).
  (b) *Lane OPS's second report is accepted* (private relay opt-in; `make restore-drill`; the web image's
  upstream resolved per request; the session cookie Secure unless the configured origin is an http loopback
  origin; docker-build leaves no root manifest; SQLSTATE class 53 and the lost connection answer 503 with
  Retry-After; the production gate on operator commands and the refusal of placeholder database passwords;
  Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy; images without pip and setuid files; CLI logging; the re-issue of the first
  Tenant Admin's invitation; a transient server fault is no computation result). Rulings on its open points:
  (1) *JOB-RETRY-TABLE-1:* the registered retry policy of JOURNAL_RUN_CALCULATE and POLICY_SIMULATION follows 05
  §5.6 where a second attempt is safe, else 05 is corrected with the reason; SYNC_RUN's row says what is built.
  (2) The catch-alls that turn any exception into a stored result take the predicate of R-105 once it is on
  main: the import commit and validation (lane SECFIX-IMP), the snapshot store (lane F-SNP), the journal
  completeness read (lane F-CLO-A); the SSP calculator stays.
  (3) A 503 has its own copy (PRD ERR-64): "nothing was saved" is not true of a lost connection that hid a
  successful commit.
  (4) *OPS-SBX-KEY-1:* no sandbox copies on the hosted shape in the first release — they fail closed as today
  (a known limitation); the worker creating a sandbox tenant's key under a sandbox-only sub-prefix is the
  design of record, built when a hosted deployment is commissioned and its grant can be verified on a live
  project. Likewise the accessor binding for adapter secrets of a workspace's namespace: not granted while the
  release ships no live adapter (a grant without a use is a dormant privilege).
  (5) Built next by the lane: the root manifest restored at exit by the compose and ZAP targets; a database
  health check that needs the application's database and role; an operator command for an identity provider's
  domains (OPS-IDP-DOMAINS-1); the outbox row's last error cleared on dispatch.
  (6) *PLATFORM-SCOPE-ALLOWLIST-1:* twelve modules open a platform session where 05 TXN-07 names three uses. That
  session is the one door across tenants: its callers are an allow-list held by an architecture test, one
  reason each, and TXN-07 states them.
  *Merge order:* lane OPS's branch before lane QA-BE's slice of R-95 — both rewrite the catch-all of the compute
  job and the database error module; the predicate of R-105 is built on the lane's `server_unavailable`.

- **R-109 (2026-09-30 16:59; lane SECFIX-IMP's pre-build line for the residuals slice of R-98 with IMP-FLOOR-AMOUNT-1).**
  (a) *An API client's upload keeps the scopes its creating token carried* (`import_upload.uploader_scopes`, NULL
  for a person's upload; Alembic revision 0102; immutable through the existing transition guard): the bounds at
  validation, dry run and commit are the client's grants at that time narrowed to those scopes. A rule resting on
  job parameters is not taken.
  (b) *In quarantine mode the unit that loads whole or not at all is the subject the rows build:* the contract,
  for every template with a contract column (the cross-row rules were evaluated with the refused row present,
  and the legacy progress template aggregates by key) — PRD IMP-134, IMPORT_CONTRACT_INCOMPLETE; and the SSP book
  version for the legacy SKU SSP template (a version approved without its refused rows is not the study that
  was uploaded).
  (c) A shredded source is named at submission (409, FILE_SHREDDED) and ends a commit FAILED with its
  processing item naming it; no new code.
  (d) *IMP-FLOOR-AMOUNT-1:* the statement of an upload's underlying approvals carries, per subject type, the
  distinct instances with their flags and amount (activation: the functional amount of the booking, none across
  currencies; estimate version: the P&L impact; modification: the change of the transaction price when the
  contract currency is the functional one; judgement record and SSP book version: no amount; the setup
  import's element version: 0.00). An unevaluated instance or amount counts as the rule's strictest outcome.
  The statement and `approval_floor` are lane SECFIX-IMP's; the reader — the approver slots routed once per
  instance, keeping the longest, an unevaluated amount meeting every amount condition — is lane SECFIX-APR's,
  after its Parts 1 to 3 are on main.
  (e) An unresolved reference in a template that is not tenant-level leaves the upload's entities unresolved, so
  its request is bound to every entity (fail-closed; R-106 (b) for its preparer).
  (f) *A refused step is not retried through another path.* The supervisor asked the lane at 16:22 to try, once,
  the read of `tests/pg/test_migration_capture_pg.py` that its session had been refused on its earlier
  worktree, now through the new worktree's path. The lane declined — the refusal names another path and a later
  turn as the same outcome — and was right; the request is withdrawn. The three capture witnesses stay red until
  Ray's rule or word lifts the refusal (RESUME §6 item 6); R-44 (d) is read to include the supervisor's own
  suggestions.

- **R-110 (2026-09-30 17:02; lane F-CLO-A's design note on item JE-CSV-FUNCTIONAL-1, measured on the corpus and on AVM-UK; an accounting
  ruling pending the independent accountant, candidate AD-55; lane FIX-D2's pre-build line for the re-marking
  job).**
  (a) *The fact.* A journal line holds a transaction and a functional amount and a batch is one document of one
  transaction currency; the CSV of REQ-JE-011 and the two ERP payloads state the transaction amounts only. So
  both lines of every FX_REMEASUREMENT entry (JET-10; transaction amount nil) leave eRev as 0.00 / 0.00 — 40 such
  lines in the corpus, and every month for an entity with an open foreign-currency position once the close run's
  FX pass exists — and the functional amount of EVERY line of a foreign-currency batch reaches the ledger
  nowhere: the ledger converts at its own rate what eRev measured at the period's average, a liability layer's
  historical rate or the closing rate. Measured on AVM-UK FY2026-P08 (GBP books, a USD contract): the FX account
  differs from the subledger by 1,080.00 for good, revenue by 540.00, and the pull answers two currencies, which
  the subledger-to-GL reconciliation refuses by name — the entity could attach no trial balance at all.
  (b) *Ruling — the lane's recommendation.* (1) The CSV carries both: the eleven columns of REQ-JE-011 stay and
  `functional_currency`, `debit_functional`, `credit_functional` join them, with functional totals in the
  manifest; the importer decides what it books. (2) A batch sent through an ERP adapter is stated in the
  entity's FUNCTIONAL currency with the lines' functional amounts, the transaction currency and amounts as
  informational members; the run, the batch key and the external ids are unchanged, and neither a conversion nor
  a rounding line is needed, because every line has its functional amount and an entry's functional debits
  equal its credits (05 ADP-11). The ledger then holds what the subledger holds, whatever its own rates, and
  eRev's JET-10 is the only remeasurement of these balances. The two journal APIs the adapters model take one
  currency and one rate per document and one amount per line, so no form with per-line functional members
  books anything there (read from their documentation by the lane; not verified against a live system). A
  separate functional batch for the remeasurement entries alone is not taken: it repairs the FX account and
  leaves revenue and the contract liability to the ledger's rate. A run already calculated is left alone.
  (3) Documents first: 03 REQ-JE-011, 04 T-SL-07 and §16.7, 05 ADP-10, the user guide and the runbook's
  integration section, which state for the operator of the ledger: the accounts eRev posts to carry these
  balances in the functional currency only, the transaction-currency detail stays in eRev, and the ledger must
  not revalue them itself.
  *For the accountant (AD-55):* whether a ledger that receives revenue, contract-balance and FX entries in the
  functional currency only, measured by the subledger, is an acceptable book of record for a foreign-currency
  contract under ASC 830 / IAS 21 as the POLICIES ALG-08 measures it; and the interplay with a ledger that
  posts its own invoices to the contract liability account in the transaction currency (ERP billing mode,
  R-74 (e)): that account then holds entries in two currencies whose functional sum is right and whose
  transaction-currency balance is not meaningful.
  *Owner and order:* lane F-CLO-A, after the CLO-12 / CLO-14 merge; CLO-15's trial balance mock is built on this
  form from the start. It is a release blocker for a workspace with a foreign-currency contract; the witness is
  AVM-UK's August batch through both adapters and the CSV, and the reconciliation of that entity tying once the
  close run's FX pass is on main.
  (c) *The re-marking job and the blocker row (amends R-101 (a), R-106 (a) (3)).* BLK-08 is labelled "Failed
  jobs": it counts a PERIOD_OPEN_REDIRTY job only when the newest job of the period state ended FAILED; a queued
  or running one is carried by the gate's detail ("Contracts not re-marked since the period was opened"), not
  by that row. The waive route refuses the gate by the same detail while the re-marking has not succeeded; no
  new copy row. Two lock decisions cannot open one period state (it is opened once), so the witness is two
  deferrals for one state giving one job.

- **R-111 (2026-09-30 17:28; independent review, by code reading, of lane SECFIX-PLT's merge a7d63e81 as it stands on main 40bc7142; record
  kept by the supervisor as `.run/sup2/reviews/secfix-plt-review-a7d63e81.md`; refines R-48).** The review confirms
  the server-side second factor (one guard behind every cookie route, the step computed per request, no route
  outside it but the fourteen public paths), the file-read registry, the OIDC binding and the secret namespace as
  ruled, and found three holes and seven defects or gaps beside them. Ruled, owner lane SECFIX-PLT unless named,
  as one slice after its three reports in flight, in this order:
  (1) *HOLE — a command binds a caller-named file without asking whether the caller may read it.* `POST
  /reconciliations/{id}/attach-trial-balance` (CLO-17, merged after the lane) takes any IMPORT_SOURCE file id: an
  accountant scoped to one entity names another entity's uploaded trial balance, the job stores it as the
  reconciliation's source and the registry then serves its content through the reconciliation's own readers; the
  422s before that tell a missing file from a wrong purpose, media type or header. The same class, weaker: `POST
  /ssp-calculator-runs` (`parameters.pool_file_id`) and `POST /migrations` (the file is opened before its purpose
  is checked: an existence-and-purpose oracle); the migration replay plan's `file_id` not verified. *Rule
  (FILE-BIND-READABLE-1):* every command that takes a file id from its caller binds it through one helper that
  first asks `file_access.readable` for that caller and answers what a missing file answers otherwise, before
  any other property of the file is read; an architecture test holds every request member that names a file to
  that helper. The reconciliation hunk is built by lane SECFIX-PLT in lane F-CLO-B's file (one call in
  `_file_lines`; that lane is told).
  (2) *HOLE — a delegate carries delegated `requires_mfa` permissions without a second factor.* The gate reads
  role grants only: a member with no role and no factor, given a delegation of `access.approve`, reads the
  requests, previews and attachments with a password-only session and, by enrolling an authenticator with that
  password alone, decides on the delegator's behalf. *Rule:* (a) a member who holds an in-force delegation of a
  `requires_mfa` permission is MFA-mandatory like its holder, for reads too; (b) a delegation is created only to
  a member with a confirmed second factor (refused by name otherwise), so a delegation never turns a
  password-only account into an approver.
  (3) The delegate's own entity scope at the decision (R-41 (1)) is not on main: it arrives with lane SECFIX-APR's
  parts — already a release blocker.
  (4) *Delegation limits (BR-PLT-07).* The delegation ends no later than 90 days after the command that creates
  it (today the 90 days run from an unbounded `valid_from`, and rows may follow one another); a delegation ends
  — it does not lie dormant — when its delegator loses the permission or the membership; an access
  administrator ends any delegation with a step-up; the command runs the separation-of-duties check a role
  assignment runs on the delegate's grants with the delegated permission (the lane first states what the SoD
  engine offers for it).
  (5) *Refusals that differ from "does not exist".* Voiding an attachment the caller did not create answers 404.
  `POST /files` answers another person's row — id, file name, uploader — to whoever uploads the same bytes:
  *item FILE-DEDUPE-READER-1, pre-build line first:* the answer never names a row its caller may not read, and a
  second uploader of the same bytes must still be able to use the upload (with (1) the first uploader's row
  would be refused them).
  (6) *The audit trail of sign-in and files.* A passed second-factor challenge writes a security event (today
  LOGIN_SUCCEEDED is written at the password step); a second-factor refusal outside an open workspace (operators,
  users of several workspaces) writes one too; the MFA and step-up refusals of the delegation commands are
  audited; a content read through `GET /files/{id}/content` is audited, success and refusal, as report and
  journal downloads are. That a pending session's refused calls reach the workspace's audit chain (up to the
  session bucket's rate) stays as ruled and is listed for the ITGC owner.
  (7) *Revision 0092 follow-up:* `app_user.identity_provider_subject` is write-once and
  `identity_provider_id` moves only from NULL, by trigger or transition guard (the application role keeps the
  UPDATE grant from revision 0004); in the revision the lane already holds for the security-event index.
  (8) *An upload is authenticated before its body is read.* FastAPI parses a multipart form before it solves
  the guard, so `POST /api/v1/files` receives and spools up to the 500 MiB limit from a client without a
  session, and the middleware checks the declared length only. *Rule:* the session or token is checked before
  the body is read, and the size limit is enforced while reading, whatever length was declared.
  (9) The review's list of rule branches without a test becomes tests in the same slice (the step becoming due
  inside a session, an OIDC or invitation session owing it, the 422 branches of the delegation command, the
  owners of the registry never exercised through `/files`, the OIDC refusals).

- **R-112 (2026-09-30 18:59; rulings given to the lanes by message between 17:09 and 19:00 on 2026-09-30, or given with
  this entry, logged together; the messages are kept in the session record. Each part names its lane; refines
  R-52, R-97, R-101, R-106, R-108).**
  (a) *CLO-20, the period-end step of a close run over an earlier period nobody closed (lane F-CLO-B).* The
  engine's close pass emits the period-end amounts of every postable period; the lane measured on WLD-K-03 that a
  run of September over an August never closed by a run would, reading "only the run's period", post the reversal
  of a reclass that was never posted, and built (C): the run also posts the earlier period's entries. *Ruled (R),
  not (C):* a close run posts the period-end amounts of ITS period and the reversal of its own reclass in the next;
  when the pass has an amount for an EARLIER postable period of the entity and book whose period end no run has
  posted, the step refuses by name ("<period> has period-end amounts no close run has posted; run its close
  first"), the run ends FAILED at that step and is resumed after the earlier period's own run. Reasons: periods
  close in order since the lock order of R-6 is on main; under (C) a run writes ledger lines into a period whose
  reconciliations may have been reviewed and into a REOPENED period without the second approval BR-CLS-06 asks of
  every other writer; (R) never posts the unmatched reversal — it posts nothing and says which period comes first.
  Kept from (C): the remeasurement and the reclass of the run's month measured on the remeasured carrying, and the
  reversal dated the first day of the next period.
  (b) *CLO-12 and CLO-14 (lane F-CLO-A; both reports accepted, main 094f7174).* (1) ADJ-VOID-REFUSE-1: `POST
  /events/{id}/request-void` refuses a MANUAL_ADJUSTMENT_APPLIED event by name until a reversal command exists — a
  voided event beside a T-SL-05 row that stays POSTED is two truths; a posted adjustment is corrected by a further
  adjustment; PRD SM-10's POSTED → VOIDED is not built in 1.0 (known limitation, user guide). Copy: a convention
  row of `invalid-transition`, PRD ERR-70. (2) A DRAFT adjustment is CHANGED only by its creator, as a judgement
  record is (R-64 (7) (a)), so the self-approval rule cannot be stepped round by a second editor: PATCH by another
  person answers 403 `forbidden` naming the creator (convention row, PRD ERR-71). Discard and submit stay open to
  every holder of the preparing permission — a discard writes no content and keeps an absent creator's draft from
  standing in the way; the creator and the submitter are both excluded from approving. The web side hides or
  disables Edit on another person's draft with CLO-25's screens (lane F-CLO-WEB). (3) The four limits for a
  foreign-currency contract are stated limits of 1.0 (for the accountant: a correcting journal or reclassification
  cannot be booked on a foreign-currency contract). (4) T-PLT-35's `period.locked` carries {id, href}, as every
  other kind. (5) Refusal copies: PRD ERR-67 (more than one active general ledger connection), ERR-68
  (`max_lines_per_chunk`), ERR-69 (an entry that cannot be chunked) as convention rows of `validation-failed`; the
  two messages of a failed batch take no number and are stated in 05 beside ADP-31. (6) From the independent review
  in lane SECFIX-CLO's part 8 (N2): `dispatch_batch` recounts and re-totals the lines it is about to send against
  the batch's stored count and totals and refuses on a difference (JRN-DISPATCH-RECOUNT-1, with CLO-15, under
  CLO-15's numbers); a BEFORE INSERT guard on `journal_line` refuses a line whose batch is not `draft` (Alembic
  0104, after CLO-15). (7) One hunk in lane F-CLO-B's `domain/close/reconciliations.py`, made by lane F-CLO-A with
  that lane's consent, is accepted as a PRODUCT DEFECT repair: `_pull_and_attach` built the adapter context without
  the connection's `base_url` and `config`, so with the worker's factories every trial-balance pull through a
  NETSUITE or QUICKBOOKS_ONLINE connection ended sync-objects-not-applied.
  (c) *JRN-FAILED-CANCEL-1, the way out of a `failed` journal run (lane F-CLO-A's design note; release blocker — a
  batch the ledger can never accept must not hold a period for good).* Ruled as the note's A with its point 5. A
  `failed` run is cancellable when every batch is `failed` or `approved` (none `exported` or `acknowledged`), it is
  the latest run of its entity, book and period, no message of the run is claimed or pending, and THE LEDGER HOLDS
  NONE OF ITS FAILED BATCHES — which stored facts cannot say (a timeout can follow an acceptance), so the cancel of
  a failed run ASKS the ledger: `POST /journal-runs/{id}/cancel` on a failed run answers 202 with a job; the worker
  calls `get_posting` per failed ERP batch; a batch the ledger holds gets its DUPLICATE receipt and is acknowledged
  and the cancel is refused by name; a ledger that cannot be reached leaves everything as it is; none held → one
  transaction under the run, batch and coverage locks cancels, after re-checking that no batch moved. E-34 gains
  `failed` → `cancelled` for run and batch (Alembic 0108); the cancelled batches' JOURNAL_EXPORT_FAILED items are
  resolved with the cancel's reason and a CTL-021 execution records it; the next run starts where the non-cancelled
  runs of the key end, with new external ids, as a `draft` to be approved again. A run with one batch already in a
  ledger is NOT cancellable; its failed batch leaves by retry or by the manual hand-over of point 5: after the same
  ledger check the batch goes `failed` → `exported` with its file and no message, a person posts it by hand and
  records the ledger's document (`acknowledge`, MANUAL_CONFIRMATION, BR-JE-03). Not in 1.0: cancelling one batch of
  a half-posted run. Stated limit: wrong content in a half-posted run is corrected in the subledger and posted by
  hand with its correction. Draft and approved runs keep their 200. The lane reuses an existing job kind if one
  fits and says so; a new kind moves the pins of E-14 with PHASES under the supervisor's instruction. Copies: PRD
  ERR-73, ERR-74.
  (d) *Kernel error mapping (lane OPS; second report on main a2b75fd4).* PRD ERR-66: SQLSTATE 57014 answers 503
  `statement-timeout` with `Retry-After: 30`, never kept for the idempotency key. `period-closed` is not kept for
  the key either — DG-KRN-IDEM-03 lists the slugs that are not kept (`lock-conflict`, `period-closed`); every other
  409 stays kept. The 503's sentence on screen is lane WEB-QA's (W-24, SCREENS_B 1.43). The seam of R-105 (2) is
  agreed by both lanes as built by lane QA-BE: ONE predicate `db.errors.is_transient` decides "propagate or store";
  `compute_group`'s catch-all keeps the `server_unavailable_in` branch first (nothing is sent to a server that
  cannot answer), then rolls the savepoint back, then re-raises a transient error; a deterministic database error
  and a crash are stored FAILED. Lane OPS owns the module from that merge and builds on it the retry policy of
  CONTRACT_COMPUTE, the last attempt's stored reason, `from_db_error` and the idempotency rule.
  (e) *The LEGACY book and the lock (lane FIX-D2, CLO-LOCK-LEGACY-1; refines R-97 (2)).* Measured: a computation
  posts a LEGACY line into a period whose primary book is locked, and a LEGACY period can be soft-closed and never
  locked. Ruled: (1) the planner agrees with the guard — bundle assembly gives the engine a LEGACY period as
  postable only when the entity's PRIMARY book's state of that period is postable too; the amount is planned into
  the first later period postable in both, origin unchanged (bundle data only; 05 RCP-15 first); (2) the rule holds
  for every LEGACY line in either journal mode; (3) guard only, primary row first, FOR SHARE — the primary decision
  takes no second state row — and the coverage guard counts the LEGACY lines of the period; (4) `is_post_reopen` of
  a LEGACY line follows the primary's state; (5) the LEGACY book has no close of its own in 1.0: start-close,
  request-lock, request-reopen and request-permanent-lock refuse a LEGACY period state by name ("The legacy book
  follows the close of <primary book>"); `open` is unchanged. Alembic 0105. Also confirmed: the seal judgement of
  LED-SEAL-REOPEN-RACE-1 (the writer reads no period state; the guard decides `is_post_reopen`); PHASES rev 1.5
  edited by the lane on the supervisor's instruction for PERIOD_OPEN_REDIRTY (a disclosed exception to DG-LAY-01).
  (f) *The computation's pin on the period state rows (lane FIX-D2's note for R-97 (3)) — accepted as designed.*
  One statement in `computation._persist`, after the replay return and before the first write: the state rows of
  every (entity, book, period) the bundle holds as open, closing or reopened, ordered, FOR SHARE; a row whose state
  differs from the bundle's ends the computation 409 `lock-conflict`, rule PERIOD_STATE_MOVED (PRD ERR-72), nothing
  written; `future` and closed rows are not pinned. Accepted cost, to be stated in 04 DB-07 and the runbook with
  the measured figures: a state change of a postable period now waits for every computation in flight that holds
  the period as postable, up to `lock_timeout`, then answers 409; share requests do not queue behind a waiting
  changer, so a steady stream of computations can hold a start-close or a lock decision off — the remedy is to
  retry, and a close is started when postings are quiet. Built after CLO-LOCK-LEGACY-1, on lane QA-BE's slice.
  (g) *API-GAPS.* G-1 accepted and on main (975742d5): judgements 1 to 9 confirmed, the architecture gate on a new
  by-person bare uuid kept. K2 (R-108): the partial index `(tenant_id, (detail->>'contract_id'), occurred_at)`;
  events of several contracts carry `detail.contract_ids` (the members at the time of the event) with a partial GIN
  index of its own — two indexes, Alembic 0101; a stream append's fact carries the import's or sync run's id;
  approval requests and decisions get the key with G-3 / G-4 after lane SECFIX-APR's merge. G-4: a display spec per
  subject type, at most 50 items per collection with "<n> more", `subject.content_current`; the as-approved content
  of a decided request is evidenced by its hash, its stored preview and the subject's audit events — a stored
  snapshot is not built (APR-CONTENT-SNAPSHOT-1, for the ITGC owner). New: G-6, from lane F-ADM-WEB's integrations
  screens — `last_sync_run` without test runs or with its `kind` and whether totals were recorded; a save that
  refuses a `base_url` the adapter client cannot reach (or a refusal that says an address is needed); a
  `started_at` sort key on GET /sync-runs. G-2 (h): `GET /approval-delegations/delegates?q=` — the ACTIVE members
  the delegation command would accept as a delegate, other than the caller (membership id, display name, email),
  open to every holder of an approval permission; after lane SECFIX-APR's merge.
  (h) *Screens.* WEB-QA: F4 (a panel tab that changes `pane` removes the list parameters of the pane it leaves;
  SCREENS 1.19); W-24. W-17's three decisions, SCREENS 1.21: (1) the Explain of Recognized, Billed and Scheduled
  opens the panel at a LIST level — the measure and measured period, the cell's value, a table "By obligation" from
  GET /contracts/{id}/obligations with a button that pushes that obligation's explanation onto the panel's Back
  stack, and the sentence on consideration payable where the contract has it; no Verify and no trace link at the
  list level; the client adds no money; (2) the stale marker of "Figures as of <period>" reads "Measured at <period
  label>, the last period this contract was computed for."; "Recalculation is queued." stays for a job that exists;
  (3) the KPI strip's heading always names the measured period; the meta row keeps "Figures as of" for the stale
  case. Banners 1 and 4 bind when lane QA-BE's slice brings API-S-Contract `computation`. F-ADM-WEB: DIN-18's
  judgements 1 to 16 confirmed, among them that its e2e runs no sync (the Salesforce mock serves another scenario;
  a poll into Avenmoor would leave the interface gate red for the other rows) — "Run sync" is held in vitest until
  the seed carries its own connections (DEMO-INTEGRATIONS-1, lane QA-BE B-4: both connections with the stack's API
  origin as a setting, `secret_ref` null, one WEBHOOK_BATCH run with totals); SCREENS §14.8's "1 order · 2 lines"
  becomes "<n> records" (T-INT-02 holds one count per side). WEB-16: the Entity filter of the SF-12 lists is an
  explicit chip `f.entity`, never the context pill's entity (as the home table, D-90a), so tenant-wide requests are
  not hidden and the count agrees with the list; the pill reads "Not used on this page" there (that line of the
  shell is given to lane F-ADM-WEB); the delegate picker reads G-2 (h) when it exists and is disabled with its
  reason for a member without `user.manage` until then. AUD-ACTOR-BIND-1 (lane F-ADM-WEB): the audit log names an
  API client and an operator from `actor.display_name` and renders "On behalf of" from the Actor.
  (i) *Configuration security (lane ENG-FX, SECFIX-CFG report; SN-7 and SN-8 FIXED, merged with this ruling).* The
  product pin's one refinement of R-21 is accepted: a product is recorded from the first computation in which a
  member contract carrying it is past DRAFT. The request for a product's policy values is one subject routed to
  `config.approve`, a floor. Ruled on what the report measured: (1) CFG-BACKDATE-1, PRODUCT DEFECT, release
  blocker: an approved template version dated BEFORE an existing contract's inception superseded the version that
  contract was computed on and re-timed its posted revenue, with an approval that showed no impact. Rule: a version
  that supersedes another takes effect no earlier than its publication — an `effective_from` earlier than the
  decision instant is refused at submission and again at the decision, by name ("withdraw it and submit it with a
  later date"; the request is left for its withdrawal or rejection); a first version keeps its free date (setup and
  migration need it). It holds for the obligation template and for every other kind of `lifecycle.publish` whose
  version a computation selects by effective date without a pin; the lane names for each kind how its version is
  selected (by date, by a pin on the contract, at a `known_at`) and leaves alone, with the reason, a kind that is
  pinned or read at a cut. POLICIES rule 3 K stands: applying a version to existing contracts needs
  `apply_to_existing`, which 1.0 does not offer. The lane first lists every seed and test that publishes a
  superseding version dated in the past and says how each keeps working. (2) PIN-K-COMBINATION-1, PRODUCT DEFECT: a
  combined group computed under the registry value in force at the combination, not under either member's pin
  (measured: 125.13 less and 375.74 more posted revenue than the same combination without the registry change).
  Rule: the first computation of a combined group takes `pinned_policies` from the former group of the member with
  the earliest inception, then external id — the order the product pins use; candidate AD-58 for the accountant.
  (3) ACT-CONTENT-PRODUCT-1: the CONTRACT_ACTIVATION content names the product state its preview was computed on
  (the pin's members), so an edit of a product between preview and approval makes the decision stale; the
  non-policy members of a product stay a direct edit (R-21). After lane SECFIX-APR's merge. (4)
  PRODUCT-CODE-FREEZE-1: a product's `code` is frozen once a contract line, an SSP entry or a mapping rule names it
  (DB-05). (5) 04 T-REF-20 says as built that the template's `policy_values` win over the product's and its
  `ratable_convention` column is the default where neither names the convention. (6) Known limitations:
  `apply_to_existing` for product changes is not offered; the approver of a COMBINATION sees no figures
  (CMB-PREVIEW-1, L4-1-Q-16). (7) The zero node of ENG-S10-FUTURE-INCEPTION-1 stands on a formula of its own
  (`pos.reclass_attribution.no_measured_period.v1`). (8) EXPLAIN-RENDER-1, PRODUCT DEFECT: 1,464 corpus nodes in
  three formulas whose narrative cannot render and a route that does not catch it — the narratives repaired, a
  committed test that renders every node of every last-checkpoint corpus bundle, and `build_explain` answering a
  named problem for a node it cannot render.
  (j) *SECFIX-CLO's integration (main 2cbd093d) and what it opened.* The earlier state rows read FOR SHARE NOWAIT
  by the lock order: accepted. Owners: CLO-PERMLOCK-PERM-1, CLO-CANCEL-CLOSE-REOPENED-1 (cancel-close returns a
  period to the state it was soft-closed from), DQ-RESOLVE-1, CLO-DECISION-MONITORS-1 (measure first),
  CLO-CHECKLIST-REFRESH-RACE-1, the terminal-state freeze (Alembic 0106), AUD-VERIFY-KEY-TENANT-1 and the
  `period.locked` emitter — lane SECFIX-CLO, second part; SYNC-RUN-CLEAR-1 — lane F-CLO-A; LOCK-ORDER-UI-1 — lane
  F-CLO-WEB; S18 — lane F-RPS-REG. FOUND AT THE INTEGRATION, RELEASE BLOCKER, lane F-RPS-REG: RPT-OOP-ROWKEY-1 —
  the out-of-period register's row key does not tell apart the late lines of one event that belong to different
  origin periods, so the lock of a period that holds such lines fails on "duplicate row_key" (S15-R-18); the lock
  order made it reachable in the lock-freeze test, whose late contract the supervisor re-dated inside the locked
  period (a STALE TEST WORLD repair; the defect stays open under its own witness).
  (k) *Practice.* A document row of a register index grows while its lane is unmerged; a merged number takes no
  second text and the next part asks for a new index. Merge order of the evening: lanes API-GAPS G-1 and
  SECFIX-CFG, then lane SECFIX-APR's Parts 1 to 3 before anything else that touches the approvals engine.

- **R-113 (2026-09-30 20:12; rulings given by message between 19:00 and 20:20 on 2026-09-30 or with this entry; the fourth
  browser pass on main d0c5eceb; post-merge runs v14 and v15. Refines R-6, R-38, R-73, R-112 (g) and (i).)**
  (a) *Five tests met the lock order on main and were repaired by the supervisor as STALE TEST WORLD — fixture
  state only, no assertion moved.* The integration of lane SECFIX-CLO (2cbd093d) was built on main 40bc7142; four
  rules that had landed beside it met on main for the first time. (1) `support.worlds.period_locked` locked a
  period with earlier periods open (lane FIX-D2's seal-race module, two register tests, the K-07 world): the helper
  now closes every earlier period of the entity first with `close_world.periods_closed_before` (08e82597). (2)
  `journals/test_adjustments.py::test_pending_adjustment_blocks_lock_until_deferred` (CLO-12) asked for the lock of
  September with January to August open: the same fixture call (5d679dd4). (3) `journals/test_summarise.py`, the
  SC-7 witness, cancelled a key's runs in the order a select returned them; CLO-14's rule R-52 (b) refuses the
  oldest while a later run stands: the witness cancels newest first (ada3f6f0). (4)
  `reports/test_registers_close.py::test_late_entry_window` unpacked the tenant's one period lock; the fixture's
  earlier periods now hold lock rows: it selects the lock of August (7d1ab7cc). Reported by lanes F-CLO-A and
  SECFIX-APR and by runs v14 (524 passed) and v15 (127 passed), which otherwise show the standing reds only.
  *Practice:* a post-merge run on main follows every integration made on an older main, before the next merge.
  (b) *CFG-BACKDATE-1 — the form follows how a version is chosen (lane ENG-FX's reading per kind; refines R-112 (i)
  (1)).* A kind the engine chooses by a contract DATE — the obligation template, and the POB_ASSIGNMENT and
  SSP_ASSIGNMENT rule sets from the day their loader exists — takes the DATE form: a version that supersedes
  another needs an effective date, and the UTC date of `effective_from` is later than today's date in every active
  entity's time zone, at submission and at the decision; the publication day is thereby closed (a contract posted
  in the morning never meets a version decided in the afternoon). A kind chosen at an INSTANT — the registry
  (policy) version and the rule sets the platform reads at the instant of an act — takes the INSTANT form:
  `effective_from` null or not earlier than the decision instant, which closes the gap at a past `known_at` in
  which neither version answered. Import mapping profiles are named by id; account mapping (chosen at `known_at`,
  same-instant republication designed) is left as it is. A first version keeps its free date; for a template that
  reaches no posted contract because the product pin holds the product's default template. PRD ERR-75. *SSP book
  versions are probed first, in two forms* — a later-approved version of the same book whose range covers a posted
  contract's pricing date, and a new book of narrower scope dated in the past — and ruled on the lane's figures.
  *Design of record:* ENGINE_SPEC S03-R-02 ("and is pinned") and POLICIES POL-070 ("Pins SSP version id and row per
  POB") promise a pin that is recorded and never read back; for 1.0 the documents say as built that the publication
  rule, not a read-back, keeps a later version from a posted contract; the read-back is item PIN-READBACK-1.
  (c) *An audit event is addressed by its chain sequence (lane API-GAPS G-2; refines R-108).* No index of T-PLT-19
  leads with `id`, and an id's own time is not `occurred_at`; a read by id would walk every monthly partition or
  cost a third index on the hottest append table. The link `drawer=event&event=<chain_seq>` carries the sequence —
  tenant-unique, immutable, what a verification cites; GET /audit-events gains `chain_seq`, not `id`; revision 0101
  stays the two contract indexes. Screen side: lane F-ADM-WEB with AUD-ACTOR-BIND-1.
  (d) *APR-RECOVERY-1 (lane OPS's design note; R-73) — accepted as designed, both modes.* One access approver left:
  the operator's command invites the person and submits an ordinary ROLE_ASSIGNMENT request prepared by the
  OPERATOR, which the remaining approver decides. None left: the command grants the role without a request, under
  four conditions — the audit event and the grant are one transaction that re-reads zero active approvers; the
  notification reaches every active holder of `audit.read`, `user.manage` or the Controller role; the assignment is
  shown and counted as "Recovery grant (<reference>)", never as an approved grant; invitation and second factor are
  not shortened. 04 T-PLT-10 states the one exception with its one writer and an architecture test holds it. Who
  the customer's requester must be and the second operator's check are procedure, written in the runbook and put to
  Ray with the hosted limits. Built after lane SECFIX-APR's Parts 1 to 3; Alembic 0109.
  (e) *R-38 (iii), an API client's scopes as an access grant (lane SECFIX-APR's pre-build note) — way A.* POST
  /api-clients creates the row in status PENDING_APPROVAL with a secret nobody knows and submits a ROLE_ASSIGNMENT
  request whose proposal is the grant; approved, the client is ACTIVE and its first secret is issued by
  rotate-secret under step-up to a holder of `api_client.manage` (the approver never sees a secret; only the
  AUTO-BOOTSTRAP case returns it at creation); rejected, withdrawn or voided it is REJECTED. E-103 gains the two
  literals (Alembic 0110). The twelve test worlds that meet a second approver gain the approval step as STALE TEST
  WORLD. Screen states: lane F-ADM-WEB.
  (f) *Step 1: five criteria are recorded and one decides (browser findings Q-52, Q-53; item STEP1-CRITERIA-1).* 03
  REQ-CON-003 asks that a Step 1 record capture criteria 606-10-25-1 (a) to (e) with reviewer sign-off. As built
  the five answers are a convention of the review form inside the free-form `questionnaire`; the API accepts and
  the gate honours a record without them (the demo seed's records have none: every seeded contract reads "Not
  recorded" for (a) to (c), with the collectibility record listed as their evidence); and the form's outcome
  follows (e) alone — a "No" on (a), (b), (c) or (d) is stored and changes nothing, so a contract activates on a
  review that says its parties have not approved it. The engine models (d), (e) and 25-4 (ENGINE_SPEC Table 2.2-A).
  *Rule:* (1) a judgement record that serves Step 1 carries `questionnaire.criteria` with the keys a to e, each YES
  or NO, required when it is submitted for review (04 T-CON-19 states the shape); (2) inside the record (e) agrees
  with the topic (YES with COLLECTIBILITY, NO with NOT_A_CONTRACT) and (d) with the contract's
  `has_commercial_substance`, each refused by name; (3) a record that answers NO to (a), (b) or (c) serves no
  probable or criteria-met assessment and no activation — the checklist item STEP1_RECORD fails by name (PRD
  IMP-135) and the contract stays a draft; (4) the screens say so before the review is submitted and show a record
  as evidence only for the criteria it answers (lane WEB-QA); (5) the demo seed's Step 1 records carry the five
  answers (lane QA-BE). Owner of (1) to (3): lane SECFIX-ACT, with its R-102 slice. *Stated limit, candidate AD-59
  for the accountant:* eRev models the not-a-contract accounting of 606-10-25-7 for (d), (e) and 25-4; a contract
  that fails (a), (b) or (c) is not booked, and consideration received before it exists is outside the subledger.
  (g) *Figures at the cut (browser finding Q-55; item CTR-TODATE-AWAITING-1, lane QA-BE).* A USAGE obligation
  activated nine open months back read "Scheduled (59,616.44)" beside "Awaiting trigger 79,780.82": the to-date
  move takes the revenue since the version date out of `scheduled` only, and this obligation's remainder is all
  "awaiting trigger". PRODUCT DEFECT of B-2. Rule: at a cut, allocated = revenue + scheduled + awaiting trigger and
  none is negative; the movement leaves the bucket it came from.
  (h) *Close screens (lane F-CLO-WEB's report of CLO-25 and item 1b; judgements J1 to J25 and K1 to K9 confirmed).*
  The LEGACY book's cockpit shows no action bar and one line naming the book it follows, bound to a member
  `close_follows_book` on API-S-Period (lane FIX-D2, with CLO-LOCK-LEGACY-1). The estimate-versus-error judgement
  of J-14.1 gets a surface for the Revenue Accountant (CLO-JDG-ESTERR-UI-1). A trial balance is uploaded by a
  holder of `import.upload`, as the file store has it (a purpose of its own under `recon.prepare` is noted, not
  promised). The multi-entity view starts one close run per entity; no multi-entity route in 1.0. *Stated limit for
  the scope decision:* no screen of 1.0 creates or edits a manual adjustment (CLO-12 is API only by the build
  spec).
  (i) *Integration practice.* A lane whose session cannot take main reports with a keep-list and is integrated by
  the supervisor in a fresh worktree, which becomes the lane's next tree (SECFIX-ACT's residuals, sprint/w7
  681d3709, in w11). Main is held for lane SECFIX-APR's Parts 1 to 3 in `approvals/`, `db/session.py`,
  `domain/close/gates.py` and `journals/commands.py`; the integrations and slices that touch them queue behind it
  (SECFIX-ACT's residuals, QA-BE's R-95 slice — with ONE implementation of "SYSTEM under the tenant's scope" at
  that merge —, SECFIX-IMP's residuals, API-GAPS G-4). A teammate's own background waiter does not re-open its
  turn: the supervisor watches the gate slots and wakes a lane when its run ends.

- **R-114 (2026-09-30 20:50; rulings given by message between 20:20 and 20:55 on 2026-09-30 or with this entry; CLO-20 on
  main at b0084d88. Refines R-108, R-112 (a), (e), (g) and R-113 (g).)**
  (a) *The close run's refusal counts net amounts (lane F-CLO-B; refines R-112 (a)).* Measured by the lane: with
  the R-11 gap — the engine does not read a loss unit's posted provision back under its own key and emits it again
  with the reversal of the posted one — a later period's run was refused naming the earlier period, and no rerun
  cleared it: a workspace with a loss contract could not close. *Rule:* "an earlier period has period-end amounts
  no close run has posted" means that the pass's TIME intents for that period do not net to zero in some account
  role and currency (transaction and functional); a set that nets to zero in every role is no unposted period end.
  A run still seals only its own period. Revision 0082 (R-11) stays blocked on a permission that is Ray's to lift;
  the test that pins its churn is red on main by its own statement
  (`test_a_second_release_of_a_loss_unit_posts_nothing`).
  (b) *The lock requires the period's close run (item CLO-GATE-RUN-1, lane F-CLO-B).* The lane found that no gate
  of the lock asks for a close run: a period could be locked with its remeasurement, loss provision and reclass
  unposted, and since R-112 (a) the next period's run no longer posts them into it. *Rule:* a fourteenth gate — the
  latest close run of the entity, book and period is SUCCEEDED and no contract group of the entity changed since —
  evaluated at the lock request and at the decision like every gate, never waivable, listed in the lock's
  certification. Test worlds that lock through the product get the gate as fixture state in the shared helpers, as
  the reviewed reconciliations are; the lane's own witnesses run the run. Built now; merged after lane SECFIX-APR
  (it touches `domain/close/gates.py`).
  (c) *Row-level security and index conditions (lane API-GAPS; confirmed by the supervisor on PostgreSQL 17).* A
  caller's condition becomes an index condition on a table with a policy only when every function in it is
  LEAKPROOF. `texteq`, `uuid_eq`, `int8eq`, `date_eq`, `timestamptz_eq` and the boolean comparisons are; the jsonb
  operators (`->>`, `@>`), `enum_eq`, `numeric_eq`, `array_eq` and LIKE are not, and marking a function leakproof
  needs a superuser the product does not have. Consequences: (1) the two expression indexes of R-108 (a) cannot
  serve the application role — *K4:* a link table `audit_event_contract` (tenant, contract, chain sequence,
  occurred at, event id; T-PLT-48; RLS-T; not partitioned) written by the chain's own append in the event's
  transaction, read by a uuid equality; 04 says the event is the evidence and the link its index; a pg test holds
  "link rows = the keys of the events" and an EXPLAIN test the index condition under erev_app; Alembic 0101 holds
  the table and no index on the audit table. (2) Twenty-six indexes of policy tables have an enum as their second
  column and are entered by the tenant prefix alone (the approval inbox by status, jobs by kind, contracts by
  status, events by type, the rate lookup): item G-7, a design note by lane API-GAPS — per index a partial index
  per hot value (predicate proof needs no leakproof operator), a leakproof column ahead of the enum, or "leave"
  with its reason; what a `q=` search can do under the policy, before the search backend (G-5) is designed.
  Correctness is not affected; the cost grows with a tenant's rows. Put to Ray with the scope decision as a design
  fact of the tenant-isolation model.
  (d) *The LEGACY book follows its primary (lane FIX-D2's pre-build line; refines R-112 (e)).* The periods read
  gains `follows` — null for a posting book; for a LEGACY row `{book_code, state}` of the tenant's primary book for
  the entity and period — and the row keeps its own state. One guard body changes (a journal line is never of the
  LEGACY book): for a LEGACY subledger line the guard reads the primary's state row FOR SHARE first. The four
  commands refuse 409 `invalid-transition`, rule LEGACY_FOLLOWS_PRIMARY (PRD ERR-76); cancel-close stays allowed.
  The planner hands the engine the primary's state where the primary's is not postable and the LEGACY row's is.
  LEGACY follows the primary book alone: a lock of a second posting book does not cover its lines. The cockpit and
  checklist reads of a LEGACY state are left in this slice; a later item makes them answer `follows` and an empty
  checklist.
  (e) *The job that re-marks a period a lock opens (lane FIX-D2; report accepted).* The failing gate carries count
  null; a CANCELLED newest job is deferred again by the tick, so a cancel cannot skip the re-marking; `is_waivable`
  is false for that reason; three attempts. The job's audit facts name its period (`period_state` joins T-PLT-27's
  subject types, in revision 0098). Its 04 and dev-guide rows take new numbers (the old ones hold merged text).
