  (f) *Figures at the cut: the bucket follows the obligation's pattern (lane QA-BE; refines R-113 (g)).* Measured
  on the corpus (252 keys, 1,092 obligation-checkpoints): today's overlay is negative in one; the supervisor's
  "schedule lines after the cut" form in 62, because the schedule holds later lines the engine does not call
  scheduled; rule B — the movement since the version date leaves `scheduled` for a deterministic pattern
  (ENGINE_SPEC S09-R-45: TIME_ELAPSED, not held) and `awaiting_trigger` for every other — never. Rule B is built,
  with the invariant as a corpus property. Open, to be ruled on the lane's next measurement: the revenue waterfall
  family adds the version's awaiting-trigger amount at the version date to revenue through the as-of (ENGINE_SPEC_B
  S15-R-01). Open, a reading by lane ENG-FX: whether the time-elapsed fixed component of a USAGE obligation is
  "awaiting a trigger" at all (ENG-USAGE-FIXED-SCHEDULE-1; candidate for the accountant).
  (g) *The reader-independent computation (lane QA-BE's R-95 slice; report accepted, lands after lane SECFIX-APR).*
  Judgements confirmed: the architecture door lists every builder of a SYSTEM principal; the writers carry a
  runtime guard; a wrapped database error is classified like a bare one. At its merge ONE implementation of "SYSTEM
  under the tenant's scope" remains: `system_entity_scope` as the primitive, `UnitOfWork.as_system()` alone also
  swapping principal and user. Findings placed: CTR-BALANCE-ROWS-1 — a combination group whose contracting entities
  have different functional currencies cannot be persisted (an approval answers 200 over a FAILED computation;
  PRODUCT DEFECT; lane QA-BE, now); the import scope still counts a line's performing entity (lane SECFIX-IMP);
  lane OPS's retry of CONTRACT_COMPUTE merges right behind the slice; an entity code's existence showing at the
  booking is accepted.
  (h) *Screens.* The approval view's flag labels are held to the API's flag constants by an architecture test (lane
  WEB-QA). The "More below" cue of a sticky footer becomes a rule of DESIGN_SYSTEM (rev 1.9) for every sticky
  footer. In a sequence of commands sent by one press a succeeded step keeps its idempotency key until the sequence
  completes, so a second press replays the stored answer and continues at the step that failed; a lint rule keeps
  the key header to one module (W-23). Request summaries that carry an enumeration literal or a period key are
  corrected with the approval content rows (APR-SUMMARY-LITERALS-1, lane API-GAPS). CTR-24's judgements J1 to J22
  are confirmed, among them that a Step 1 assessment anywhere on the stream closes the draft's edit form until
  R-102 (c) is on main; "New modification" is offered on ACTIVE contracts only; an estimate version cannot name its
  modification (MOD-LINKED-ESTIMATES-1). The report runs register opens for `report.run` or `audit.read` (RPS-18).
  (i) *The out-of-period register's row key (lane F-RPS-REG; RPT-OOP-ROWKEY-1; report accepted).* The key is
  `<origin period>:<posting period>:<event key>`; no conversion of the old form (no tenant is deployed): a relock
  of a period first locked under the old form lists each row as changed in the attribute `row_key` alone. The
  rollforward's refusals for a period without the lock it opens from carry rule T-RPT-01.
  (j) *Practice under scarce gate slots.* A lane's branch is merged on its gate A and its own targeted database
  runs; the supervisor runs the lane's gate B list on merged main (R-91) instead of waiting for a slot. Merged so
  tonight: CLO-PERMLOCK-PERM-1, CLO-20, the approval view's first part with the crawl, CTR-24.

- **R-115 (2026-09-30 21:39; rulings given by message between 20:55 and 21:40 on 2026-09-30 or with this entry; main
  33b48524. Refines R-72, R-98, R-113 (b) and (f).)**
  (a) *After a combination each member was stated twice (lane F-RPS-REG, found while measuring R-72's cases; item
  RPT-FORMER-GROUP-VERSIONS-1, PRODUCT DEFECT, release blocker).* A former single-contract group keeps its last
  ACTIVE version after its contract has joined a combined group, and the reports read the latest version of every
  group: the contract balances report shows two rows per member under one row key and fails its tie-out; the lock's
  CONTRACT_BALANCES and RPO datasets are refused for duplicate row keys, so a period cannot be locked; the RPO
  rollforward froze with doubled figures that its own tie does not see. *Rule:* a version counts for a member
  contract only while that contract is a member of the version's group at the run's cutoff; a bound run keeps its
  bound ids; an as-of run before the combination still reads the former group. In `tie_outs.latest_versions` and in
  every builder that selects versions itself. First in that lane's order.
  (b) *The contract balance rollforward (lane F-RPS-REG's D1 + D3, on main 45fbd738; candidate AD-60 for the
  accountant).* (1) A BILLING or CREDIT_MEMO line stands before the other lines of its date in ENGINE billing mode
  as a billing document does in ERP mode, so that both modes state the same lines for the same facts. (2) Where an
  opening baseline shows: LEGACY_MIGRATION — the baseline is the OPENING of the period that holds the first day
  after the cutover (the next period when the cutover is a period's last day; otherwise the cutover's own period,
  whose activity counts from the day after); BUSINESS_COMBINATION — the BUSINESS_COMBINATIONS line of the period
  that holds the acquisition date, contract by contract (ENGINE_SPEC_B S15-R-06). Before its baseline a contract
  states nothing: no row and no balance from the engine's reconstruction of earlier positions. (3) D2's rule that a
  run opens from the previous lock's frozen CONTRACT_BALANCES dataset is not weakened for test worlds: the shared
  fixture that closes earlier periods freezes that dataset for the last period it closes, with the product's own
  producer. (4) The refusal of a run for a period without the lock it opens from carries rule T-RPT-01.
  (c) *Entity-scoped grants (lane F-RPS-REG, SECFIX-SCOPE item 1; refines R-63 (e)).* The scope rule holds for
  every membership command of API-R-05, rename and resend-invitation included. A change of a role's DEFINITION is a
  tenant-wide act: the role-definition commands need `role.manage` for all entities (as tenant-wide lists do under
  R-28); an administrator of named entities is refused by name.
  (d) *Sandboxes (lane F-SNP; SNP-3 and SNP-4 on main 33b48524 — security finding SF-1 FIXED).* SNP-3's decisions
  are confirmed: an archived sandbox's membership stays readable with its status and the pickers offer ACTIVE
  workspaces only; the reset moves the requester's sessions in place and records it, without a cookie rotation; the
  reset writes in the source tenant what every load writes there. Two restrictions ruled in: a connection test in a
  sandbox is refused by the guard like every other outbound command (SBX-PROBE-1), and a notification raised in a
  sandbox is delivered in the app only — no email leaves a sandbox (SBX-EMAIL-1). *The snapshot cut of
  `import_upload.named_entity_ids` (refines R-98):* the set is a requirement — a reader and the decider must cover
  every element — so dropping a cut element would widen who may read the copy; on any cut the column is exported
  NULL, its fail-closed value, and no copy is refused for it.
  (e) *A PLATFORM registry version takes the instant form (item CFG-PLATFORM-PIN-1; refines R-113 (b)).* Measured
  by lane F-SNP: the snapshot retention policy is held to the first day of a period that has not started, so a
  workspace provisioned in the middle of a period cannot take a sandbox copy for up to a period's length. A
  retention policy is no accounting policy. *Rule:* a registry version of category PLATFORM is effective at its
  publication or at a later instant; the period-start rule stays for the accounting categories. Lane ENG-FX builds
  it with CFG-BACKDATE-1; the refusal a user meets says from when copies are possible (PRD ERR-77, lane F-SNP).
  (f) *STEP1-CRITERIA-1 as it is built (lane SECFIX-ACT's pre-build line; refines R-113 (f)).* The five answers are
  optional while a record is a draft and whole at submission; (e) is held to the topic and (d) to the contract's
  commercial-substance member at every write and again at submission; STEP1_RECORD fails for any enabled book whose
  latest assessment rests on a record that answers No to (a), (b) or (c), a not-probable one too; the record still
  serves the not-probable assessment and its gate. The seeds and the test helpers gain the answers in that lane's
  slice. The legacy Contract Setup composer writes none — the legacy system recorded no such answers and five
  invented ones would be a judgement nobody made — and its records serve as before. PRD IMP-135 prints the
  criterion's label, not a paragraph of one framework.
  (g) *The balance figures of a contract name their explanation (item EXPLAIN-BALANCE-LINKS-1, lane QA-BE; found by
  lane WEB-QA on the real stack).* The Explain triggers of contract liability, contract asset and unbilled
  receivable have never loaded: the route resolves a balance row's id and no response carries it. The balance
  entries gain `links.explain_*` with the period of the cut, and an API test holds that every explanation link a
  contract read sends resolves.
  (h) *Practice.* A frontend lane that adds a route with a path parameter brings the e2e crawl's records for it
  (the seeded record, the persona, the row of project `screens` that leaves it) in its own branch; the supervisor's
  check of a frontend merge lists the e2e tests, because the crawl refuses to start otherwise (lane F-CLO-WEB's
  head, held back for it). A gate context is kept no longer than the reading of its captures: the machine's disk
  fell from 58 to 28 GiB in a day; the supervisor removed the installed dependencies of three closed worktrees and
  the lanes delete read contexts. Docker's build cache (28 GB, 19.6 GB reclaimable) is on a shared daemon and is
  Ray's to prune.

- **R-116 (2026-09-30 22:50; rulings given by message between 21:40 and 22:50 on 2026-09-30 or with this entry; main
  350fbcfb. Refines R-98, R-109, R-112 (b) and (c), R-113 (g), R-114 (b), (c) and (f).)**
  (a) *The fixed, time-elapsed component of a USAGE obligation is scheduled (lane ENG-FX; item
  ENG-USAGE-FIXED-SCHEDULE-1; PRODUCT DEFECT of the engine's classification; candidate AD-61).* Measured on a key
  of the lane: a fixed minimum beyond the open periods is placed whole at the obligation's end date in the RPO
  bands (75,000.00 / 0.00 / 225,000.00 against 100,000.00 in each), its current part reads 0.00 of 225,000.00 and
  the waterfall is empty after the open periods; postings do not move. *Rule:* one predicate — a FIXED component
  measured by time elapsed that carries an allocation is deterministic (TIME_ELAPSED as today; USAGE; the guarantee
  of a right-to-access ROYALTY; not RIGHT_TO_INVOICE) — read at the schedule's `is_deterministic`, the projection
  to the term end and the current split. One NEW engine key witnesses it, its expected values derived independently
  and entered as a candidate for the accountant, not as an approved key; the corpus comparison shows no moved hash
  of an existing key. Whether K-08's quarterly minimums are a stand-ready fee or a minimum that usage draws down
  goes into the accountant's addendum.
  (b) *The rate of an open period (lane ENG-FX).* ENGINE_SPEC S12-R-01 stays: no rate, no posting, no engine
  fallback. FX-REPUBLISH-DIRTY-1 (PRODUCT DEFECT, release blocker for a multi-currency workspace): the approval of
  an FX rate set version marks dirty the groups it can move (trigger FX_REPUBLISH, which no code issues today), so
  a rate replaced after a computation reaches it before the lock; built after lane SECFIX-APR (the approval hook's
  file is held). FX-OPEN-PERIOD-RATES-1: a period's rates are published when it opens (provisional) and replaced
  before its lock — a stated procedure in the runbook and the user guide — and a period that opens for an entity
  with a foreign-currency contract and no rate of that period raises ONE data-quality WARNING per entity and
  period. Candidate for the accountant: whether a provisional rate replaced before the lock is acceptable practice
  as designed. JET-02's mechanism is left for 1.0.
  (c) *The report family states its figures at the as-of (lane QA-BE; item RPT-ASOF-FIGURES-1; release blocker;
  candidate AD-63; refines R-113 (g) and R-114 (f)).* Measured: the RPO a lock freezes (ENGINE_SPEC_B S15-R-08 as
  built: lines after the date plus the version's awaiting amount at the version date) reads 79,780.82 for a
  remainder of 20,164.38 on K-08, above the allocation at an earlier date, and the waterfall counts the periods
  between an as-of and the last posted period twice for every pattern. *Rule:* (1) the awaiting column of the
  waterfall family is the reader's figure at the end of the as-of period — rows, control totals, the cell's
  explanation, the dashboard panel, the frozen dataset; (2) the RPO at a date is the remainder at that date, the
  reader's scheduled plus awaiting, banded as today; (3) at an as-of before the last posted period, recognised is
  the posted lines of periods up to the as-of period and scheduled is the lines after it of obligations whose
  remainder rule B calls scheduled, so that the total equals the allocation at every as-of — a corpus property over
  all obligation-checkpoints; (4) `latest_contract_status` states one date. Built after lane F-RPS-REG's
  RPT-FORMER-GROUP-VERSIONS-1 (same builders).
  (d) *The SSP version an obligation was priced with is read back (lane ENG-FX; item PIN-READBACK-1, SSP half, with
  PIN-K-COMBINATION-1; PRODUCT DEFECT, release blocker).* Measured on an ACTIVE contract with 35,718.53 of revenue
  posted: a later version of the same SSP book dated in the past, approved today, re-allocates the contract at its
  next computation and posts +1,335.34 over seven open periods; a new, better-scoped book with a first version
  dated in the past does the same to the cent with ONE approver and no flag; in both the approver is shown no
  contract and no figure. Four more paths lead there (a version filling a gap, an equal-scope book with a smaller
  code, a rename, a BY_LABEL version). Cause: every computation selects book and version by date and scope again;
  the version recorded on the obligation is never read. *Rule:* the bundle builder hands the engine, for every
  obligation that already has a version in the book of account, the recorded version as its named version (POLICIES
  POL-070 pin K at level O; ENGINE_SPEC S05-R-03) — the standard's own rule that the transaction price is not
  reallocated for later changes in standalone selling prices (ASC 606-10-32-43; IFRS 15.88). No publication refusal
  is added; past effective dates keep their use for obligations not yet priced. The merge is held to five points:
  the pinned obligation states its basis and a replay rebuilds the same bundle; an approved POL-070 override and a
  line change that names a version win over the pin; the pin follows the obligation through a combination; POL-080
  D18_DEFAULT as written; no figure of an answer key, the corpus or the demo seed moves. Stated limit: a contract
  whose inception date is corrected keeps its recorded version until an override names another.
  (e) *The lock's fourteenth gate measures unposted period ends on the group (lane F-CLO-B; item CLO-GATE-RUN-1;
  refines R-114 (b)).* A digest of head computations was refused as the measure: it moves with every computation,
  also one that consumed only a later period's fact, so the gate — never waivable — would fail between the run and
  the second person's decision in any workspace that keeps recording. The ordinary compute path does not see TIME
  intents (the engine emits a period-end amount only under a pass). *Rule, as the lane proposed:*
  `combination_group.period_ends_open`, one entry per entity and book, states the date before which that scope's
  period-end amounts are posted; a close run's first period-end step sets it for every group it passes to the day
  after the period's last day; a computation evaluates it only inside a window — a close run of one of the group's
  entities has a SUCCEEDED first period-end step for a period that is still postable — by the three passes run dry
  over its own bundle, lowering the entry to the first day of the earliest period whose TIME intents do not net to
  zero (R-114 (a)); the gate for an entity, book and period is: the latest run SUCCEEDED and no computed group that
  is not dirty holds an entry on or before the period's last day, the count being those groups. A dirty group
  belongs to NO_DIRTY_GROUPS and its waiver. Sequence 13, the certification moves to 14 in provisioning's list;
  seeded by provisioning only (no workspace is deployed; a system gate added after a first deployment needs a
  seeding step — known limitation); failure texts "Close run not completed", "Close run <no> is <status>", "Close
  run out of date, run it again: <n> contracts". A sandbox replay of a source lock lays PASSED "as certified at the
  source lock" over this gate only and records where the result came from; a source lock without the result leaves
  the period blocked. Held to four more witnesses: the reopen order, a computation that posts an EVENT line into
  the period under close, the engine run count inside and outside a window, the snapshot's class of the column.
  (f) *Index conditions under the policy: the remedy; search (lane API-GAPS's design note; refines R-114 (c)).* The
  count is 57 indexes of 331 on policy tables, not 26: 56 with an enum in the key, one numeric expression. Measured
  at 200,000 rows a tenant: live jobs 16.5 ms against 0.08 ms with a partial index; a source record by its identity
  25.6 ms against 0.07 ms with the key reordered — once per ingested record, so ingestion is quadratic in a
  tenant's rows, as is the replay check of every posting. *Rule:* item PERF-RLS-INDEX-1 (release item, lane
  API-GAPS, Alembic 0112): fifteen keys reordered with the enum last, six status indexes replaced by five partial
  and three plain ones, index definitions only; a catalogue test with a closed list (the 23 bounded cases with
  their reasons, and the per-entity and per-contract cases left for a table's next revision); a plan test read as
  the application role per hot read; a dev-guide rule (an index a read depends on binds only columns compared with
  leakproof operators up to the last column the read names; enum and numeric columns last or as a partial index's
  predicate; jsonb and array content written a second time as columns; a prefix search by `starts_with` on a
  C-collated key). The list kernel leaves NULLS LAST out of the ORDER BY for a NOT NULL sort key (every descending
  list sorted its whole filtered set). *Search:* 04 §16.13 changes now — a term matches the beginning of a word of
  the primary or secondary text, an exact primary first — because a substring can never be an index condition under
  the policy and matching must not change after a release; 1.0 implements that sentence by a scan (minimum term
  length 2, a limit per scope, the request's statement timeout); the key table that serves it by index is item
  SEARCH-KEYS-1, put to Ray with the performance phase. The 21 lists keep `q` as a substring filter.
  (g) *Imports (lane SECFIX-IMP's residuals report; on main as 350fbcfb; refines R-98 and R-109).* Confirmed as
  built: a refused row with an unreadable key refuses no other row; an API client's upload stored without its
  scopes holds nothing; a failed plan states one unevaluated approval for every subject its template can perform;
  submit and cancel answer 404 outside the caller's read scope; a tenant-level template does not require its write
  permission of the uploader (04 §16.6; the approval is the control). *Ruled the other way, for the lane's next
  slice:* in quarantine mode EVERY object a file builds from several rows loads whole or not at all — also the
  DRAFT version an ssp_values file builds, the mapping version of an account_mapping file and a bundle with its
  components — because the reviewer of a DRAFT sees what the version holds, not what the file meant to put there.
  Accepted as a stated property: the ssp_values scope is the one frozen at validation.
  (h) *The lock decision (lane FIX-D2's item 6).* The `future` next period is taken FOR UPDATE NOWAIT before the
  gates; a held row answers 409 `lock-conflict`, rule NEXT_PERIOD_HELD (PRD ERR-78). One SYSTEM reader per decision
  (peak of two connections). PRODUCT DEFECT of the two-connection form: the decision's own connection idles in its
  transaction while a dataset is produced, and a dataset over 60 s ends it while it holds the period's row — both
  transactions of a lock decision set `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` for themselves, a setting with a
  default of 15 minutes. Known limitation LOCK-DECISION-SYNC-1: the datasets are produced inside the deciding
  request. `assert_own_fresh_basis` is called under the row lock in the lock and reopen hooks; a pg test and a lint
  rule hold the period guard's trigger on every partition. The re-marking job's `job.finish` keeps its payload.
  (i) *Journal export (lane F-CLO-A; CLO-15 with JE-CSV-FUNCTIONAL-1 and JRN-DISPATCH-RECOUNT-1, and
  ADJ-VOID-REFUSE-1: reports accepted).* The download's render path recounts like the dispatch and refuses a batch
  whose lines differ from what was approved (409 `invalid-transition`, rule BATCH_RECOUNT, PRD ERR-79).
  JRN-FAILED-CANCEL-1's readings confirmed: the JOURNAL_EXPORT job with a mode; `POST
  /journal-batches/{id}/hand-over` with `journal.export`; a handed-over batch is `exported` and unacknowledged
  until its ledger document is recorded; ERP batches only; E-34 gains `failed` to `cancelled`. An attachment
  another holder adds to a draft adjustment is not an edit of it. For the accountant, stated and not decided: under
  ERP billing the contract liability account holds entries in two currencies whose functional sum is right, and
  eRev's JET-10 is the only remeasurement of them.
  (j) *Screens.* The Scheduled list of the contract workbench shows two amounts per obligation, recognised to date
  and scheduled at the cut, so that its rows add up to its header. The Schedules, Billing documents and Journals
  tabs are registers of what is recorded to date; a tab that holds a row dated after the measured period carries
  one caption saying so. Under "All entities" a period filter stays and its editor says "Choose an entity in the
  context bar to filter by period." An approval's subject label for a product's policy or principal-agent change is
  "Product change". Items: EXPLAIN-CUT-MEASURES-1 (lane QA-BE: the explain service answers an obligation's
  scheduled and awaiting amounts at a cut by rule B; until then the pane opens the recognised amount at the
  period); CTR-FUNCTIONAL-VIEW-1 (known limitation: no Transaction | Functional switch in 1.0; put to Ray with the
  scope); SBX-RETENTION-UI-1 (lane F-ADM-WEB: no screen sets or confirms the snapshot retention policy, so a
  workspace run from its screens can take no copy); MOD-QUESTIONNAIRE-GUARD-1 (lane SECFIX-ACT: the first
  classification stored as the chosen treatment leaves a departure nobody chose); W-26 (the crawl opens every
  Explain trigger of a KPI strip). A balance link's merge and the screen's binding go to main together.
  (k) *Demo and practice.* DEMO-CLOCK-1: SCH-05 opens FY2026-P10 on 1 October in every running stack while the
  demo's rates end on 30 September — the seed carries rates through the month the wall clock is in, or the demo
  does not auto-open; DEMO-FX-CONTRACT-1: one ACTIVE demo contract in a currency other than its entity's functional
  currency. A lane whose session cannot take main is integrated by the supervisor on main by the lane's keep-list
  (SECFIX-IMP's residuals), and its next tree is cut from the merged head. A frontend lane's e2e row is run on main
  after its merge; the first such run of WEB-16's bulk row was red for a product defect (a column header cut at the
  capture's width) that no run of the lane had reached, fixed in the lane's next head.

- **R-117 (2026-09-30 23:43; rulings given by message between 22:50 and 23:45 on 2026-09-30 or with this entry; main
  9b5e1e4c. Refines R-106 (a), R-112 (c), R-113 (b), R-115 (a) and (e).)**
  (a) *Reading a contract after a combination, as built (lane F-RPS-REG; RPT-FORMER-GROUP-VERSIONS-1 on main as
  92e65393; refines R-115 (a)).* Confirmed: a read "at a date" goes along the contract's chain of groups — the
  versions recorded during each membership, in membership order — because with membership at the cutoff alone a
  contract booked in January and combined in September would have no version at 31 August, its opening for
  September would read 0 against a locked August closing and the whole re-allocated price would read as new
  contracts; read along the chain the opening is the former group's closing and the combination is a movement under
  MODIFICATIONS that sums to zero. A row without any membership is read as recorded. The contract history numbers a
  contract's versions along its chain in record order (two rows shared one key and the Version column read 1, 1).
  *Item RPT-FORMER-GROUP-READERS-1 (PRODUCT DEFECTS of the same cause, lane F-RPS-REG):* `GET /schedule-lines`
  answers the former group's lines beside the combined group's (measured: 3,202.55 and 2,956.20 for one contract
  and period, the cost line twice), and the close monitors' current version keeps a former group's last version for
  ever (by the code; measured first).
  (b) *A registry version holds its category's whole value set, made whole by the server (lane F-SNP, measured;
  item REG-VERSION-WHOLE-SET-1; PRODUCT DEFECT, release blocker; refines R-113 (b) and R-115 (e)).* Measured
  through the product's commands: a version stores the values sent, publication supersedes the earlier version of
  the category without merging, and resolution reads only the newest version in force — so a later version that
  carries one parameter returns every other parameter of its category to the default (PRACTICAL_EXPEDIENT:
  `costs.obtain_expedient` DO_NOT_APPLY from 1 October reads APPLY again from 1 November, when a version carrying
  only `sfc.one_year_expedient` takes effect; a confirmed snapshot retention policy is unset by the next platform
  version). *Rule:* at submit the server stores the values of the version in force at that scope immediately before
  the draft's effective instant, overlaid with the draft's values, less the keys the draft names in `unset`; a
  draft may state `basis: DEFAULTS` instead (the values sent are the whole set); the request shows the whole set
  and the difference to the predecessor — changed, added and returned to default by name — and the content hash
  covers the whole set; the approval checks under its lock that the predecessor is the one of the submission, else
  the request is void as stale; effective instants are monotonic per category and scope; an ENTITY version states
  the keys overridden at that level and passes the others to the TENANT level as today; resolution does not change.
  CFG-PLATFORM-PIN-1 moves to this lane and item: a PLATFORM-category version takes effect at its publication when
  `effective_from` is null, or at a later instant, never earlier, without the period-start rule — with it the
  workspace settings screen, which sends no `effective_from`, can submit a value at all (today 422). Lane ENG-FX
  builds CFG-BACKDATE-1 for the accounting categories, the templates and the rule sets on top.
  (c) *Post-reopen lines (lane SECFIX-CLO; item 2 on main as 9b5e1e4c; item REOPEN-CLOSING-FLAG-1, PRODUCT DEFECT,
  a control point).* `is_post_reopen` is stored from the state literal (`reopened`), so a line posted while a
  reopened period is in soft close again — where the close run posts its period-end entries and adjustments are
  submitted — is stored false, and the re-lock's difference and the post-reopen registers miss it. *Rule:* the flag
  means "posted into a period that was locked once and is not locked now"; both period guards and the posting
  commands' predicate read the period's current lock record being a REOPEN. Lane FIX-D2's seal test, which pins
  false for that case, is a stale expectation under this rule. Accepted as a stated property: the table still
  admits closing to open under a REOPEN record, because a replayed history must be able to hold what its source
  recorded. The NULL reason that passes two transition checks although 04 says "Required" is tightened in the same
  revision if the fixture sweep is small.
  (d) *Journal export.* The JOURNAL_EXPORT job in its cancel mode that finds the ledger holding the batch writes
  the duplicate's receipt, acknowledges the batch and ends SUCCEEDED_WITH_EXCEPTIONS with the refusal in its result
  and a DENIED audit event; only an unreachable ledger is raised and retried; the job's result states `outcome`
  (CANCELLED or NOT_CANCELLED; HANDED_OVER or NOT_HANDED_OVER) so that a screen can tell the two apart. No new job
  kind (refines R-112 (c)). CTL-025's test is green with CLO-15 on main.
  (e) *The audit log's contract key (lane API-GAPS; on main as 0f8c0c5e).* The lane's seventeen judgements are
  confirmed, among them: three closed lists classify every audited type; an event of a contract-scoped object
  without a contract is refused when it is built, a DENIED event excepted; reads by contract and by chain sequence
  take no default start; the trail's statement reads the link's rows and looks each event up LATERAL, because a
  plain join was not driven by the link; `actor_membership_id` is on the event for every holder of `audit.read`.
  Owed with the approval content rows (G-3 / G-4): the key on approval requests and decisions, and the witness of a
  request rejected and resubmitted.
  (f) *Imports inside an import cycle take the module, not the name (repair of main in merge 0f8c0c5e).*
  `reference/period_redirty_job.py` bound `newest_of_subject` by name from `platform.jobs`, which reaches it again
  through `approval_queries` and `close.gates`: importing `platform.jobs` first failed, the application's own
  import order hid it, and a new unit module that entered the cycle there failed at collection on the merged tree.
  The module is now imported as `close.gates` imports it and the function read at call time. Owed: an architecture
  test that refuses a name import between two modules of one import cycle (item ARCH-IMPORT-CYCLE-1, owner to be
  named with lane OPS's next report).
  (g) *Test worlds and gates.* `test_sc6_a_frozen_dataset_of_a_period_lock_is_not_shredded` locks two periods in a
  row without running the re-marking job the first lock defers: a stale test world under R-106 (a), repaired by its
  module's owner (lane SECFIX-IMP) as a commit of its own; the shared helper is the place if more worlds do the
  same. The platform answer-key runner's world gives its approver no active membership (EREV-TRN-001 at an approval
  decision), which blocks the EX21 key before any report is built: item AK-PLATFORM-RUNNER-1 (lane QA-BE), a stale
  world of the runner; the five platform keys stay "not run" in the whole-suite shard until then. e2e on main
  303f2f47: RPS-18's new row addressed a column header by a prefix that begins another header's name (lane
  F-ADM-WEB; the first run of that row was on main); one visit of the crawl never settled on a period read the
  server had answered (lane WEB-QA). `GET /periods` on the e2e stack has a median of 1.2 s over 1,211 reads on a
  loaded host; its two period-state keys are in PERF-RLS-INDEX-1.
  (h) *Practice.* A lane that can merge main does so itself before its report, with the supervisor's notes on what
  the other side of each conflict is (lane SECFIX-APR: seventeen files against tonight's main; notes with lane
  SECFIX-IMP's keep-list in the supervisor's working files). A revision's unit test must not pin its
  `down_revision` (it moves at every merge, R-68 (e)); the head pin of the migrations test is the one place.

- **R-118 (2026-10-01 00:57; rulings given by message between 23:45 on 2026-09-30 and 00:55 on 2026-10-01 or with this
  entry; main 55291038. Corrects one sentence of R-102 (b) and one of R-117 (c); refines R-98, R-111, R-112 (d),
  R-114 (f), R-115 (g), R-116 (f) and (j), R-117 (c).)**
  (a) *Figures at a cut: a movement forward takes from scheduled first (lane QA-BE; rule B on main as 878ff228;
  refines R-114 (f)).* For a movement FORWARD from the version date the reader takes the revenue since that date
  from the scheduled amount first and then from the awaiting-trigger amount, with no predicate: between the version
  date and the cut nothing but time passes, and what time alone recognises is what ENGINE_SPEC S09-R-45 calls
  scheduled. The obligation's pattern decides a look-back only (revenue returns to the part it came from). On
  today's engine this is rule B in every case; after ENG-USAGE-FIXED-SCHEDULE-1 (R-116 (a)) it follows the engine's
  one predicate without repeating it. The answer-key runner's own overlay keeps its rule: it is the oracle's side
  and no leaf tells the two apart.
  (b) *Correction of R-102 (b)'s sentence for the accountant (found by lane ACCT against the code; candidate
  AD-56).* The log said that an ACTIVE book goes on recognising until the reviewed not-probable assessment is
  recorded. The code on main is the fact: the SUBMISSION of a judgement record that names an ACTIVE contract places
  a SYSTEM recognition hold unless the record is auto-approved (REQ-POL-010); recognition therefore continues only
  between the flag and the submission; the review releases the hold and catches up; the not-probable assessment
  recorded next takes the book out, from a date that may lie back at the flag. The RESUME record's sentence on
  AD-57 was wrong as well and is corrected there: revenue released from a deposit under 606-10-25-7 beside the
  contract header's recognised figure is a question put to a lane's reading (R-106 (c)), not a ruling.
  (c) *Post-reopen lines, as measured (lane SECFIX-CLO; corrects R-117 (c)).* No line is missing from a re-lock's
  difference or from a register: the difference compares datasets by row key and no register selects by the flag.
  Two attributes are stated wrong for a line posted while a reopened period is in soft close again — the line's
  `is_post_reopen` and the journal entry's `is_post_close` (live, and in the JE population a lock freezes). The
  rule of R-117 (c) stands; ONE guard body changes (the subledger line's; a journal line has no such column),
  reading the lock record by a second statement only when the period has one (+21 to 23 µs a line there, nothing
  for a period never locked); for a LEGACY line the flag is "its own row's or the primary row's current lock record
  is a REOPEN" (revision 0113 on lane FIX-D2's 0105). Both transition checks that let a NULL reason pass are
  tightened in that revision.
  (d) *Postings into a restricted period need a second person (item CLO-REOPEN-APPROVAL-1; PRODUCT DEFECT, a
  control; release blocker; lane SECFIX-CLO; found by lanes SECFIX-CLO and FIX-D2 independently).* PRD BR-CLS-06 (a
  reopened period) and BR-CLS-04 (a period in soft close) hold for imports and manual adjustments only: a manual
  event is appended and computed in a reopened period by one holder of the record permission; the submission of a
  journal run, a void request, a modification and an estimate version allow auto-approval there; the posting
  guard's predicate has no caller; BUILD_SPEC CLO-7's acceptance test was never written. A pre-build line comes
  first — every command that can post a line into such a period, measured through the product, and the smallest
  change for each; built after lane SECFIX-APR's Parts 1 to 3.
  (e) *Modifications and estimate versions (lane SECFIX-ACT).* A refusal's date is printed DD MMM YYYY, and the
  Step 1 family reads "This assessment is dated before the contract's latest Step 1 entry in <book label> (<date>).
  Date it on or after that date."; BEFORE_ACTIVATION is removed (the other sentence refuses all it refused).
  COPY-DATES-1: every date and instant in user-facing copy, as a pass of its own with a list of every sentence and
  pin (52 sites by an AST scan: 28 dates in messages, the six of the Step 1 family among them, 10 instants, 14 that
  are no copy). MOD-DISCARD-1: `POST /modifications/{id}/discard` takes any DRAFT without a pending request, also
  one that was submitted before; no reason; it refuses while a review of one of the modification's own judgement
  records is pending, and while a linked estimate version is SUBMITTED; linked versions that are DRAFT, REJECTED or
  WITHDRAWN go with the modification (their `submit` then answers 409 naming it). MOD-LINKED-ESTIMATES-1:
  `estimate_version.modification_id` set at creation and never changed; the modification's approval refuses by name
  while a linked version is not approved — one decision never stands for several subjects; for the accountant,
  stated and not decided: the link orders two approvals and moves no figure, and whether an estimate change made
  inside a modification must take effect only with the modification is the engine's event order. EST-DISCARD-1: a
  DRAFT estimate version gains a discard (PRD SM-04 gave it one exit; SCREENS §8.4 offered a command no route
  served).
  (f) *A data-quality item is settled only when no book of the entity finds it (lane SECFIX-CLO's item 3).* A
  monitor run settles an open item of its entity and period only when no evaluated book of the entity finds its
  key, settles nothing of a period while another book's row for it is closed or permanently locked, and a `future`
  row does not count; between the locks of two books a finding that is fixed still needs a waiver.
  (g) *Explanations of figures at a cut (lanes QA-BE and WEB-QA; the links on main as 878ff228, their binding as
  55291038).* The screen follows `links.explain_<measure>` as sent and composes no address; a figure whose entry
  names no explanation is printed without a trigger. EXPLAIN-BALANCE-LINKS-2: the balances read names an address
  for every balance member it returns (ten more), null where no node holds it; an obligation's links are null at a
  cut where nothing is measured for it (today they answer the version-date figure). EXPLAIN-CUT-MEASURES-1 covers
  the contract's scheduled and awaiting-trigger amounts as well as the obligation's (the awaiting cell is at the
  cut since rule B and its explanation is not); until it lands the two cells open a list level by obligation.
  (h) *The LEGACY book follows its primary, as built (lane FIX-D2; on main as 93e02513).* Confirmed: a LEGACY row
  without a row of the primary book is not postable; `follows` is optional in the schema and always sent; the
  refusal comes after the permission check and If-Match; a request created before the slice is decided by its hook.
  PER-LEGACY-POSTING-PERIOD-1: the platform's posting-period helper and the import predicate answer for the LEGACY
  book by the same rule.
  (i) *Index conditions (lane API-GAPS; refines R-116 (f)).* The catalogue test's closed list is the 22 indexes its
  own rule finds — a usable column behind one the policy cannot use, or nothing but the tenant in front of such a
  column, or an expression or non-b-tree index — not a count of exceptions; the outbox claim's index stays partial
  on the three statuses the claim can take; the source record's order key puts the version order in front of the
  two enumerations.
  (j) *Platform security (lane SECFIX-PLT's three, on main as 757a8e10; its R-111 slice).* Confirmed: a command
  that reads a stored file answers one the caller may not read as a missing one; an import's source and diff are
  read by its uploader and by holders of `contract.read` or `import.approve` for every entity it names; the
  requester attaches to, and uploads for, a request of theirs while it is PENDING — and voids an attachment they
  attached; a link's token is derived under the platform key, named by the outbox row and stored nowhere; a
  password change supersedes open reset links; the sign-in surface is counted per address and an email without an
  account is locked answer for answer as an account is. For the slice: the SoD engine counts permissions held
  through a delegation in force; FILE-DEDUPE-READER-1 — an uploads table (T-PLT-49) records each uploader with
  their own file name and time, and a caller who reads a file only as an uploader is answered with their own;
  accepted as a stated property: the id is the stored row's and time-ordered, so known bytes uploaded again tell
  that they were stored before in the workspace and roughly when, never by whom. Known limitation
  RATE-LIMIT-PER-PROCESS-1: the limits are per API process.
  (k) *The kernel's error mapping, as built (lane OPS; on main as 2f97b1ac; refines R-112 (d)).* The period tick
  isolates every exception per period and per re-deferral outside the 503 set, at error level, as R-50 (a) does for
  the fan-out. Transient states outside the 503 set and 57014 stay 500 with a stack trace. JRN-DISPATCH-DEAD-1
  (lane F-CLO-A): a journal dispatch that dies of a database error at its last attempt leaves a DEAD message beside
  a batch that is not `failed`, and `last_error` names the exception's class where the slug would name something.
  (l) *Screens.* The audit screens bind the new reads (AUD-SCREEN-BIND-1): a member that is null before and after
  is no change; the actor filter's options are the actors of the range on screen. The Home's recent activity uses
  the event's object label and a verb for the frequent actions. A context period the calendar does not hold is
  replaced in the address by the period resolved. A timestamp column's default width holds an instant. A SCREENS
  revision is closed by its merge like every other number.
  (m) *Practice.* A lane reports on the head its gates ran on and does not merge main again for the report; the
  supervisor takes the difference at the merge. A lane's own slot run whose head is already merged is stopped by
  the lane if its session may; a refused stop leaves the run alone. A test-world repair of a red on main is one
  commit of its own, taken onto main ahead of the lane's slice.

- **R-119 (2026-10-01 02:20; rulings given by message between 00:55 and 02:20 on 2026-10-01 or with this entry; main
  eee85099. Corrects form (1) of R-115 (a) / R-117 (a) and the order of R-117 (b); refines R-63 (e), R-75, R-111,
  R-113 (e), R-115 (c), R-118 (e), (j) and (k).)**
  (a) *Reading a contract while its group awaits computation (lane F-RPS-REG, found and measured by the lane on its
  own item; PRODUCT DEFECT on main since 92e65393, release blocker; corrects form (1) of the reading rule).* Form
  (1) — each group's latest version, of the groups that hold a member contract at the cutoff — states NO version
  for a contract between a move and its group's next computation: an approved combination computes its group inline
  only up to 200 obligations and 30 s of engine time and defers it otherwise, and while the job is queued, retried
  or dead the combined group holds the members and has no version, and the former groups have versions and hold no
  member. Measured 01:39 with the deferral forced: RPT-02 at 30 Sep 2026 stated 30,246.58 of 107,345.17, the
  waterfall one contract of three; RPT-11, which reads along the chain, was right. By the code a LEAVE whose
  computations are deferred reads the leaver twice. Not a figure at a lock: the lock gates refuse a dirty group.
  *Rule:* one rule for "the latest" — a contract is read from the last version along its chain recorded by the
  cutoff among the versions computed with it (the computation's `stream_heads`; a version's record time is its
  latest event's, so a version a group computes after a contract has left can be stamped before the leave and must
  not count for the leaver), in membership order and then version number, the status test applied to that last
  version only; a reader keeps (contract, version) pairs. Outside the two windows this is form (1); inside one a
  contract is stated once, as last computed. Built inside RPT-FORMER-GROUP-READERS-1 (register index 96): the
  balances and the waterfall's population in `reports/tie_outs.py`, `GET /schedule-lines` and the close monitors'
  two collectors; witnesses failing first on form (1) — the deferred JOIN as measured, the LEAVE window measured
  before it is asserted, a computation that ends FAILED or DEAD, a bound run that repeats a live run taken inside a
  window pair for pair; outside the windows no expected value moves; the statement's plan under RLS beside form
  (1)'s, on access paths the index catalogue test accepts. Stated property: while a group's computation is pending
  or has failed a live read states its contracts as last computed. Item RPT-PENDING-COMPUTE-NOTE-1 (a live read
  does not say that it holds such contracts), later. The first version of a combined group carries no previous
  obligation version, so RPT-10's "Previous" columns of that version are those of a first version: left as built, a
  candidate for the accountant.
  (b) *Entity-scoped grants, as built (lane F-RPS-REG, SECFIX-SCOPE item 1; on main as fcab677e; refines R-63 (e)
  and R-115 (c)).* Confirmed: a member's scope for the commands on a membership is the union of the member's
  unrevoked assignments, expired ones included (it fails closed; an administrator of all entities can always act);
  the scope refusal comes after the step-up check, the 404 and the own-membership refusal and before validation and
  state; the operator's resend runs as SYSTEM and is never refused for scope; a role's definition is guarded before
  the read, so a scoped administrator gets 403 for an unknown role id; a scoped administrator still lists every
  member and assignment, each without the entities he does not see, until the SC-4 sweep; the DENIED event of a
  domain refusal names the command. *Added:* a grant request that reaches beyond its grantor — all entities asked
  by a grantor of named entities, or a code that names an entity outside the grantor's scope — writes one DENIED
  event (rule T-PLT-10, no entity) and answers the 422 it answers today; a code that names no entity writes none.
  The SoD commands and the access-review campaigns take the same two rules in the SC-4 sweep. Entity-scoped
  assignments written as rows in about 21 test modules stay; a module is converted when its lane next touches it.
  Item API-CLIENT-ENTITY-SCOPE-1 (`POST /api-clients` refuses every entity code; one call of the resolver) goes to
  lane SECFIX-APR with its API-client approval slice. The approver's side — a decision on a ROLE_ASSIGNMENT needs
  `access.approve` for every entity the proposal names — comes with lane SECFIX-APR's Parts 1 to 3; a witness
  through the product on merged main is owed.
  (c) *Journal dispatch (lane F-CLO-A; refines R-75 and R-118 (k)).* Measured: a commit cancelled after the ledger
  accepted leaves the batch `approved`, and the cancel then lets a recalculated run post the period twice.
  JRN-DISPATCH-DEAD-1 is built with the kernel hook and not in the handler alone: a journal export message never
  dies beside a batch that is not `failed`; the relay calls a per-topic hook when a message dies, before it records
  DEAD; a hook that raises leaves the message unsettled, to be taken again after 15 minutes as the last attempt
  again; `last_error` keeps the slug of a catalogue problem, else the exception's class; the hook is the one place
  a batch is recorded `failed`. JRN-CANCEL-APPROVED-PENDING-1 (same lane, after lane SECFIX-APR's Parts 1 to 3):
  the cancel of an `approved` run refuses while a batch's message is claimed, pending, or FAILED and due again.
  (d) *Setup completion and the lock decision (lane FIX-D2, item 6, register index 91).* SETUP-COMPLETION-LOCK-1
  (PRODUCT DEFECT, found by the lane as a measured deadlock): `setup.evaluate_setup_completion` takes the tenant
  row FOR NO KEY UPDATE — it still serialises two completions, changes no key column and no longer waits for every
  inserting transaction's key-share lock; witness: the measured interleaving in a tenant whose setup is incomplete,
  ending as 04 says, failing first by SQLSTATE 40P01. The idle bound of a dataset freeze holds for every caller of
  `snapshots.freeze_datasets` — the lock decision, the close run's DATASET_FREEZE step and the sandbox period
  replay — through one function, and the setting's name says "a dataset freeze". The BR-CLS-08 read that meets a
  held earlier period row answers 409 `lock-conflict` under a rule and copy of its own (EARLIER_PERIOD_HELD; PRD
  ERR-85), on the request path and the decision path alike.
  (e) *Modifications and estimate versions (lane SECFIX-ACT, register index 97; refines R-118 (e)).* E-12 gains
  VOIDED for a discarded estimate version. Added from lane F-CTR-WEB's CTR-27: MOD-PREFILL-READ-1 (no read answers
  the proposals of a classified draft; they come only with the answer of /classify, which writes an event, moves
  the row version and clears a stored preview); MOD-PATCH-CLEAR-1 (PATCH leaves a member sent as null, so no client
  removes `price_change_amount`, `judgement_record_id` or `reference` from a draft); MOD-ANSWER-PREVIEW-1 (submit,
  withdraw, PATCH and classify answer `impact_preview: null` while the row stores a preview; only GET attaches it);
  MOD-PREVIEW-JOURNAL-RULE-1 (the preview's journal lines move with the clock — 1,906.79 on 30 Sep, 16,039.25 in
  October for one K-02 draft — while SCREENS §7.12 expects none at the modification date: the rule is stated from
  the code first). MOD-K02-SPLIT-1, measure and classify, nothing changed meanwhile: in the demo world the K-02
  change of PRD WLD-X-06 previews O1 166,723.94 / O2 48,454.14 where the PRD, SCREENS §7.12 and the CTR-17
  acceptance (R-17) have 148,451.55 / 66,726.53; pool, RPO and revenue by period agree; O1 is weighed with 2,400 ×
  100.00 instead of 2,400 × 472 ÷ 730 × 100.00 although both worlds book the same line. If the difference is a
  question of accounting and not of data it goes to the accountant's list.
  (f) *Platform security slice (lane SECFIX-PLT, register index 98; refines R-111 and R-118 (j)).* Revision 0103
  backfills nothing (corrects the backfill the lane proposed and the supervisor accepted by message): a revision
  runs without a tenant context under forced row security and sees no stored file (DG-MIG-13); a file stored before
  0103 is read by its first uploader through the stored row, T-PLT-49 records every upload from 0103 on, and a
  second uploader of an older file reads it again by uploading it again. T-PLT-49 has no surrogate id (key: file,
  uploader, uploader kind). E-79 gains MFA_CHALLENGE_PASSED and MFA_PENDING_DENIED; the identity-provider guard is
  a trigger raising EREV-IMM-001 (409, no new problem code). `POST /contracts/{id}/events` binds its
  `evidence_file_ids` like every command that takes a file. The second preparer's 403 on a void in lane F-CLO-A's
  test becomes the 404 of R-111 (5): a stale expectation. The SoD engine with delegations and the delegation limits
  read scopes through `auth/entity_scope.py`.
  (g) *Evidence shredding, as built (lane SECFIX-IMP part B; reported, merge after lane SECFIX-APR).* Confirmed: a
  manual adjustment's attachment is held by its request's flag, not by the stored amount; every live attachment of
  such an adjustment is held; a file that records of both kinds hold is refused without override; the decision
  compares the holding records with the proposal's by table, column and row id and refuses by name when they
  differ; a reconciliation holds its source in every status but DRAFT and a migration batch from IMPORTING to
  PROMOTED. Item EVIDENCE-COUNT-SHREDDED-1 (same lane): every rule that asks for a document counts attachments
  whose file can still be read — by reading, a draft adjustment satisfies "needs an attachment" with a live
  attachment row whose file was shredded; measured first. The shred request is bound to the entities of the holding
  records once `SubjectSpec.proposal_entities` is on main.
  (h) *Screens.* The audit log's "Export" is unavailable under an Object or Outcome filter, with its reason, until
  RPT-43 takes `contract_id` and `outcome` (item RPT-43-PARAMS-1, lane F-RPS-REG: the export's rows are the list's
  rows). API-client states (refines R-113 (e)): the kit's E-103 table gains PENDING_APPROVAL and REJECTED ahead of
  the backend slice, because a status chip refuses a word outside its vocabulary and the first pending row would
  take the pane down; the rest binds when the slice is on main. CTR-27 (on main as 76fe6140): the lane's 29
  judgements confirmed; the modifications tab's toolbar pair goes; the step tracker's caption shows nothing partial
  and its region is busy until its label read answers, so that captures and the crawl wait by the rule they have; a
  blocked primary button that keeps its fill and the required-comment copy "Minimum 1 character" go to the kit
  (lane WEB-QA). REOPEN-CLOSING-FLAG-1 (on main as da2ce39e): the lane's five judgements confirmed; the cockpit's
  copy for the soft close after a reopen goes to lane F-CLO-WEB. The workspace switcher names the open membership
  by the session's active tenant id (PRODUCT DEFECT, lane F-SNP: it could not move between a workspace and its
  copies); an archived sandbox membership is not listed. `/me` states `permission_scopes`; no screen reads it yet
  (W-12, lane WEB-QA).
  (i) *Engine lane (lane ENG-FX).* EXPLAIN-RENDER-1: an explanation that cannot be rendered answers a named problem
  `explain-unavailable` (500, PRD ERR-84), logged at error level with node, formula and narrative key; never a
  quiet panel. PRODUCT-CODE-FREEZE-1: a product's code is refused a change (422, rule DB-05) while an obligation,
  an SSP entry or a mapping rule references it, with a trigger behind it (revision 0115). The registry half of
  CFG-BACKDATE-1 was already built in the lane's tree, so it lands first and lane F-SNP's whole-set rule is built
  on top of it (reverses the order of R-117 (b)).
  (j) *Gates and instruments.* The crawl's watch forgets what was in flight when the main frame commits a new
  document (lane WEB-QA; on main as eee85099): with the request guard intercepting, Chromium reports no end for a
  request that is in flight when its document is replaced, and the watch waited 45 s for a read of the landing page
  on two routes; a read of the page under test is watched as before; the resource-timing check accepted at 01:31 is
  not built. Two field-lock expectations were stale after the audit contract key and are repaired on main
  (d6f54cb7). The e2e gate on main d6f54cb7 is green (1 + 74 + 93); the `SF-06:run-lines` red of 55291038 did not
  recur and stays with lane F-CLO-A for its attribution. AK-PLATFORM-RUNNER-1 is not small — three stale layers of
  the runner's world are repaired in lane QA-BE's tree (nobody accepted the invitations; no SSP study attached; the
  approval called the hook without a decision) and the activation checklist is the fourth — so RPT-ASOF-FIGURES-1
  goes first and its merge does not wait for the EX42 block.
  (k) *Practice.* A lane whose turn never ends receives nothing: its turn is stopped and one consolidated message
  sent (lanes ENG-FX and F-SNP tonight); every lane reads its inbox file before each item and each report. A slice
  that changes what every audited event carries greps the tests for the event's detail and adds those modules to
  its gate B list.

- **R-120 (2026-10-01 05:34; rulings given by message between 02:20 and 04:30 on 2026-10-01 or with this entry; main
  de3a93ed, lane SECFIX-APR's Parts 1 to 3 on it as f97cd1e6. Refines R-24, R-63 (e), R-87 (3), R-98, R-102, R-106
  (b), R-109 (d), R-113 (e), R-118 (d), (f) and (k), R-119 (a), (b), (e), (f) and (g); corrects one launch
  practice.)**
  (a) *A manual event needs another user's approval — item CTR-6, never built (REQ-DAT-014, P0; CTL-009; found by
  lane SECFIX-CLO's measurement for CLO-REOPEN-APPROVAL-1; RELEASE BLOCKER; refines R-24 and R-118 (d)).* Measured
  through the product: a progress event entered by one holder of `event.record` is appended and computed at once
  (+4,860.00 of revenue in the lane's reopened period; by the code in every period); `contracts/events.py` says
  that routing through an event submission "waits for CTR-6 (deferred)", and since R-24 the item had no owner. It
  is lane SECFIX-CLO's now, whole and first: built as BUILD_SPEC CTR-6 states it, on the event-submission path the
  attribute change already takes; an addendum first measures which test worlds record such an event as a signed-in
  person, the world helper (the product's own path, a second persona's decision) and what the screens and e2e rows
  do with the new answer. With it CLO-REOPEN-APPROVAL-1: both restricted states — soft close and under a reopen —
  for every command through one predicate (PRD BR-CLS-04 is extended; a month's close then stops the tenant's
  auto-approval for commands whose dates reach the period, a behaviour change for Ray's information); a second
  person for every COMMAND that can post into such a period, while the system's recompute, the re-marking job and
  the close run's own entries are a stated property (they follow approved inputs, and the re-lock is decided over
  its difference report) — the stronger rule, a posting refused unless its trigger carries an approval, is a
  question for Ray; an activation's reach is its inception date; an event sent by an API client into such a period
  is measured first (BR-DAT-07 says adapter loads wait for human review); a request whose posting reaches such a
  period carries the flag PERIOD_IN_CLOSE, which the data model names and no command set.
  (b) *The approvals kernel, as merged (lane SECFIX-APR, Parts 1 to 3, on main as f97cd1e6; findings SN-4 + SC-5,
  SC-N5, BR-JE-01 through delegation and SN-9 closed).* The lane's eight judgements are confirmed, among them: a
  reader who covers one of a request's entities reads the request and cannot decide it or read its upload (R-25's
  visibility stands; what such a reader is shown of the content is to be answered); the ordinal of an import's
  approver is counted over the whole request; a request whose upload is gone answers `can_decide` false and 404;
  the deferral request of a manual adjustment names the same entities as its posting request. Auto-approval is read
  only for a contract activation and an import commit by an API client and for the two seeded rule sets, so a
  tenant rule decides no journal run, no void of an event and no attribute change in any period — three cases lane
  SECFIX-CLO had measured on main before the merge. *Ruled on the lane's notices:* APR-AUTHOR-EXCLUDED-1 — whoever
  wrote the content of a draft does not approve it, also when another person pressed submit (seven subjects:
  estimate version, policy override, modification, the event submissions, a directly entered FX rate set, the
  principal/agent change, the SSP override; a pre-build line states per subject what the row records of its
  authors); APR-DENIED-AUDIT-1 — every refusal of a decision writes its DENIED event, as the subject's own check
  does; the commit of an import attributes each act to the approver who answered for it by ordinal.
  APR-WAITING-SPECLESS-1: a pending row of a subject type without a specification is waiting for nobody; the two
  types whose phases are unbuilt keep their place in the pending list and take no placeholder (refines R-87 (3)).
  IMP-FLOOR-AMOUNT-1, the reader's half (refines R-109 (d)): no reduction to the longest list — each instance of an
  underlying approval is a list of its own; for an amount that could not be evaluated, every rule that could win
  for some amount gives a list of its own, in rank order down to and including the first without an amount
  condition — never weaker, never more people than ordinals.
  (c) *Reading rule and grants (lane F-RPS-REG; refines R-119 (a) and (b)).* The lane's gate B list on merged main
  found three reds of SECFIX-SCOPE item 1 that its own runs had not reached: the operator's resend of an invitation
  is refused for scope (the operator's principal is not the SYSTEM kind the helper knows — by the supervisor's
  reading a product defect against REQ-PLT-038, to be confirmed by the lane), a second operator test, and a role
  definition refused in the approval engine's concurrency test. Repaired first, as one commit, with a table of
  every kind of principal against the five guards. USER-ROLE-ENTITY-COUNT-1 (found by lane WEB-QA): a role row of
  API-S-User states how many entities the role names, because a viewer who sees only some of them was offered a
  command the server refuses.
  (d) *Configuration versions, pins and products, as merged (lane ENG-FX, on main as b800a501; revision 0115).* The
  integration's rename witness moved to products nothing references is a stale test world under R-112 (i) (4); a
  rename is lawful only for a product nothing references. Owed and placed: the obligation template editor has no
  "Effective from", so a superseding template version cannot be submitted on screen until it gains one (item
  TPL-EFFECTIVE-FROM-UI-1, lane F-ADM-WEB, first); the approver must not read the preparer's sentence; an
  instant-form version dated today is sent as noon UTC and refused after it. Stated limits: a booking in flight
  during a rename (PRODUCT-CODE-RACE-1); a source order line and an integration alias do not freeze a code.
  (e) *Step 1 after activation, as reported (lane SECFIX-ACT; refines R-102 and R-119 (e)).* Confirmed: a record
  that answers No to criterion (d) serves no criteria-met re-assessment — all five criteria of 606-10-25-1 hold
  before a contract exists, and the engine tests commercial substance at the activation event only. A refused
  activation request of an ACTIVE contract with nothing out answers without an engine dry run. *Item
  STEP1-HOLD-RELEASE-1:* measured on the lane's world, the review of a judgement that concludes "not a contract"
  releases the hold placed at its submission and the book recognises the whole period again (2,956.20) until the
  assessment is recorded (492.70); proposed rule, to measure and correct: the hold is released at the review only
  when the record does not conclude not-a-contract, and otherwise stays until the assessment that cites the record
  is appended. For the accountant beside AD-56: whether a release that precedes the assessment was ever acceptable,
  and the fact that the hold is contract-level, so a review about one book holds every book of the contract.
  MOD-K02-SPLIT-1 is classified: a defect of the demo seed's data — the SSP entry of AVM-SEAT-MO is seeded with
  value basis AMOUNT and no quantity unit where the PRD prices per seat-month; the engine follows S06-R-11 in both
  worlds; no question of accounting (item DEMO-SSP-BASIS-1, same lane; three term-priced series entries take
  PER_BOOKED_TERM). The wizard's request names no basis and no override (lane F-CTR-WEB's traced bodies).
  MOD-ANSWER-PREVIEW-1 is corrected by the lane's reading: PATCH and classify clear a stored preview, so their null
  is true; submit, withdraw and discard answer through the reader GET uses. The five keys of an estimate's
  constraint checklist are named after the factors of 606-10-32-12 and go into 04 (EST-CONSTRAINT-KEYS-1).
  (f) *The fourteenth gate's race (lane F-CLO-B, found by the lane's own review of CLO-GATE-RUN-1).* A computation
  that lowers a group's mark without posting a line into the period holds nothing the lock decision waits for, and
  can commit between the decision's gate read and its commit: the period locks with that period end unposted. Built
  into the item before its merge: a computation inside a window takes the state rows of the window's periods FOR
  SHARE, in one fixed order, before it posts.
  (g) *Evidence (lane SECFIX-IMP; refines R-119 (g)).* Part B is not merged by keep-list: against the approvals
  kernel as merged it needs code (the closed lists of entity statements, two call sites, one expectation that
  becomes the kernel's 404), so the lane ports it onto main in a fresh tree with the entity-set binding built in;
  the Avenmoor seed's missing routing rule for EVIDENCE_SHRED is a defect of part B, found by a test that walks the
  enumeration. EVIDENCE-COUNT-SHREDDED-1 is measured and is three rules — the threshold adjustment's attachment,
  the SSP study at submit and at approve, the reconciliation's source at prepare: a document a rule asks for is one
  whose file can still be read. An approved shred of a document that a pending request rests on makes that
  request's decision refuse as stale, and the shred request's preview names the pending requests it would void.
  (h) *Reads and lists (lane API-GAPS).* PERF-PERIODS-LIST-1: a row of `GET /periods` answers no `blockers` — no
  reader takes them from the list, each cost a statement over 23 tables (14.6 ms a row, 2.6 to 3.6 s for a page of
  180 to 200 rows on this host, 1,187 locks held by one read that every page makes); the single read, the cockpit
  and the commands' answers keep them; the run and the lock of the page's rows come in two statements. Rule for the
  dev-guide: a list answers no member that costs a statement per row. For PERF-RLS-INDEX-1 the outbox claim's index
  is partial on `status NOT IN ('DISPATCHED','DEAD')`, and the list kernel binds enumeration literals as constants,
  so that a list by status can prove a partial index.
  (i) *Operations (lane OPS; refines R-118 (k)).* The period tick isolates per tenant as per period: each tenant's
  two reads in a unit of work of their own, the server's own state ends the tick, anything else is logged at error
  level and that tenant's step is skipped; a tenant list that cannot be read ends the tick. The tick's, the
  fan-out's and the sweeper's error events state where the exception was raised (module and line of the last frame
  of this code base, no values, no message) through one helper. ARCH-IMPORT-CYCLE-1 is a static replay of the
  interpreter's import order for every module as the first import (DG-ARC-17), held to the interpreter by fourteen
  small packages; the replay named the ten first imports that failed before the repair of 0f8c0c5e and no other;
  the import of every module by the real interpreter is no gate (16 minutes) and is run at reports and before a
  release candidate. `erev db reset` for a worktree's own development database is an item of that lane.
  (j) *Screens.* W-12 (lane WEB-QA): one module is the only reader of `permissions` and `permission_scopes` and
  answers four questions — held for this entity, held anywhere, held for all entities, held for every entity of a
  list; a lint ratchet keeps a bare permission test out; a command held for another entity only is hidden; the
  approval decision form follows the server's `can_decide`. W-23 as built is evidenced by its own e2e run (173
  passed); what merges is a second head with the command sites that reached main since converted. The reopen banner
  states two facts (post-reopen flag, the difference report) and no second approver until the control exists. "New
  API client" offers no entity choice until the server takes one, and a server error on a field shows at the field
  (the same defect was found and fixed in the webhook drawer and the revoke dialog). A run's progress is shown to
  its starter and to a holder of `audit.read`. The drill's rows carry the contract's external id
  (JRN-DRILL-CONTRACT-NAME-1, on main as de3a93ed): the source-lines drawer made 91 contract reads, 18.8 s
  together, before it showed a row.
  (k) *Data quality, as merged (lane SECFIX-CLO, on main as 2d81bfef; refines R-118 (f)).* The lane's six
  judgements are confirmed: the resolver is the system whoever ran the monitors; no new audit action; no
  notification; an item another transaction holds is skipped for a later run. Open, with item 4: the sweep
  evaluates a LEGACY row for ever, so a period its primary book has locked keeps raising items that nothing
  settles; a finding corrected between the lock request and the decision refuses the decision until some run
  settles it.
  (l) *The demo world.* The seed keeps four contracts as drafts (K-03, K-04, K-08, K-10) for reasons that no longer
  hold at the submission — each passes the activation checklist and its dry run today (lane F-CTR-WEB; the approval
  not measured). With the wrong SSP basis, the clock that opens the next period minutes after a seed, and the
  worlds the screens' wireframes assume and the seed lacks, the demo world is measured as one inventory (lane
  F-ADM-WEB, read-only) before any of it is built.
  (m) *The accountant's addendum (lane ACCT; on main as 8b5b79a9).* A dated addendum, not the one of the
  release-candidate milestone; the lane's labels U-1 to U-17 stand until that cut, where every point put to the
  accountant takes a number. POL-074 is registered as a question: its row gives a level, a pin and a parity value
  to a parameter whose approval code says it is fixed and not configurable. CTL-009 is named in neither the package
  nor the addendum; the lane inventories every designated control against main — its witness, whether it is built,
  the evidence line.
  (n) *Practice.* Every detached run is launched at normal priority: zsh starts a background job at nice 5 with its
  whole tree, a user cannot renice it back, and beside other sessions' work at nice 0 the same test took three to
  ten times as long (measured by the Engram supervisor and confirmed here) — through bash, or with BG_NICE unset;
  the slot wrapper warns and records it; the whole-suite run alone is left at nice 5 on purpose. A here-document
  writes a temporary file under /tmp in zsh; commands set TMPPREFIX into the worktree or run a script file. The
  supervisor runs nothing heavy beside its own e2e gate: the run on 0653b044 lost five first renders to the
  supervisor's own merge checks and is no evidence. A slice that changes who may run a command runs the directory
  of that command's domain before its report; a slice that adds an enumeration value runs the modules that walk the
  enumeration, found by the iteration and not by the name.

- **R-121 (2026-10-01 08:43; rulings given by message between 04:30 and 08:45 on 2026-10-01 or with this entry; main
  58eba434. Refines R-28, R-78 (c) and (d), R-98, R-108 (b), R-111, R-112 (c), R-116 (d), R-118 (a) and (e), R-119
  (a) and (c), R-120 (a), (b) and (g); records fifteen merges, three repairs by the supervisor and the triage of
  lane ACCT's control inventory.)**
  (a) *On main since R-120 (first parent, in order).* 5d52e697 the supervisor's repair of CFG-BACKDATE-1
  (submitting an account mapping version answered 500); 815471c9 WEB-QA "Run journals" keeps each entity's start
  under its key; a37c5008 OPS the period tick per tenant; aa8b91ac API-GAPS PERF-RLS-INDEX-1 (revision 0112);
  838aa8de a stale expectation of the Step 1 slice; 9b881b70 FIX-D2 item 6 (the lock decision holds the next
  period's row; ERR-78, ERR-85); 15ccedb6 F-CLO-A the exits of a failed journal run (revision 0108; ERR-73, ERR-74,
  ERR-79); abccbcbe F-CLO-B CLO-GATE-RUN-1 (a period locks only on its close run; revision 0111); 4050c50d
  SECFIX-APR the setup rule per request and the spec-less waiting item; 27c8e193 SECFIX-ACT the modification
  discard, PATCH clear, answered preview, the Step 1 copy point (ERR-82); c6c8db5f WEB-QA W-12a (the access module
  and lint rule DG-FE-16); 58b6a171 F-SNP the sandbox screen, the open workspace, SBX-PROBE-1, SBX-EMAIL-1, R-98
  (ERR-77); 84ee5002 SECFIX-ACT DEMO-SSP-BASIS-1; dc534e7f QA-BE RPT-ASOF-FIGURES-1 (release blocker closed;
  candidate AD-63); e2233f67 F-ADM-WEB TPL-EFFECTIVE-FROM-UI-1; 0df0404d a stale test world of item 6 at the join
  with CLO-GATE-RUN-1 (eight tests); bc20ff06 F-CLO-A JRN-CANCEL-APPROVED-PENDING-1; 58eba434 SECFIX-IMP the
  EVIDENCE_SHRED port (revision 0094; IMP-129). The Alembic chain on main ends 0099, 0113, 0115, 0086, 0112, 0108,
  0111, 0094; the copy catalogue holds 79 ERR + 135 IMP = 214 rows; the audit catalogue 232 command operations, 226
  required. Three joins were repaired by the supervisor at the merge, each named in its commit: the audit event
  `modification.discard` names its contract; routes/settings/sandbox.tsx and the lock-decision test world as above.
  A trial merge of F-CTR-WEB's CTR-25 (8228c13e) was cancelled before any commit: on the merged tree the whole
  vitest was red four runs of four on one racing test of that lane, and the lane merges main and repairs it.
  (b) *CTR-6, the lane's addendum (lane SECFIX-CLO; refines R-120 (a)).* P1: the documents' evidence rule stands
  (required for progress, milestone, cost and a delivery whose trigger is ACCEPTANCE; offered for the other
  deliveries and a return); the approval is asked of all five kinds. P2: the evidence files are attachments of the
  request. P3: one approval hook for MANUAL_EVENT and ATTRIBUTE_CHANGE — check again, append, compute — so an
  approved void posts its reversal at the approval. P4: a submission is checked again where it is appended; that it
  was not is a PRODUCT DEFECT of main (measured: two approved submissions of 60 units against 80 remaining leave
  240 of 200 in the stream; CTL-008 by-passed), and a finding voids the request as stale. P5: the approver's
  preview (REQ-PLT-015, P0) is its own item, MANUAL-EVENT-PREVIEW-1, built before the release candidate. P6 to P10
  as the lane proposed; the applied event's row of the API names its preparer and approver. The answer-key platform
  runner is converted by the lane at the join (an item with `is_manual` false is sent as an API client). The seed
  records as its personas: maya submits with evidence, priya approves. *CLO-REOPEN-APPROVAL-1:* a batch dated into
  a period in soft close or under a reopen is stored as a submission whoever sends it — an integration included —
  except a batch of memo updates alone and the two Step 1 types; TWO flags, `PERIOD_IN_CLOSE` and
  `PERIOD_REOPENED`; the four other roads into a stream (holds, regroup before posting, adapter documents, memo
  updates) are measured before anything is built for them; a reopen for ERROR_CORRECTION names its judgement record
  in free text only and nothing checks it — a question for Ray and the accounting reviewer, nothing built.
  (c) *A request's content is for a reader who covers every entity of it — item APR-CONTENT-SCOPE-1, RELEASE
  BLOCKER (lane SECFIX-APR, measured; refines R-25 and R-120 (b)).* A reader who covers one entity of a two-entity
  import read its preview file and content naming the other entity's contract and figures. The header (number,
  status, steps, the entities the reader may read, `entity_count`, `can_decide`) is for a reader who covers at
  least one entity; the content — summary, `subject.display`, amount, flags, the decisions' comments, the preview
  and its file, the attachments, lane API-GAPS' content rows — only for a reader who covers every entity, by one
  predicate beside `visible`; a boolean `content_withheld` says so; files answer 404; no exception for the
  requester; the list's sort, filters and search read a content column through one function, and the preparer's
  notifications compose their title for the recipient.
  (d) *The approvals kernel's further slices (lane SECFIX-APR).* The bootstrap exception ends per request, for the
  entities another approver has covered (reading B; merged). Slice (3), an API client's scopes as an access grant,
  is accepted as lined up: statuses PENDING_APPROVAL and REJECTED, `has_secret`, the first secret issued through
  rotate-secret after another person's approval, no row action on a pending client; API-CLIENT-ENTITY-SCOPE-1 with
  it.
  (e) *Policy overrides and pins (lane ENG-FX).* PIN-READBACK-1 is built in two steps, the second step's carrier on
  the POL-070 read-back row, ENGINE_VERSION unchanged. POLICY-OVERRIDE-BUNDLE-1 — an approved policy override
  reaches no computation — is a RELEASE BLOCKER, ruled on six points: the engine change for a read-back value that
  carries an option; a contract-level override of a pin-P parameter takes the periods postable at its approval and
  a locked period keeps its value (candidate for the accountant); the seven override levels no stage reads are
  withdrawn — creation refused by name with what decides instead — unless an answer key or seeded world creates
  such an override through the product; the catch-up follows POLICIES section 0.5 rule 3; the preview at submit and
  the computation at the approval are inside the item; two slices. To confirm or refute by measurement: that a
  pin-P value is stamped on every period of a bundle (PINP-PERIOD-VALUE-1).
  (f) *Modifications, Step 1 and estimates (lane SECFIX-ACT).* The journal preview of a modification states when it
  was computed and for which posting period, with a warning line (form A; forms B and C are recorded for Ray). A
  discarded draft frees its reference (revision 0114). STEP1-HOLD-RELEASE-1: the hold stays after a rejected,
  withdrawn or stale-voided review; for a NOT_A_CONTRACT record it is released by the assessment that cites the
  record or by supersession, and is placed at submission whatever the routing; `release-hold` refuses a judgement
  hold while its record awaits review (today the preparer releases alone the hold her own record placed); a
  not-probable assessment of an active book without a significant-change flag is refused (IMP-136) instead of
  accepted without effect. Estimates: the constraint checklist holds all five keys or is null; the linked-version
  check sits at the modification's submission (ERR-87) and stays at the approval; one open version per element;
  SM-04's evidence is asked by the API too; a version without a pending request is discarded only as DRAFT.
  (g) *Reports (lanes QA-BE and FIX-D2; refines R-78 (c) and (d), R-118 (a)).* A contract enters the RPO, its
  rollforward and RPT-11 at its ACTIVATION — the effective date of CONTRACT_ACTIVATED, CONTRACT_CRITERIA_MET or
  OPENING_BALANCE_ESTABLISHED — which replaces "at its first measured period"; at a look-back the remainder at the
  date decides which obligations are in, and the contract read's RPO follows the report; a late event after a lock
  gets the row LATE_EVENTS; the exact ledger reader is not built. The item moves to lane FIX-D2 with the
  waterfall's entity: an obligation is stated under its PERFORMING entity (PRODUCT DEFECT for an intercompany
  contract; candidate AD-49). PER-LEGACY-POSTING-PERIOD-1: the posting period of the LEGACY book follows the
  primary's, ERR-15 names the primary's book, and the primary's lock decision also opens the LEGACY book's next
  period (BR-CLS-03 for the book that follows), so that no event is refused after a lock for want of a LEGACY
  period.
  (h) *Journals (lane F-CLO-A; refines R-112 (c), R-119 (c)).* A message never attempted does not refuse the cancel
  of an approved run; a claimed one and a failed one due again do. The EXPORT_FAILED notification does not say that
