  the ledger rejected what eRev itself failed to send. JRN-RETRY-CLAIMED-1: a retry is refused while the batch's
  export message is claimed (PRODUCT DEFECT of CLO-14, measured: a cancel can pass beside a dispatch after a worker
  stopped between two commits). JR-CLOSED-PERIOD-GUARD-1: a journal run is neither calculated nor cancelled in a
  closed period, by a trigger beside the named refusal (ERR-86), with the line guard of a batch that is no longer
  draft; a `future` period is refused at the command, as PRD SM-08 always said.
  (i) *The close's reads (lane F-CLO-B).* GET /exceptions gains `blocking=<period state id>`, the gates' own
  predicate, and `combination_group_code`. One attribution everywhere: the list's `entity` filter and Home's figure
  take an item as an entity's by the gates' rule; an item of an import holds the close of the entities its upload
  names, a failed import's control totals and a sync run's item likewise; Home's close row reads "Exceptions
  holding the lock". A reader of one entity is told the count and is not shown an item he may not read. The lock
  record's read gains its certification, ledger head hash, snapshots and request numbers; the audit head's MAC is
  not stated; `config.read` stands.
  (j) *Registry versions (lane F-SNP).* REG-VERSION-WHOLE-SET-1 with CFG-PLATFORM-PIN-1: no column (`unset` as
  nulls of a draft, `basis` a request member); the predecessor's preset code is carried; the instant form is for
  the categories PLATFORM, CLOSE, INTEGRATION, SECURITY and AI, none of whose parameters a computation reads;
  `lifecycle.reopen` takes its kind's creation lock, for all five version kinds; the retention evidence names the
  version that last changed the retention families. Ten settings are read by nothing and are recorded. A sandbox's
  invitation and password-reset mail stay; its notifications are delivered in the app only.
  (k) *Entity scope, from lane WEB-QA's check of a member of one entity in a real browser.* EXC-IMPORT-SCOPE-1,
  RELEASE BLOCKER (lane SECFIX-IMP): the exception items of an import are read by a member who cannot read the
  import, with figures; a row-level finding names the contract and entity its row resolved, and an import's item
  without an entity is read and acted on only by the import's readers. SCOPE-WORKSPACE-LISTS-1, RELEASE BLOCKER
  (lane F-RPS-REG, after READERS-1): the tenant-wide lists of R-28 — audit events and their companions, jobs beyond
  one's own, control executions, source records, six workspace registers — still answer a holder of named entities.
  (l) *Platform slices.* Lane SECFIX-PLT (R-111): a delegation counts for the SoD from its creation, started or
  not, at a command, and as it stood at an as-of report; RPT-25 gains "Through delegations"; an upload's body is
  bounded before the form is parsed. Lane OPS: `erev idp domains`, `disable` and `enable`; the platform scope's
  allow-list; `erev db reset` only in a development environment, on a development database, with no tenant that
  lacks the demo marker; the doctor's production check of index conditions warns and does not stop a rollout.
  EVIDENCE-COUNT-SHREDDED-1 (lane SECFIX-IMP; R-120 (g)) is lined up in five places; a prepared or reviewed
  reconciliation keeps its sign-offs.
  (m) *Screens.* W-12b (lane WEB-QA): a journal run's History is rendered only with `audit.read` for all entities
  (PRODUCT DEFECT: three seeded roles got an error with a Retry that could not work); the audit log asks all
  entities. TPL-EFFECTIVE-FROM-UI-1's thirteen judgements stand; an accounting policy version keeps its date.
  CTR-25's twenty-seven judgements stand with one change: a stale-voided request is a warning banner on both
  records. JRN-FAILED-EXITS-UI-1's copy is approved. To lane WEB-QA: `useCommand` leaves the body of a 202 unread
  (a connection per command), the capture helper waits for no busy region.
  (n) *Lane ACCT's control inventory (49 rows read on abccbcbe, nothing run: 12 built, 3 not built, 34 partly; new
  clause-level gaps in 16 rows).* Placed for measurement and a pre-build line: the void of an applied amendment,
  estimate change, regroup, booking or contract void under one `event.approve` step (lane SECFIX-CLO, release
  blocker if measured), the over-delivery bound after a modification and the missing over-billing check, the SSP
  version pin by attribute change (same lane); the judgement records REQ-POL-008 makes mandatory (lanes ENG-FX and
  SECFIX-ACT); the activation flags REQ-CON-006 names and never derives (lane SECFIX-ACT, release blocker while an
  auto-approval rule is published); a failed scheduled job that tells nobody and the hosted alert filters that name
  nothing the application emits (lane OPS). Unplaced, for Ray's scope decision: the rollforward's tie to the
  general ledger with the two rollforward reconciliation kinds, the legacy modification import's missing
  modification, the product-usable gate, the RPO expedients that exclude nothing, CTL-041 and CTL-048 as missing
  features, and the smaller list. The lane writes the inventory as a committed document.
  (o) *Practice.* A step that a lane's session refused is taken over by nobody: lane SECFIX-IMP's removal of its
  own scratch trees (about 1 GiB) is recorded for Ray and not run by the supervisor. Document numbers come from the
  register's index, not from the highest number in any tree.

- **R-122 (2026-10-01 17:51; rulings given by message between 08:45 and 17:45 on 2026-10-01 or with this entry; main
  b25b3cf0. Refines R-22, R-23, R-25, R-50 (c), R-64 (6), R-77, R-87 (4), R-95, R-105 (2), R-106 (b), R-112,
  R-113 (f), R-114 (b), R-115 (c), R-118 (m), R-119 (b), R-120 (a) and (e), R-121 (b), (c), (f), (g), (k) and (n);
  replaces the supervisor's ruling of 05:23 on the price test (Q-P2 (a)); records forty-seven first-parent
  commits on main since R-121 — forty-one lane merges, five repairs by the supervisor, one record commit.)**
  (a) *On main since R-121 (first parent, in order).* b85bcca1 a stale test world of the deposit transfer; e6f6a0f0
  the as-of lock witness runs its close run again; 3fb94364 QA-BE the reader-independent computation (R-95; class
  55 transient); b8aee66d F-RPS-REG the chain readers (release blocker of R-119 (a)); 8ec7a521 WEB-QA W-12b;
  ac5e337b F-ADM-WEB the product drawer's refusals; 5f914f85 API-GAPS the periods list and GET /search; 20557bd3
  SECFIX-APR the import's approvers; 93cdbe1d, c2f6630e, bdd4d301 ACCT the control inventory; 58ab6aa9 SECFIX-PLT
  the R-111 slice (revision 0103); 67fe0c12 F-RPS-REG RPT-43's two parameters; a08d0104 F-CLO-B the blocking list
  and the lock record's members; 89d7471d OPS the provider commands; 2280e855 QA-BE the runner's world through the
  product; 9ed28c4f F-CTR-WEB CTR-25; fe2b65ca ENG-FX PIN-READBACK-1; 4464a69c API-GAPS `code_frozen`; b90b74db
  F-CLO-WEB the exits of a failed run; 30b53401 WEB-QA `send` reads an answer whole; a3da9d32 SECFIX-ACT two heads
  (revision 0114 on 0103); 591e208a FIX-D2 the LEGACY book's posting period; e445bcb1 and 65e9c970 two stale
  expectations repaired by the supervisor; 5a2a14d1 SECFIX-ACT J-06 in the built order; 2e011fcc F-ADM-WEB demo
  slice A; afe02323 OPS the alert names; 344b1f15 F-CTR-WEB the History chip; 1b72b85c SECFIX-APR
  APR-CONTENT-SCOPE-1; 69b21331 WEB-QA W-12c c4; 369d48e2 SECFIX-CLO BUILD_SPEC CTR-6; f227efad SECFIX-ACT J-06's
  two steps (PRD 1.164); 47a6c177 QA-BE MAP-RULE-FOREIGN-1; 2a3072b8 QA-BE SSP-STUDY-SERIES-BASIS-1; e0a4bfed
  F-ADM-WEB two drawers; 7ecc0628 WEB-QA KIT-FILTER-LEAVING-1; 90d03fd6 FIX-D2 RPT-RPO-ROLLFWD-1; f85dd3c0 WEB-QA
  two liveness repairs; d809b15e QA-BE the lock-wait witness; d93d57e0 F-RPS-REG SCOPE-WORKSPACE-LISTS-1; a978ba29
  FIX-D2 the waterfall's entity; 5c21d4a3 F-CTR-WEB the modification item; 923b20af SECFIX-APR APR-SETUP-RULE-2;
  4f40c360 F-CLO-WEB JRN-RETRY-FOLLOW-1; b25b3cf0 ENG-FX ENG-INCEPTION-ESTIMATE-1. The Alembic chain on main ends
  0113, 0115, 0086, 0112, 0108, 0111, 0094, 0103, 0114; the copy catalogue holds 82 ERR + 135 IMP = 217 rows.
  Joins repaired by the supervisor at a merge, each named in its commit: the door DG-ARC-16 (two entries);
  tests/api/test_oidc.py; the estimate form's typed checklist; test_reader_independence.py and the impact
  summary's pin at CTR-6; a header entry the governed-documents test read as a revision number.
  (b) *How the shop works (every lane).* Load rule L-1: every heavy run goes through the slot wrapper, a pool of
  six, one per worktree; a hold is the file the wrapper reads. A registry-held row of a governed document goes into
  the CODE commit, and every commit of a lane head passes the architecture directory; a head that touches a
  governed document runs the governed-documents and document unit tests with gate A. A landed revision is not
  amended in place: a correction takes a number. A lane whose session was refused a merge of main merges nothing:
  it builds on its own head and reports a keep-list, or delivers one patch per item against a named head of main;
  the supervisor never orders such a merge (the error of 09:01 and of the thirteen merges of lane SECFIX-CLO was
  the supervisor's). A join made by hand is run as a candidate commit in a verification tree before it is
  committed on main. For every refusal that names an object, a lane states the states that object can be in and
  what the operator can do in each (lane F-CLO-B's lesson from its own defect).
  (c) *Approvals (lane SECFIX-APR; refines R-87 (4), R-106 (b), R-121 (c)).* APR-CONTENT-SCOPE-1 accepted as built.
  The setup rule: reading B is the meaning — the rule ends for a request when ONE other person covers every entity
  it names, and for all requests at setup completion; the automatic approval's event names the entities another
  approver covers, read from the roles as they are at the approval (the residual of R-87 (4), stated). Setup
  completion is evaluated at an invitation's acceptance, at a reactivation and before a role request is routed
  (`completion_due`, read without a lock; the stamp stays the command's last step); the open period is read under
  the tenant's SYSTEM scope; the same read goes to `POST /periods/{id}/open` and to an API client's scopes. A
  request of a subject type without a specification is refused by name (409, SM-01). NEW, because the seam makes
  it common: the preparer of a request is told the decider's comment of a REJECTION of her own request, in the
  notification and on her read, and nothing else of withheld content (APR-REJECTION-REASON-PREPARER-1).
  (d) *Lists and acts of the whole workspace (lane F-RPS-REG; refines R-115 (c), R-119 (b), R-121 (k)).* Q1 to Q7
  as answered: the control executions, the audit log with its actors and the verify command for all entities, the
  two verification reads at any scope; a job is read by its owner or with `audit.read` for all entities (stated
  limit JOBS-BY-SUBJECT-SCOPE-1); a source record through a link of a contract the caller reads; (c1) tenant-level
  administration — the tenant's settings and currencies, the checklist templates' commands, snapshots, sandboxes,
  the reset, webhooks, support grants — takes its permission for all entities, and a SUPPORT_GRANT request spans
  every entity, so a holder of named entities is not listed it. The approval registers state a request only in a
  run that covers every entity it names; "every entity" is every legal entity created by the run's record cutoff.
  (c2) with (c3), measured release-blocking: a connection is in reach when the caller's scope covers EVERY entity
  it serves; migrations take `migration.run` for all entities in this release; a new SoD rule version and an
  access-review campaign are acts of the whole workspace, a campaign's reviewers hold `access.approve` for all
  entities, and a command on one member takes that member's scope; a combination suggestion is listed for a
  reader of at least one of its contracts and names only those; the reconciliation lines' 500 (a zero read as
  `0E-18`) is repaired by the lane. Recorded: NOTIFY-ROLE-HOLDERS-SCOPE-1, MIG-BATCH-ENTITY-SCOPE-1,
  ACCESS-REVIEW-SCOPED-REVIEWERS-1; WS-OBJECT-AUTH-1 (what a role for one entity may do to workspace-wide
  configuration) is read by lane ACCT before it is ruled.
  (e) *The close (lane F-CLO-B; refines R-112, R-114 (b), R-121 (f)).* CLO-GATE-RUN-2 as the lane proposed after
  its witnesses failed first on main (measured: the Controller's approval answered 200 and January closed with a
  reclass of 1,000.00 unposted): R-STEP, R-GATE with the waived-quarantine exception, R-COMPUTE, R-START (only the
  period's latest run is resumed), R-STORE. The lane's own defect of that build, measured, and its repair: an
  entry in a CLOSED period refuses no later run — (a') the first period-end step is refused only when a POSTABLE
  period ends on or after the entry and starts before the run's period, and names the earliest such period; (c')
  the writer marks a group whose entry stands on or after the period's first day and, when no earlier period is
  postable, every group the step passed. Residual 1 (a group that only performs for the entity and holds no
  entry) and residual 2 (a period locked by two waivers keeps its unposted reclass; the waivers and the lock are
  the tenant's decisions) are stated in 04 as measured. Revision 0111's downgrade restores the thirteen-literal
  check NOT VALID with NOTICE EREV-MIG-0111 and deletes no row (DG-MIG-13's second form in the downgrade
  direction). Recorded: CLO-GROUPS-EMPTY-MEMBER-1 (before an emptied group's dirty flag is cleared),
  CLO-WINDOW-STEP-START-1, PERF-CLOSE-FANOUT-1 (126.5 s of a 142.5 s lock for 106 groups in three passes).
  (f) *Journals (lanes F-CLO-A and F-CLO-WEB).* A retry writes its message only when the message the batch names
  is settled: claimed, 409; still to be sent or due again, 202 and nothing written, named with its instant in the
  job's `result.waiting` (the message keeps the wait the ledger stated). The run's retry lives in the API —
  `export` on a run with failed batches retries each under that rule, all or nothing beside a claim; until it is
  on main the page offers no header command on a Failed run. F9: the cancel of a failed run and the hand-over of
  a failed batch are refused while a message died less than fifteen minutes ago of anything but the ledger's
  refusal, and outright where the ledger accepted and does not show the journal yet (an acceptance is a class of
  its own); the window shrinks and is not closed, and 05 says so. F10: a re-run exit job returns what its own
  decision event records. An empty run: none is made when a run covers every posting of the period (409 by name,
  ERR-96); the empty first run of a period without activity is made, is never submitted, and the period locks on
  it. A journal run is neither calculated nor cancelled in a closed period (ERR-86); the hand-over, the retry and
  the acknowledgement of a batch that stands there are taken. The export dialog sends the adapter of the run's
  batches. The subledger line's read model gains the obligation's key, null wherever the contract's name is.
  (g) *Events, holds and the regroup (lane SECFIX-CLO; refines R-22, R-120 (a), R-121 (b)).* CTR-6 accepted and
  joined by its keep-list. Three approvals reached by a lesser route are closed: a void is requested only for
  the twelve fact-capture types the events route records (the significant-change flag among them; a booking, an
  amendment, an estimate change, a regroup, a contract void and the other command events are refused with the
  sentence that names what undoes them), with the same list where a stored void is appended; the attribute-change
  route refuses the two SSP pin members; and `POST /contracts/{id}/regroup` is taken for TWO DRAFTS and for
  nothing else in this release — before posting it moved an obligation into an active contract and posted
  35,901.37 without a request, and after posting the road moved 95,868.49 — the first day's remaining allocation,
  while 35,901.37 stood posted — and, once the contract it booked was activated, recognised the moved
  obligation's revenue to date a second time (REGROUP-AFTER-POSTING-AMOUNT-1; REQ-CON-012's
  second half is withdrawn from this release until it is rebuilt — a scope fact for Ray). The delivery bound reads
  the quantity IN FORCE, folded from the live stream. A person's usage report is a manual event (it recognised
  5,000.00 alone, without evidence). A recognition hold is neither applied nor released while the entity's
  current date lies in a period in soft close or under reopen. BR-DAT-07's "affecting" means posting into: what
  posts no line under the policy in force is appended, what posts waits — measured under `billing.posting` = ERP,
  to be measured under ENGINE. In a combination group of several contracting entities the preparer of an event
  submission is held to her own contract's contracting entity, the request stays bound to the group's entities for
  readers and deciders (the seam of R-106 (b) registered a second time; MANUAL-EVENT-PREPARER-SCOPE-1).
  (h) *Modifications, Step 1 and estimates (lane SECFIX-ACT; refines R-23, R-113 (f), R-121 (f)).* REPLACED: the
  ruling of 05:23 that the price test is always reported beside a stored answer — it made a confirmed answer read
  as a proposal and the wizard dropped it (the e2e row "SF-07:detail", red on main since a3da9d32). `prefill_reasons`
  names a question only while the row holds no answer of the preparer; the price test's facts are a member of
  their own when a screen binds them (MOD-PRICE-TEST-FACT-1); the wizard keeps what the row holds. Step 1: a
  probable or criteria-met assessment that cites a record answering No to (e) is refused (extends R-113 (f) (3));
  the hold of a reviewed NOT_A_CONTRACT record is released by the first not-probable assessment that cites it, by
  supersession, or by the review of a COLLECTIBILITY record of the same subject and book; the release by hand is
  refused while a record awaits review. R-23's second order, measured true: the hold follows the record's
  `contract_id`, not its subject type; a record whose subject is a modification or an estimate version holds
  nothing, because its subject changes nothing before its own approval and its review precedes that approval.
  Estimates: a version's content pins the contract's head (a stale request answers 409 at any decision); one open
  version per element, also as an index (revision 0117); evidence at the submission, an attestation of no change
  told by its values and refused when the values differ; a variable-consideration version names a CONSTRAINT
  record bound to its element and is approved only when the record is reviewed. A modification is not submitted
  or approved over a DRAFT or SUBMITTED judgement record whose subject it is; a judgement record gains a discard
  (revision 0118). "Revise" as PRD SM-03 states it: the first edit of a REJECTED modification returns it to DRAFT
  (measured: it could be neither revised, discarded, nor entered again under its reference). J-06's two estimate
  requests route two steps each (PRD 1.164).
  (i) *Engine and policies (lane ENG-FX).* ENG-INCEPTION-ESTIMATE-1 as merged: an estimate in force at inception
  is in the inception price wherever its event stands; corpus hashes identical; D-92's precedence stands and the
  two record orders differ at the inception date until ENG-INCEPTION-AMEND-ORDER-1 (options III and II; II moves
  64 bundle rows of 23 keys — the analysis of what moves comes before any key is regenerated). PINP-PERIOD-VALUE-1:
  the PERIOD row of entity E and period p carries the value in force at the earlier of `known_at` and p's last
  instant in E's zone; an undated successor is in force from its predecessor's end; a FIRST version without a date
  answers for every earlier period; POL-123 is read per period, else POL-004 at that period; the readers outside
  the bundle are a stated limit (PINP-READERS-OUTSIDE-BUNDLE-1). POLICY-OVERRIDE-BUNDLE-1: POL-233 keeps level C,
  seven levels withdrawn; pin P means not closed at the approval; of two members that override one period-pinned
  parameter the last approved wins. The catch-up of a policy override, as the lane measured it on SF-ORD-30201
  (100,571.43 / 27,428.57 before; 95,319.15 / 32,680.85 after): fourteen posting intents, two per open period
  from P03 to P09, each period taking its own difference — O1 -1,025.53, O2 +3,079.42, revenue +2,053.89 to
  37,772.42 — where the per-obligation SSP override, an event, posts the same +2,053.89 once in the period of its
  approval; a locked period's difference posts in the first open one. POLICIES rule 3's sentence is corrected to
  that (rev 1.121).
  (j) *Reports, bundles and the runner (lanes FIX-D2 and QA-BE; refines R-50 (c), R-121 (g)).* The waterfall
  states an obligation under the entity that performs it; the Home's three figure panels read as the reports'
  jobs do. A contract enters the RPO, its rollforward and RPT-11 at its activation; the rollforward states late
  events on a line of their own; a look-back includes what remained of an obligation at the date; the residual
  case (124,910.14 where the ledger gives 118,430.14 at 31 January) is a stated limit. RPT-12 follows RPT-11. The
  period pin (ERR-72, PERIOD_STATE_MOVED) is checked at the end of the appending transaction: in the persist for
  a command that computes, with the window read for an appender that computes nothing; a person's manual event
  cannot straddle a lock since CTR-6. A cell explanation reads wide and names the contributors the caller's
  scope reaches, the rest as one line per entity. A close run of the LEGACY book is refused by ERR-76. A mapping
  rule for a product or an entity the bundle does not hold is left out of the bundle; history is not reposted.
  A draft from an SSP study keeps the entry's basis, is refused whole with the reasons in `detail`, and takes no
  catalogue row. The answer-key runner: the subledger block is read as the group's sealed postings through the
  cutoff; a checkpoint at a period end runs the product's close run once that step is built, and its blocks end
  "not run" by name until then; a LEGACY_PARITY key's templates are created by the plan; the group's key name
  comes from the reader; a report row whose every expected measure is zero compares clean when absent; GT07 ends
  "not run" by name.
  (k) *The registry (lane F-SNP).* A version reopened after a later version of its key was published is refused
  (ERR-92); the publication snapshot states the whole set and the date; the create takes the key's lock before
  it reads its predecessor; `diff_against_current` of a published or superseded version is the difference it
  made; a version that only carries the retention families is not withheld from auto-approval; the order rule
  admits a tie with a published version that names no date; the demo seed's PLATFORM and INTEGRATION versions
  lose their date. A field that governs nothing is not offered: two fields leave the workspace settings screen,
  and the ten parameters nothing reads are a scope question for Ray (SETTINGS-UNREAD-1).
  (l) *Platform, files and operations (lanes SECFIX-PLT and OPS; refines R-105 (2)).* A shred issued in a sandbox
  destroys no file of its source: the guard at three doors, with own-key files (the first segment of the storage
  key is the sandbox's tenant id) shreddable there. B4, measured: the key was destroyed with the row unmarked
  and no audit event under an ordinary lock wait — decide durably, then destroy, with a completion event and
  three roads that finish it (FILE-SHRED-DURABLE-ORDER-1). A failed job leaves an INFO item, dismissible; after
  the last attempt of a computation job nothing is stored — the group stays dirty and holds the lock; a policy
  retries only what another attempt can cure, REPORT_RUN included. A disabled provider's sessions are ended by a
  command of its own, and a sign-in in flight reads the provider again under the identity's lock. The doctor
  gains a seventh baseline check (every entity's time zone resolves in the process that runs it). The readers of
  the owner URL are held by an architecture test. The zap script owns the down only when its own up was reached.
  (m) *The screens (lanes WEB-QA, F-CTR-WEB, F-CLO-WEB, F-ADM-WEB).* A navigation without a path from a route the
  router has left is dropped (SCR-URL-33), and one sent while a navigation to another pathname is on its way; a
  write after an awaited command is made for the page that sent it. A form that names no members is shown every
  sentence of a refusal, each once (two heads). The "Run journals" form offers the entities `journal.run` is held
  for. The screens of the tenant-wide acts ask `holdsForAll`. The content-withheld pane renders a comment
  whenever it is not null. The close screens' commands ask the entity of the path. The modification item, its
  three patch items, the screens of the two close reads, the source-lines drawer's order and RPS-19's two
  dashboards are ruled as the lanes' pre-build lines with the defaults named in the register. The demo world: the
  close is a stage the seed runs only when asked; the perf seed closes at test scale with fewer months; slice D's
  connections with their seven points; DEMO-FX-CONTRACT-1 waits for HOME-MULTICURRENCY-1.
  (n) *The accounting reviewer's list (lane ACCT; corrects R-121 (n)).* The control inventory counts BUILT 13,
  PARTLY 33, NOT BUILT 3 — not the 12 / 34 / 3 that R-121 (n) quotes. AD-64 to AD-72 stand as numbered and the
  waterfall's entity keeps AD-49. AD-71 stands: R-120 (e)'s "no question of accounting" was about the seed's
  defect; the candidate is the other question — 04 allows basis AMOUNT on a series product's entry and nothing
  says when that reading is right. AD-65, AD-66, AD-67, AD-68 and AD-70, as the lane's packets state them, are
  candidates for the accountant; AD-66's figures are in (i). New candidates of today: AD-73 an SSP study does not
  compare booked terms; AD-74 a change order's estimate changes post at their own approval; AD-75 an attestation
  of no change needs no file; AD-76 the last approved of two overriding members wins; AD-77 D-92 (1) at the
  inception date; AD-78 answer key GT07 keeps a nondistinct obligation apart; AD-79 a first policy version
  without a date answers for every earlier period; AD-80 over-billing is bounded by nothing outside one import;
  AD-81 the RPO's entry date; AD-82 the look-back inclusion; AD-83 what a regroup after posting moves; AD-84 a
  judgement record on a combination group holds the one contract it names. The lane's read of every ruled repair
  found three security orders of 09-30 named by no commit — R-22 (the regroup), R-23's two orders — all placed
  today; a ruled repair needs a register line that a commit closes.
  (o) *For Ray.* Whether a lane session may merge main into its own branch (five sessions are refused); R-11 and
  the capture witnesses' world edit; REQ-CON-012's second half; SETTINGS-UNREAD-1; release preparation and replay
  verification are functions without a caller; PERF-CLOSE-FANOUT-1; HOME-MULTICURRENCY-1; WS-OBJECT-AUTH-1; the
  two rulings on a preparer of one entity in (c) and (g); CTR-14 / RPT-31, IPL-11.
  (p) *Verification on main.* full12 on 2280e855: 31 nodes red twice — the standing set of 25 (three capture
  witnesses, the audit walk's two, three registers, the R-11 witness, the perf seed's eight, the volume generator,
  the POS-117 platform witness, six answer keys), revision 0111's two walks, and four repaired since; one passed
  alone (order). v32 117 passed; v33 144 passed, 1 skipped; v34 647 passed, 5 failed (0111's two, the capture
  three); v35 191 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed (the J-06 witness, repaired). Whole e2e: on 58ab6aa9 181 of 181; on
  69b21331 and on 90d03fd6 184 of 185, the one red the prefill defect of (h). The audit walk's SSP line is closed
  by 2a3072b8. full13 and a further e2e were running when this entry was written.

  263e1431. Refines R-10, R-23, R-28, R-41 (8), R-44 (b), R-63 (b), R-68 (a) and (b), R-69 (a), R-74, R-97, R-103 (b),
  R-105 (3), R-110, R-112, R-115 (c) and (e), R-116 (g), R-117 (b), R-118 (m), R-119 (b), R-120 (n), R-121 (i), (k) and
  (n), R-122 (b), (g), (j) and (l); AMENDS R-11; corrects four rulings of the supervisor's own, named in (b); records
  forty first-parent commits on main since R-122 — thirty-nine lane merges or integrations and one repair by the
  supervisor.)**
  (a) *On main since R-122 (first parent, in order).* 02c30901 SECFIX-ACT the classification's proposals; 43caada9
  WEB-QA a write after an awaited command; cd302602 QA-BE the runner's world at activation; 1bb089bb F-CLO-B
  CLO-GATE-RUN-2 (revision 0111); 254b139a API-GAPS EXC-IMPORT-SCOPE-1; ccd57182 F-SNP REG-VERSION-WHOLE-SET-1;
  ec55ad33 SECFIX-ACT the price test's fact; 2abaec38 SECFIX-APR the rejection's reason to its preparer; 98c88bba OPS
  JOB-FAILED-ITEM-1; 5310275d F-CLO-B the emptied group at a period-end step; eb90a0cf SECFIX-CLO four refusals of one
  class; 787fbe85 SECFIX-PLT the shred's scope and the evidence count; 3cecdb44 QA-BE CTL-032's first tagged test;
  61cc2ced, d10d1cbd, 81ce7857 WEB-QA the kit's unplaced refusals (head 1), KFL-2, W-12d; f13f89df F-CTR-WEB the
  price test on the wizard; 701bf0ab FIX-D2 the period pin (ERR-72); c8a3e812 F-CLO-A the closed-period guard and the
  claimed retry (revision 0104); 0226e28d ACCT the ruled repairs read against main; afb04675 the supervisor, a stale
  pin of the migration walks since 1bb089bb (found by lane SECFIX-APR's gate); 3d96086f F-CLO-WEB the close screens'
  two reads; 7bae9b43 API-GAPS the quarantine's whole objects; 55bef5e4 WEB-QA W-12c head c3; abdffed8 and 9b0abb10
  FIX-D2 the LEGACY close run refused, the explain reader's scope; 84bc44e3 SECFIX-ACT STEP1-HOLD-RELEASE-1; 5a7b0041
  F-RPS-REG SCOPE-WORKSPACE-LISTS-1 (c2) with (c3) and NOTIFY-ROLE-HOLDERS-SCOPE-1; 51f9bbbe SECFIX-APR the request's
  reason and the decision's code as content; f70d2c46 and 6a50a69b QA-BE the close-run step with the window read, the
  control markers; b167c0f5 F-SNP SBX-EMPTY-BOOTSTRAP-1; b1cb31a8 F-CLO-A the exits' settling; f9fded71 QA-BE the
  window-mark witness; ff3cf95b F-ADM-WEB `seed demo --with-close`; 3815c80f and 263e1431 F-CTR-WEB patch items (a),
  (b), (c); a858d027 SECFIX-CLO the quantity in force, usage as a manual event, the regroup's permission; ded25545
  WEB-QA the e2e sign-in steps; 12f3894b SECFIX-PLT the delegation list's scope and the migration job's lock.
  (b) *The supervisor's own rulings corrected.* (1) R-122 (j) "RPT-12 follows RPT-11" was ruled without the lane's
  measurement and is withdrawn: RPT-12 keeps the stored rule (the latest version effective on or before the date); the
  four numbers given for it are unused. (2) The period pin's condition is "the bundle HOLDS an event recorded in this
  transaction"; "the newest event" was wrong for a command that waited for its contract row. (3) R-68 (b)'s lock on
  the generation of a reconciliation is replaced by counts (e). (4) The ruled sentence of JRN-EMPTY-RUN-1 claimed
  coverage and was false in three of four cases; the lane's sentence stands. Also: answer Q4 to lane F-RPS-REG (the
  suggestions' route) withdrawn in favour of lane API-GAPS' rule; `entity=*` for "all entities" withdrawn in favour of
  a screen parameter, on the proposing lane's own correction; the conversion of the answer-key runner's plan is lane
  QA-BE's, the steps named by the lane whose head needs them.
  (c) *The integration branch.* Merges are concluded on `sup/next` in the main checkout; `main` moves by fast-forward
  only when the lanes' database witnesses have passed on the stacked tree (one module per process on an export, the
  supervisor's private database) and, for web heads, the whole vitest and the whole e2e. A lane is told "merged on the
  integration branch as <sha>" and merges main only on the main SHA. A red that an export fails for want of a venv or
  node_modules is the export's own and is shown green in a tree with its setup before it is set aside.
  (d) *Entity scope.* (1) SCOPE-WORKSPACE-LISTS-1 (c2), (c3): out of reach is 404 without an event; a list beyond the
  caller's scope is 422 with a DENIED event; a delegate of `access.approve` is no reviewer; an access review needs two
  approvers for all entities (stated, for Ray). (2) A notification addressed by role goes to the holders whose role
  covers the event's entity, the workspace's to holders for all entities; the delegates of `notify_permission_holders`
  follow (NOTIFY-DELEGATE-SCOPE-1). (3) The activation checklist and the 409 of submit-activation name the other
  contract of an open suggestion only to a session that reads it: IMP-103 with "a contract outside your entities" for
  a session and "a contract of another entity" for a stored line. (4) R-28's remainder, measured by lane F-RPS-REG:
  judgement records, combination groups and a posting by id leak across entities — built by that lane (R28-READS); the
  SSP calculator's runs, the SSP books of an entity and the SSP resolve read — lane SECFIX-APR (SSP-ENTITY-SCOPE-1: the
  provider within the requester's `contract.read`, the scope stored on the run, a book in reach by its entity, the
  resolve read under the tenant's scope). (5) WS-OBJECT-AUTH-1 (lane ACCT) is split: what PRD ACT-44 with BR-UX-06
  already decides is built — the tenant's settings by every road: POST /entities, PATCH /books and the enabling of the
  workspace's book through an entity's keep, the calendars with every kept book's period states written whoever asks,
  and PATCH /entities/{id} asked FOR that entity; the settings that travel as policy versions follow on a pre-build
  line — and the rest (who prepares and decides configuration and master data that no entity owns) is one question for
  Ray, not built. (6) An API client's rotate-secret and revoke take T-PLT-10's rule; a client out of the caller's reach
  answers 404 as the read does, WITH the DENIED event — the one 404 that records, because it is a credential; the list
  holds what its reader may act on. (7) The users list and the member page read memberships of other workspaces
  (USERS-MEMBER-TENANT-1). (8) The list under `blocking` is the reader's subset of the gate's items; the gate's count is
  the period's.
  (e) *Close and journals.* (1) REC-GEN-LOCK-1: a reviewed reconciliation is out of date by chain position and document
  counts; both kinds are counted; the billing kind's count is narrowed to the contracts with a billing event dated in
  the period; the ledger kind's count is the events dated through the period PLUS their inclusions in a version (V5).
  Limit said: not proved for a recomputation that writes a version without a new event dated through the period. (2)
  The role basis lands as ONE head with the demo stage's trial balance and index 211. R-110's witness pins account 2100
  at 0.00 / -540.00 with its arithmetic, and a world that also runs July's close ties at 0.00 as a test of its own. (3)
  CLO-RATE-AFTER-RUN-1 is a release blocker as measured: a closing rate corrected after the close run leaves every gate
  PASSED; the gates must say a run is owed when what the run read is no longer in force. (4) JRN-HELD-AFTER-EXPORT-1,
  release blocker: the next run takes over the lines an exported run left out under a hold; the close run's journal
  step gains that case; no API member. (5) CLO-REOPEN-APPROVAL-1: inside a period in soft close or reopened, a batch
  of a manual type or pre-standard revenue waits whoever sends it; a batch of billing, credit memo, receipt or memo
  waits when the engine's dry run posts into the restricted period (not a table of types: a billing under ERP posts
  for a foreign-currency contract); an adapter's document raises PERIOD_RESTRICTED (IMP-142) and a person's reprocess
  applies it; a recognition hold is refused there; flags PERIOD_IN_CLOSE and PERIOD_REOPENED. The Step 1 assessment
  stays appended by its sender — a stated residue of SoD-6. (6) F4/F11: the backstop answers PERIOD_LOCK_IN_FLIGHT
  (ERR-98); the tail check at five call sites and a door on every caller of `append_events`; an import's commit runs
  once more for PERIOD_STATE_MOVED alone. (7) PERF-CLOSE-FANOUT-1 (a) by the skip rule; (b) not in a form that makes
  (3) permanent.
  (f) *Contracts, estimates, approvals.* (1) The estimate head: the request pins the contract's head; one open version
  of an element; evidence at submission and approval; the CONSTRAINT record of a variable-consideration version.
  ERR-94 one row. (2) An SSP override reads the pin of the obligation's latest computation whatever group computed
  it; an emptied group is not left dirty. (3) STEP1-CITE-LATEST-1: an assessment cites the newest reviewed Step 1
  record of its subject and book across both topics, reviewed at or after the flag it answers. (4) ACT-FLAGS-1,
  release blocker: MATERIAL_RIGHT, VARIABLE_CONSIDERATION and NEW_SKU are derived; consideration payable, noncash
  consideration, a line out of scope, a line with its own SSP version or accounts and a significant financing
  component give NON_STANDARD_TERMS; the booking states `acceptance_clause` and `side_letter`, and an absent
  statement withholds auto-approval; the thresholds are amounts of one stated currency after conversion. (5)
  EVT-EVIDENCE-1: after the approval every appended event carries the request's evidence; the evidence of a pending or
  applied submission and of a direct append is held. (6) The preparer of a manual event covers the contract's own
  entity; the request names the group's. (7) A delivery is bound by the quantity in force; a REMOVE line ends the
  obligation. (8) API-S-Event is one shape on both routes and gains `void_refusal`. (9) APR-DENIED-AUDIT-1: the
  refusals of a decision record a DENIED event with no content of the request.
  (g) *Reports.* (1) RPT-PERIOD-KEY-CALENDARS-1: a run by period key over entities of more than one calendar, and the
  waterfall and the disaggregation with or without a key, are refused 422 CALENDARS_DIFFER. (2) REG-LATE-NOLINE-1,
  (A) on the accounting reviewer's reading: a late event that a computation included is listed with zero effects, the
  event's own amount as a column, the effects named as posted out of period; the LATE_EVENT item is the stored source.
  (3) Schedules and the report view take the pill's context when the address names no entity; "all entities" is a
  screen parameter. (4) The dashboard draws its panels for one entity, or for a scope of one calendar and one
  functional currency.
  (h) *Engine and registry.* (1) PINP-PERIOD-VALUE-1 (A): a version of an accounting category may be dated before now
  while the workspace has no legal entity; the order of effective instants (ERR-80) yields there against an UNDATED
  predecessor only. (2) R-11 AMENDED: `subledger_line.subject_key` is nullable at the table and an explicit argument
  of `subledger.post` that every product caller passes; the read-back answers the stored key; NOT NULL waits for the
  four shared test builders (Ray). ENG-COST-READBACK-1 is its finding: 73 of 309 bundles post again at a second
  computation, and a period behind a lock cannot be closed after a recompute without a new event. (3) Recorded for the
  engine's lane: a credit memo under ENGINE on a foreign-currency contract quarantines it; a second approved SSP
  override quarantines its group.
  (i) *Platform.* (1) `jit = off` for the application's sessions. (2) Row 4: a failed dry run ends the upload INVALID;
  a hook takes an upload still in the state its job found it in, under the row guard; the two kinds register no failed
  item; an import that failed before its commit holds no lock. (3) JOB-STALL-COMMIT-RACE-1 (measured by lane OPS): a
  commit can land under a job the sweeper settled FAILED. (4) The planner over 181 partitions, not JIT, is what costs
  on the shared server (lane OPS): the audit list is bounded from above; the partition window is an item of its own.
  (5) A schema build or a migration must not run under the request's statement timeout. (6) SBX-EMPTY-BOOTSTRAP-1: the
  bootstrap admin is known by the seed's own grant. SBX-FILE-READ-1: a sandbox opens its source's key through
  `source_tenant_id` alone. (7) The performance seed stores a STORED_BACKUP and the harness restores it load-only;
  SNP-2's check is not loosened. (8) The identity's row before the security chain's lock (AUTH-LOCK-ORDER-1).
  (j) *Tests and gates.* (1) DG-TST-06 is read of a control: its tagged tests exercise the failure path, at least one
  of them. (2) A row the product stamps with the application clock is placed in the answer-key ledger by its
  `created_txid` against the server's horizon. (3) A head that adds a downgrade walk adds its line to the pinned list.
  (4) The pool is four slots since 03:53 on 10-02; new gates are held below 12 GiB free (the swap file grew by 3 GiB
  in the night: it is memory). (5) A lane's refusal to stop its own gate is not overridden by anybody.
  (k) *For Ray.* Added to R-122 (o): WS-OBJECT-AUTH-1's one question; eleven settings read by no code
  (SETTINGS-UNREAD-1); whether a lane session may stop its own gate; R-11's NOT NULL; whether a tenant may publish
  `billing.posting` ENGINE in 1.0; an EMPTY sandbox staffed by one person; the Step 1 assessment's SoD-6 residue; what
  ACT-FLAGS-1 means for an integration that does not state its terms; free disk and memory on the host.
  (l) *Verification on main.* full14 on 3d96086f: 9,073 passed first pass, 24 did not pass twice — exactly the
  standing set (six answer keys, the POS-117 platform node, the second release of a loss unit, the perf seed's eight,
  the volume generator, the audit walk's AI category, balance aging CHK-010, K-07, the K-03 loss provision register,
  three capture witnesses). Whole e2e: afb04675 185 of 185; 325521d6 (integration branch) 187 of 187. Whole vitest:
  fc454121 (integration branch) 1,596 in 171 files; one case of exceptions.test.tsx failed twice under load and passes
  alone (with its lane). Candidate rounds on the integration branch: 51f9bbbe 15 modules 192 passed; b1cb31a8 21
  modules 259 passed and the standing POS-117 node; 12f3894b 11 modules, all passed but one test that my export failed
  for want of its setup and that passes in a tree with it.
  (m) *Numbers.* Register indexes 191 to 240 as the register states them. Unused and not to be reissued: SCREENS_B
  1.84, dev-guide 1.244, header 1.202, fragment 14 rev 1.60, 04 1.251, dev-guide 1.249.

  (b) *The supervisor's own rulings corrected.* (1) R-123 (i) (4), "the planner over 181 partitions, not JIT, is
  what costs on the shared server", attributed to the product what belongs to that server: lane QA-BE measured the
  same statements on a container at the same machine load — a first plan of a 181-partition table in 1.6 to 3.2 ms
  against 13.6 s, `alembic upgrade head` in 14 s against 4.6 min — and read the cause: every process of the shared
  PostgreSQL runs at scheduling priority 20, and lane OPS found an open-file limit of 256 beside it (not verified
  on the running postmaster). Both are Ray's decision on that server; nobody of this programme touches it. (2) The
  tail check in the adapter's document (ruled 02:47 on 2026-10-02 from a reading): built as ruled an invoice is
  lost; the job applies once more in a new transaction and a second refusal fails the attempt (lane FIX-D2's
  measurement). (3) The stored scope of an SSP calculator run in the grant's shape: a run's scope is a requirement,
  so one nullable array as T-IMP-02 has it (lane SECFIX-APR). (4) The judgement register listing a record "to a
  reader who reads every contract it names": a stored run is opened by every member whose scope covers the run's
  entities, so the run's entities stand for the reader's (lane F-RPS-REG). (5) "Run report" after a refusal by a
  count that `restart` advances: it sends the refused set again; the creation answers to the address write itself
  (lane F-CLO-WEB's measurement). (6) JOB-STALL-COMMIT-RACE-1 as "the handler takes its job's row in its final
  transaction": the kernel says otherwise in four places ((d) (1)). (7) R-120 (a)'s ground, "the re-lock is decided
  over its difference report", is FALSE as built: the report is computed in the decision's transaction, so the one
  Controller who re-locks cannot read it before deciding; it compares end states (a hold applied and released shows
  nothing), and "Open diff report" leads to a report that has no builder (lane SECFIX-CLO's reading on d6e7d0f8).
  CLO-REOPEN-APPROVAL-1 stands ruled for both restricted states (R-120 (a), R-123 (e) (5)) and is built on no
  branch ((m)). (8) R-122 (i)'s last sentence, "POLICIES rule 3's sentence is corrected to that (rev 1.121)",
  states a correction NO BRANCH HOLDS. POLICY-OVERRIDE-BUNDLE-1 (register index 112, a release blocker by R-121
  (e)) is built nowhere: an approved policy override reaches no computation (the bundle resolves the registry
  without a contract; read by the supervisor on the tip of 2026-10-02 after lane ACCT's correction of 16:47),
  POLICIES stood at rev 1.97 at the end of this entry's window (its rows 1.122 and 1.123, of PINP-PERIOD-VALUE-1,
  come with lane ENG-FX's head of index 248, joined after it), rule 3 still says "in the first open period", and
  the item's numbers are unused (04 1.203, 05 1.142, dev-guide 1.186, ENGINE_SPEC 1.144, POLICIES 1.120 and 1.121,
  PRD 1.132, header 1.134, fragment 13 rev 1.17). The supervisor had dropped the item from every list of
  2026-10-02, the one given to Ray at 09:37 included; what it told Ray that afternoon of an override's effect holds
  only for the day the item lands. The decision is his ((m)). (9) R-55 (d) left WAIVER-SPECIFICITY-1 to Ray; index
  283 answers it by the count rule ((f) (9)) on the supervisor's ruling — reversible, and his to confirm. (10)
  Ruled and withdrawn on a lane's measurement or reading: the bring-up's report as the carrier of index 190 (the
  keep-up line stands: an interrupted bring-up writes no report); "the code" for 281's `release_refusal` (it is the
  sentence); "covers the date" for 260's rate (the engine's spot lookup, the latest on or before the date); the
  creator alone for 247's "Discard" (a holder of `judgement.create` for the record's entity); lower case for
  JRN-JOB-MODE-1's modes (EXPORT, RETRY, CANCEL, HAND_OVER); clause (iii) for a combination in 279 (it is (i)); "on
  main before 6 October" for test_days_to_close_last_three (its world holds no contract, line or version); the loss
  release on the predicate in index 87 (it stays on the method, on lane ENG-FX's dissent). (11) The second ruling
  at 242's merge called what the hooks of index 141 leave "another actor's transaction on the record in the instant
  of the settlement", as if chance: for an import it is the ordinary moment after a failed last attempt whenever a
  cancellation or a submission waited for the row ((i) (2)). (12) "Index 301 touches CTL-013, CTL-033 and CTL-034":
  it touches no row of the control inventory ((k)).
  (c) *The wrap-up test.* Ray asked at 09:36 on 2026-10-02 how far the project is from finishing and said that he
  wants to wrap it up. Until he answers, by the supervisor's reversible default: NO NEW ITEM IS PLACED OR GATED
  unless it is (1) a wrong figure in the posted ledger or under a lock, (2) data or authority across an entity or
  workspace bound with default roles, or (3) a step of a PRD journey that the screens cannot complete; heads in
  flight finish; everything else is measured where that is cheap, given its shape, and HELD without numbers ((l)).
  He has not answered; no message of a lane and no notice of a task is his answer. Documents-only items that take
  no slot and make a governed sentence true are taken (the follow-up of 242, the guides of 304, 299's list).
  (d) *Kernel: jobs, sessions, migrations.* (1) JOB-STALL-COMMIT-RACE-1 (index 242, lane OPS), K1 + K2 + K3 as
  merged: a heartbeat or a progress write is its attempt's — it applies to this attempt's RUNNING job and raises
  `JobLost` otherwise; a unit of work of a job takes the job's advisory lock, shared, as its first statement, is
  refused at its beginning when the job is no longer the attempt's, and writes its heartbeat on the job's row as
  its last statement before the chain's head (a plain update of a non-key column: eleven tables refer to a job and
  take KEY SHARE on it); the sweeper's settlement asks for that lock exclusively without waiting, before the job's
  row, and logs `job.sweep_passed_over` at every pass where it is refused. JOB-06 states the bound honestly:
  TXN-03's timeouts end a transaction that hangs, nothing ends one that is busy, and a handler silent OUTSIDE a
  transaction for ten minutes is still stopped. Found by the build and kept: the sweeper had already passed over,
  in silence, a job whose open unit had inserted a row that refers to it; a commit that failed left its transaction
  open — the unit of work is now discarded before the error leaves `commit()`, the audit chain's failure included
  (no caller took the error); a job context with a job row is the registry's and is refused without an attempt.
  NTF-05's stalled body: "... no heartbeat for 10 minutes. The job was stopped; what it had not committed by then
  is not kept."; PRD ERR-49's message and 03 REQ-OPS-006's parenthesis follow it (03 rev 1.147). Not built: an
  alert on the pass (a hosted policy on the log line is the proposal); the beat inside an adapter's wait —
  RECONCILIATION_GENERATE and SYNC_RUN are stopped as before while an adapter retries past ten minutes, and the
  attempt then keeps nothing; the hooks of index 141 ((i) (2)). (2) MIGRATION-STATEMENT-TIMEOUT-1 (index 239, lane
  QA-BE): a migration's connection has a statement timeout of its own (a setting, 1,800 s by default) and keeps the
  10 s lock timeout. (3) INVITE-ACCEPT-SESSION-1 (index 243, lane OPS), measured: two sessions were open after a
  password change between the acceptance's two transactions. The acceptance looks at the password again under the

  (index 257, lane SECFIX-PLT): the route's session question before the body, the answer kept on the request for

  (e) *Entity and workspace scope.* (1) R28-READS (index 232, lane F-RPS-REG): judgement records, combination
  groups and postings carry no entity and are read through what binds them — a record with the contract it names, a

  COMBINATION-PROPOSAL-RECORD-1 (253): a group's proposal is the record its command wrote, never the latest record
  of the topic. SCOPE-WORKSPACE-LISTS-1 (d) (233): the workspace's reference settings ask the permission for every
  entity. Index 182 (lane F-CLO-A): a journal line's contract name is null for a reader whose entities do not hold

  (i) *Imports, sandboxes, seeds, platform.* (1) Row 4 (index 203, lane API-GAPS, revision 0122): a failed import
  job ends its upload — three failure hooks under one rule. Rows 2 and 3 (204): an import's scope is one set, the
  contracting entities. IMPORT-HEADER-CELL-CONFLICT-1 (295): an import no longer stores a value its file
  contradicts — a cell of one object, or of one line, repeated on several rows with another value is an ERROR
  finding (HEADER_VALUE_CONFLICT; measured: a mapping version stored effective 1 July from a file whose second row
  states 1 October). (2) WHAT STAYS BEHIND A FAILED JOB — stated in 05 §5.6, NOT CLOSED. The hooks of indexes 141
  and 203 pass over a record another transaction holds. Since 242 the holder is never the job's own work; it is a
  command that locks the record before it checks anything — a cancellation or a submission of an import, an
  exclusion or a draft version of a calculator run. One that waited for the job's transaction takes the row at its
  rollback, the moment before the hook looks; every such command but the uploader's cancellation of an upload not
  yet submitted is refused and rolls back, and the record then stays in the job's state under a job that has ended
  — an import left APPROVED or COMMITTING for good, its file refused as a duplicate, its source held, no command
  out (read by lanes API-GAPS and OPS). MEASURED by lane OPS on 2026-10-03: the hook met the row held in 0 of 240
  cancellations refused behind a commit that failed on its last attempt, and in 10 of 10 once the holder was made
  150 ms slow (one host, one process). A commit job cancelled while QUEUED leaves its import APPROVED likewise (API
  only; read). The remedies — the hook waits for the row inside its savepoint, bounded by the lock timeout; or the
  two commands check before they lock; a hook where a QUEUED job is cancelled — are not built: Ray's first line
  ((m)). (3) FILE-SHRED-DURABLE-ORDER-1 merged; SBX-FILE-READ-1 (231): a sandbox copy reads the files it was copied
  with; a copy still reads a file whose shred is decided and not completed — stated, not closed. (4)

  holds on any server: a window cannot be extended over a month that has a row in the DEFAULT partition, and a

  At the merge of a head that adds a refusal, a scan or a closed list from a base far behind the integration

- **R-125 (2026-10-03 13:15; rulings given by message between 04:00 on 2026-10-03 and that time or with this
  entry; main 4832c645. Refines R-64 (6), R-114 (a), R-116 (a) and (b), R-124 (c), (e) (6), (f) (6), (j) (1) and
  (l); CORRECTS R-124 (e) (6) and (f) (6) and further rulings and work of the supervisor's own, named in (b);
  records seventeen first-parent commits on the integration branch since R-124's base, among them the revert and
  the return of register index 87, and one move of main over forty-eight first-parent commits.)**
  (a) *On the integration branch and on main since R-124 (first parent, in order).* Behind b51034a7d: 88cc92cde the
  supervisor, test only — the walk of revision 0129's database test joins the pinned list of downgrade walks;
  eb30ddc2d the supervisor, a repair of the join of index 266 (`_discard_refusal(row, with_its_group=…)`);
  648262d88 lane ENG-FX's index 248 (ENG-COST-READBACK-1, revision 0124 re-pointed onto 0129) with
  PINP-PERIOD-VALUE-1 (143); 56528d27e lane SECFIX-CLO's index 87 (ENG-USAGE-FIXED-SCHEDULE-1); bedcacc7a lane
  WEB-QA's K-3 series; dc57d8fc9 R-124; 2e3c90aba index 87's version follow-up; b72b72957 lane F-CLO-B's patch for
  the join of 248 with 272; 68863f956 lane SECFIX-PLT, test only (the combination's case of the behind-group
  module); 0063afc0c THE REVERT of index 87 and its follow-up; d3a1aa5f6 THE MERGE OF INDEX 301 (parents 0063afc0c
  and 6a23ffbf7); c832c396b index 306; d156de06d the merge of index 307; 22c84e160 THE RETURN OF INDEX 87;
  c7ff504a4 the merge of index 277; 5c3b5347c the supervisor, documents only, one clause of DG-CMD-10 in lane
  SECFIX-PLT's words; 4832c645c the merge of lane WEB-QA's repair of check C-10 of the QA pass, one file of the
  pass ((h) (3)). The two repairs of the supervisor's own were built with a temporary index under the open merge of
  248's head and the branch moved by `git update-ref` with the old value checked, so that they stand below that
  merge. On the head the Alembic chain ends 0126, 0130, 0129, 0124, 0128; the volume seed's generator version is
  14, the demo seed's 9. MAIN MOVED ONCE, to 4832c645c at 13:09 on 2026-10-03, by fast-forward over forty-eight
  first-parent commits — the thirty-one beyond main that R-124 recorded and the seventeen since — after the whole
  suite and the web verification on its first parent 5c3b5347c, to which it adds one file of the QA pass, and after
  lane WEB-QA's QA pass of record on the commit itself ((n), (h) (3)). Behind it on the integration branch stand
  the record's commits and no other: the QA record 21772a2fe, the state record 4ae009f54 and this entry.
  (b) *The supervisor's own rulings and work corrected.* (1) R-124 (e) (6) ruled that the approvals kernel asks a
  preparer by permission. As built (04 rev 1.319) every principal but SYSTEM was asked so, a subject no read
  permission names being read by any permission of the preparer's own for every entity. The provider's operator at
  the command line holds none: `erev support-grant request` was answered 403 "This item belongs to legal entities
  outside your roles …" — five cases of tests/domain/platform/test_support_grants.py, found by the whole suite on
  d3a1aa5f6; no gate of index 301 and no candidate round had run the module. Corrected by index 307 ((e) (2)). (2)
  The join of index 266 handed the mark `proposal_of_group` on under its own name, and the census of the proposal's
  writers counted a third caller: red for six and a half hours (eb30ddc2d). (3) The merge of index 285 did not run
  the pin of the downgrade walks: red for three and a half hours (88cc92cde). (4) The merge checks' unit list was
  hand-picked, and both pins of (2) and (3) stood outside it. It now holds every unit module at the top of
  tests/unit that walks source files, a catalogue or a census, with the policies and parity directories. (5) R-124
  (e) (6) (v) said of the three inline commands "to be measured". Measured since by lane QA-BE: the void, red on
  the base (7 of 81 members; revenue before 0.00 in six periods) and green on the tree; the activation, 0 of 78 on
  a draft; the estimate version is not measurable in WLD-K-04 (ACCOUNT_MAPPING_MISSING for every preparer) — the
  architecture test is its only pin. (6) "I run `make parity` on the candidate merge before I commit it" (to lane
  ENG-FX on 2026-10-02): `make parity` clones HEAD into a gate context; what ran before the commit of 248 is the
  inner recipe `make parity-gate` on the joined tree, 122 of 122. (7) The supervisor's reading of a first-pass red
  of the behind-group module — a host clock behind the database's, a helper asking a cutoff the product does not —
  was wrong. Lane SECFIX-PLT measured it: the case's world held the application clock one second ahead of the
  server's while the membership's end is on the database's clock, so a quiet machine goes red and a loaded one
  passes; a stale test world of 285's rework, repaired test-only (68863f956). (8) "Do not merge my tip; I join" (to
  lane QA-BE, index 301) could not stand beside "291 at your merge": reversed, the lane made the join ((e) (1)).
  (9) R-124 (f) (6) shortened lane ACCT's reading of the acceptance of a rate corrected after a lock: it needs "no
  less authority than the reopen" — two approvers, at least one a Controller (PRD row PERIOD_REOPEN). (10) Index 87
  was committed and called verified on its lane's gate and on round 15; round 16, seventy-seven minutes later,
  found that with it the volume tenant's close run refused at its dataset freeze. Neither lane's base could show
  it: the perf seed closes its months only since index 198, which stood on the integration branch alone. The item
  was reverted and has returned ((g) (3) to (5)); the join's round on the branch's newer worlds is the supervisor's
  and runs BEFORE a commit is called verified. (11) "The base is named when you commit" (index 277): withdrawn —
  the lane was ready hours before the tip could stand still, and committed on 68863f956. (12) The supervisor
  attributed to lane WEB-QA a reading it had not made ("no screen sends a usage report") in a message to lane
  SECFIX-CLO; the lane said so; the reading was then ordered and bears the sentence out ((h) (2)). A finding is
  attributed to a lane only with its message at hand. (13) The supervisor's word that the e2e ports' lock is shared
  between worktrees was wrong: it is each worktree's own. (14) Lane ENG-FX's session failed at 04:13 on an API
  error that its own idle notification named; the supervisor overlooked it for two hours ((j) (5)).
  (c) *The wrap-up test, applied.* Ray's question of 2026-10-02 09:36 is unanswered and the reversible default of
  R-124 (c) stands. Under it since R-124 — PLACED: index 306 (class 1, a wrong figure under a lock on main) and
  index 307 (class 3, the provider's support access cannot be completed; a regression of the branch, never on
  main). RETURNED: index 87, a head that was in flight, behind the item that removed what broke it. NOT PLACED:
  index 294 (RATE-PIN-NET-1 — it needs 277's code; its measure is orders (b) and (c) of (f) (2)); the repair of the
  RPO roll-forward's cause map ((k) (2)); the siblings of the one-array bind ((l)); a thirteenth member of the QA
  pass ((h) (3)).
  (d) *Kernel: migrations.* (1) Revision 0124 (index 248, `subledger_line.subject_key`) stands on 0129, and
  EREV-MIG-0124 refuses a table that holds a line: a ledger written before the revision does not cross it — every
  development, e2e and lane database that holds ledger lines is reset and seeded anew (pre-release). (2) A database
  test does not pin the chain's head to its own revision: the tests of 0129 and of 0124 read the head from the
  script directory, and 0128's does the same. (3) Revision 0128 (index 277) stands on 0124; the PREVIOUS body of
  the transition function it re-renders is 0126's.
  (e) *Entity and workspace scope.* (1) Index 301 (READ-SCOPE-BY-PERMISSION-1 with PREVIEW-INLINE-SCOPE-1) is
  merged at d3a1aa5f6 as R-124 (e) (6) ruled it: `require(P)` hands a transaction the scope P is held for (404, 422
  on a create, no DENIED event outside it); the approvals kernel asks ONE read permission per subject type of a
  preparer, of a delegate and of the reader of a request's content (`approvals.readers`); once that question has
  answered the request's row is the kernel's — looked for, inserted, auto-approved and read back under the tenant's
  scope; three inline reads run under the tenant's scope. THE THIRD READ WAS KEPT (`adjustments._measure`): on main
  a release of 100.00 by a preparer of the contracting entity alone was measured, stored and routed as 4,890.61 — a
  second wrong figure of main, closed by the item. THE JOIN: lane QA-BE's session reached its budget with the merge
  open; resumed once, it committed the merge early as a take-over point (d8efa9c61), and lane SECFIX-APR finished
  it — a reading of the joined kernel against the mechanical merge, then witness 3 of index 291 turned and measured
  (422 on the create equal to a stranger's; 404 on the change, the test and the submission; no DENIED event), 05
  rev 1.213. With it lane SECFIX-APR's corrections of its own landed text of 291 and 220, seven replacements in
  four files: who reads is written from the row policy and the scope a permission is asked for, not from the
  route's guard. (2) Index 307 (SUPPORT-GRANT-OPERATOR-SCOPE-1, lane SECFIX-APR; 04 rev 1.321, dev-guide rev
  1.297), merged at d156de06d: the kernel's own-scope question is answered by the ENTITY SCOPE of a principal that
  holds no permission of its own and that no route admitted — kind SYSTEM, as before, and kind OPERATOR with
  `auth_method` "system" and no permission scopes, the provider's operator at the command line; one predicate,
  `engine.asked_by_entity_scope`, for the question and its SQL twin. Not by `auth_method` alone: a sandbox's period
  replay acts as its requester with her copied grants under the same method, and her roles' union would be a read
  again. The lane's census: no second principal is refused since rev 1.319; `support-grant request` is the one
  command that reaches the question with a preparer who holds nothing. (3) Carried from 301's report and held: rule
  1 leaves 72 of the 422 operations without a single declared permission on the union; an API token with a write
  and without its read submits nothing that names an entity; a tenant-level template job runs under the union;
  sixteen files of the web gate by a bare permission, and a command can be offered that answers 404.
  (f) *Close and FX.* (1) Lane F-CLO-B's patch for the join of 248 with 272 (b72b72957): a close run's registry
  read follows the run's period (`run_inputs.period_values`), with twelve pins; until it landed one unit pin was
  red and the run's digest failed closed. The failing branch of the registry term has no database witness any more
  — no world holds a run made before its own period — and stands on unit pins and three mutants. (2) Index 277
  (FX-REPUBLISH-DIRTY-1, revision 0128), taken from lane ENG-FX's uncommitted files by lane SECFIX-PLT and merged
  at c7ff504a4: the approval of an FX rate set version MARKS the groups its changed keys reach —
  `close.rate_reach.marked`, a hook in front of index 272's finding, in the approval's own transaction;
  `dirty_since` the later of the stamp that stands and the approval's instant, `dirty_trigger` FX_REPUBLISH; a
  bundle asked under COMMAND that first-includes no event is stored under the mark's trigger; the out-of-period
  register lists what a republication posts behind a lock as a TRIGGER row whose transaction amount is nil. RULED
  IN THE PORT: (i) THE LIMIT is stated in three orders as measured — (a) the approval commits between a bundle's
  read and its posting, with `published_at` not later than the cutoff: the computation is stored FAILED
  (REQ-FX-006), nothing is posted, the next computation posts; not made by the item (the same with the mark off);
  its blocking item clears by a reprocess; THE FORM of this failure, FAILED with a blocking item where a bundle
  behind its group is answered 409, is held; (b) the approval commits after the computation and (c) the version is

- **R-126 (2026-10-03 15:02; Ray's delegation of 2026-10-03 and the supervisor's thirteen decisions under it;
  main 4832c645. Decides what R-124 (m) and R-125 (m) left to Ray as far as his word covers it; refines R-124 (c);
  places register index 308.)**
  (a) *Ray's word and what it covers.* To the supervisor's status of 13:21 on 2026-10-03, which listed thirteen
  decisions as his, Ray answered: "make those decisions for me please". The supervisor decides the thirteen in (b);
  each is his to reverse. NOT covered by that word, and still his: the independent accountant (G12; AD-1 to AD-40;
  VC-CHK-113); the hosted inputs; the permissions only he can give — among them the steps a lane's session was
  refused (the record's item 6: the one-line world edit behind the three capture witnesses) and the RUNNING of the
  `/dev/shm` command, which this session's permission classifier refused: a general delegation is not that
  command's approval, and the supervisor does not run it; a push, a deployment, a publication.
  (b) *The thirteen decisions.* (1) THE CUT LINE AND THE FREEZE. Release 1.0 is the product as it stands on main
  4832c645c — the freeze of 2026-10-03 13:09 — with what this entry places ((c)) and with the repair of any finding
  of the three classes of R-124 (c): a wrong figure in the posted ledger or under a lock; data or authority across
  an entity or workspace bound with default roles; a step of a PRD journey that the screens cannot complete.
  Nothing else is placed. Out of 1.0 and into the next release, by the record's item 1: the legacy migration beyond
  what is built, forecast, AI assistance, the rest of the demo world and of the performance phase, the registers
  and report builders that are not built. Everything held under R-124 (l) and R-125 (l), and the open questions of
  the record that ask for a build — its items 11, 14, 18, 22, 24, 30 (a), 31 and, but for the accountant's points,
  33 — become STATED LIMITS of 1.0 as the product is built, written in one pass ((d)). (2) The `/dev/shm` command
  is not needed while no lane gates at width, and is not run by the supervisor ((a)); the line stays in the record
  for Ray. (3) INDEX 301 STAYS on main. (4) POLICY OVERRIDES ARE WITHDRAWN FOR 1.0 — index 112 in lane ENG-FX's
  size B, not its build: nothing can then be approved that the books ignore; REQ-FX-002's exception by a
  contract-level override (POL-163) is not delivered in 1.0; placed as index 308 ((c)). (5) THE LEGACY MIGRATION IS
  OUT of 1.0 beyond what is built, with three stated limits: a legacy Contract Modification on a combined group
  quarantines the group (no combination under the legacy preset); the tie-outs of onboarded history; the exact-text
  rule on the command side. (6) THE STUCK IMPORT is a stated limit: behind a commit job that fails while a command
  holds the import's row, the import can stay APPROVED or COMMITTING with no command out (measured in 0 of 240
  natural cases and in 10 of 10 with a holder made slow); its remedy is the next release's first kernel item. (7)
  THE FIVE SITES that state a locked month from the versions — the Home's contract liability panel, the cost
  roll-forward's closing tie, balance aging, the book bridge, the adoption bridge — are a stated limit: the lock's
  datasets are the record of a locked month. (8) PERFORMANCE AT VOLUME is not a gate of 1.0: it is stated as not
  measured; `make perf` at volume, with the harness's month 24, is the next release's. (9) INDEX 294 is not built:
  orders (b) and (c) of R-125 (f) (2) are a stated limit, with the practice that a corrected rate is approved while
  no import and no computation runs. (10) INDEX 306 STAYS: the refusal at the door. (11) THE RETURN OF INDEX 87
  STANDS. (12) THE WATERFALL'S ENTRY RULE is not built: a stated limit in lane ACCT's sentence, first among the
  next release's report items. THE CLASS, for the test of R-124 (c): a figure of a standard report that is right by
  the report's own stated rule is no wrong figure under a lock; class 1 is the posted ledger, a disclosure, and a
  report figure that contradicts its own rule. (13) THE RPO ROLL-FORWARD's unexplained usage fees are a stated
  limit in lane ACCT's sentence, the entry in its cause map the next release's; the date to give a usage report —
  the last day of its usage period — becomes one sentence of the user guide, in the limits pass.
  (c) *Index 308, POLICY-OVERRIDE-WITHDRAW-1 — the one item placed behind the freeze.* Why this one and no other:
  it is the one open matter in which the product says what it does not do — an override can be requested on the
  screens, approved by a second person and stored, and no computation reads it; every other held matter either
  refuses, or shows its own failure, or is right by a rule that can be stated. THE SCOPE is lane ENG-FX's size B as
  it wrote it on 2026-10-03, and nothing beyond: `create_override` refuses every creation by name, with the
  sentence of what decides instead (the registry's value at tenant or entity level; the template's policy values;
  the SSP override of a line; the modification's SSP basis; the estimate version's method; the reviewed judgement
  record); submit and approval keep their code and meet no row; no Alembic revision, no engine file; the "Request
  policy override" drawer is no longer offered on the screens; the feature's tests are restated as the refusal's
  witnesses, and the tests that used an override as a means (a group's mark, the audit walk, the contract trail,
  the route table) stand on another act or on a row; the answer keys' platform runner loses its override step, and
  the three keys whose plans used it (POS-CHK-012, DLT-CHK-020-CHK-022-GT07, POS-CHK-117 — standing reds today)
  stay red and are tied to the next release's road for POL-122; the documents mark the override's rows as not in
  this release. Slice 1 of the build, kept by lane ENG-FX in scratch, is the next release's first engine item. A
  LINE FIRST from each lane; numbers at the line.
  (d) *The limits pass.* Lane ACCT writes the stated limits of 1.0 FOR MERGE, in one document and one pass, from
  R-124 (l), R-125 (k) and (l), this entry's (b), the control inventory and the record's items — each limit one
  sentence in the product's words, with its consequence and, where there is one, the practice that avoids it; in
  three classes: what stands before production use (the accountant's review, the hosted inputs, performance at
  volume); the limits of 1.0; what moved to the next release. The fifteen standing names of the whole suite are
  tied there to their causes, by name. No test is skipped, loosened or marked for it.
  (e) *What "finished" is.* The RELEASE CANDIDATE of 1.0 is declared on the commit that holds index 308 and the
  limits document, after a whole backend suite with the fifteen standing names and nothing else, the web
  verification, and lane WEB-QA's QA pass of record on that commit. With it the programme that Ray resumed on
  2026-09-29 ends; what stands before production use ((d)) is not the programme's to close.
  (f) *Numbers.* Register index 308. Given to it, returned if unused: PRD rev 1.209 with ERR-102 for the refusal;
  03 rev 1.149; 04 rev 1.322; 05 rev 1.215; dev-guide rev 1.298; POLICIES rev 1.124; header rev 1.285; fragment 13
  rev 1.80; SCREENS rev 1.80. The numbers of index 112 stay unused and are not reissued (R-124 (b) (8)). Next free
  with this entry: index 309; Alembic 0131; 03 1.150; 04 1.323; 05 1.216; PRD 1.210; dev-guide 1.299; POLICIES
  1.125; header 1.286; fragment 11 rev 1.44; fragment 13 rev 1.81; fragment 14 rev 1.91; SCREENS 1.81; SCREENS_B
  1.107; ENGINE_SPEC_B 1.169; IMP-149; ERR-103.

- **R-127 (2026-10-03 21:47; the rulings from 2026-10-03 15:02 to the release candidate; main f8e46e54. Corrects
  R-126 (b) (1), (c) and (e); records index 308 as built and index 309, placed behind it; declares the release
  candidate of 1.0.)**
  (a) *What this entry is.* R-126 froze the programme at main 4832c645c and said what the release candidate is.
  This entry records what was ruled between that entry and the candidate — on the three parts of index 308, on a
  second item placed behind the freeze (index 309), on the limits document — corrects three sentences of R-126,
  records the candidate's verification and declares it. Every decision here is the supervisor's under Ray's
  delegation of 2026-10-03 (R-126 (a)) or under the delegated authority of `docs/01-DECISIONS.md`; none is an
  accounting conclusion; each is Ray's to reverse.
  (b) *Three corrections of R-126.* (1) R-126 (b) (1) names, among what leaves 1.0 by the record's item 1, "the
  rest of the demo world and of the performance phase". The record's item 26 names three phases: the rest of DMO;
  PRP — the platform path of the answer keys, the suite's run of the platform keys on the database platform, where

  (c) *Index 308, POLICY-OVERRIDE-WITHDRAW-1, as ruled on the lanes' lines and as built — three parts, cut by

  (d) *The financing limit, and the supervisor's reading corrected.* Found by lane SECFIX-PLT while it built 308
  (read, not measured): `sfc.discount_rate_basis` (POL-047, level Contract only) holds a basis and a compounding
  and no rate; the engine takes a financing rate from a contract-level override alone; without one a financing
  component that needs an adjustment raises `SFC_RATE_MISSING`, an ERROR. The supervisor first wrote that such a
  component is "reported and not adjusted". Lane ACCT read the tip and corrected it: an ERROR stops the computation
  of the whole contract group with no output (CV-15) — a draft is refused at activation's dry run and is never
  activated; an active contract's computation is stored quarantined with a blocking item and nothing more of the
  group reaches the ledger; the item's waiver, with the waiver of the gate that counts the groups still to compute,
  lets the period lock without the group. RELEASE 1.0 COMPUTES NO CONTRACT WHOSE FINANCING NEEDS AN ADJUSTMENT —
  before 308 already, since no computation read an override; with 308 by name. CLASS: a stated limit, not a wrong
  figure, since nothing unadjusted is posted (the limits document, rows B3-14 and A-11). Three roads keep a
  contract clear of the finding: the one-year expedient (POL-046 `APPLY`, the default); a reviewed financing
  assessment that concludes not significant or names an exception of 606-10-32-17; no payment schedule and no
  record that says significant. For the accountant: the pressure of the limit falls on the "not significant"
  judgement record. The road to a rate is index 112's first slice, the next release's first engine item. The
  refusal's sentence for the parameter: "The framework's default basis applies to every contract in this release,
  and no discount rate can be given: a contract whose financing needs an adjustment is not computed
  (SFC_RATE_MISSING)." — confirmed by lane SECFIX-PLT against CV-15.
